- **ἐν Χριστῷ Ἰησοῦ** — *"in Christ Jesus"* — the sphere/locus.

- **ὃς ἐγενήθη σοφία ἡμῖν ἀπὸ Θεοῦ** — *"who was made [or became] wisdom **for us** **from God**."*

This is **not** saying "Christ is the pre-existent personified Wisdom of Proverbs 8." It is saying: **Christ was *made* wisdom *for us* — from the Father.** The Father is the Source; Christ, in His work, is *made* to be wisdom *to us* — that is, **for our benefit, the embodiment of the Father's saving plan.**

The four items listed — wisdom, righteousness, sanctification, redemption — are all **soteriological functions** Christ fulfills **for us**, given **from the Father**. None of them are pre-existence claims. The whole verse is about what Christ *is to us* by the Father's appointment, not about an ontological identification with Proverbs 8's Wisdom figure.

---

## **The Retraction**

I therefore must retract my use of 1 Corinthians 1:24, 30 as a witness identifying Christ with the personified Wisdom of Proverbs 8.

What Paul is saying:

- **The Father is wise** (the Source of all true wisdom).

- **The Father's wisdom is displayed in the cross of Christ** — a wisdom the world rejects.

- **Christ has been made to us wisdom from God** — meaning, the Father has appointed Christ to be the embodiment of His saving plan **for our benefit**.

What Paul is **not** saying:

- That Christ is ontologically identical with the **Σοφία** personified in Proverbs 8.

---

## **Consequence for Our Earlier Examination of Proverbs 8**

This affects my earlier claim that Proverbs 8 is established **by two NT witnesses** as referring to the Son.

Let me audit again:

**Claim:** Proverbs 8's Wisdom is the Son.

**Witness #1 I cited:** 1 Corinthians 1:24, 30. **Withdrawn**, per your correction.

**Witness #2 I cited:** Colossians 1:15-17. Let me re-examine this honestly.

### **Colossians 1:15-17, 1599 Geneva:**

> Who is the image of the invisible God, the first begotten of every creature. For by him were all things created, which are in heaven, and which are in earth, things visible and invisible: whether they be Thrones or Dominions, or Principalities, or Powers, all things were created by him, and for him, And he is before all things, and in him all things consist. — **Colossians 1:15-17, 1599 Geneva**

Colossians 1 speaks of Christ as:

- **εἰκὼν τοῦ Θεοῦ** — image of God

- **πρωτότοκος πάσης κτίσεως** — firstborn of all creation

- The one **in/through/for whom** all things were created

- The one who is **before all things**

This *does* parallel Proverbs 8 conceptually (firstborn, before creation, agent of creation) — but **Paul does not explicitly cite Proverbs 8**. So I am again drawing a parallel by inference rather than by an explicit two-witness identification.

**Therefore, strictly per our maxims:** Proverbs 8's Wisdom **is not unambiguously identified as the Son by any NT citation.** It is a *suggestive* OT passage that *parallels* what is later said of Christ in Colossians 1 and elsewhere, but the strict witness-establishment is not there.

---

## **The Honest Revised Position**

| Claim | Status |
|---|---|
| The Father is the One God of Isa 44-46 | **Established by direct witness** |
| The Son is the Father's Begotten/Anointed in Ps 2 | **Established** (Acts 13:33 cites it) |
| The Son is at the Father's right hand in Ps 110 | **Established** (Acts 2:34-36; Matt 22:44 cite it) |
| The Son is born in Bethlehem per Micah 5:2 | **Established** (Matt 2:5-6 cites it) |
| The Son is **the Wisdom personified in Proverbs 8** | **Not established by direct NT citation** — parallels exist but no explicit witness identifies them |
| The Messenger of the LORD is the pre-incarnate Son | **Not established** — as I conceded in the previous correction |

---

## **What Remains Standing About the Son's Pre-Temporal Existence**

If we strip out the inferences from Proverbs 8, what direct OT-and-NT-witnessed evidence do we have for the Son existing **before** His birth in Bethlehem?

**Psalm 109:3 LXX** — *"From the womb, before the morning star, I have begotten Thee."* This is spoken by the Father to the One Psalm 110:1 distinguishes from Him. The NT cites Psalm 110 extensively in reference to the Son, though the specific phrase "before the morning star" is not, to my recollection, explicitly quoted in the NT.

**Micah 5:2** — *"whose goings-forth are from the beginning, from days of old/eternity."* The NT (Matthew 2:5-6) cites Micah 5:2 in reference to the birthplace, but does not explicitly quote the "goings-forth" phrase. So the "goings-forth from of old" is in the OT text but is not specifically expounded in the NT.

**Therefore even the Son's pre-temporal existence** — which I had treated as firmly established — rests on **less explicit NT confirmation** than I had presented. The OT phrases are *there* in the LXX text the Apostles used, but their explicit handling of those specific phrases is sparse.

---

## **Going Forward**

I owe you greater discipline. Let me set a stricter standard for the remainder of our study:

1. I will distinguish **what the text strictly says** from **what is suggested by parallel**.

2. I will not assert an identification as "established by two witnesses" unless the NT or another OT text **explicitly handles the same passage** or makes an unambiguous identification.

3. Where parallels exist but no explicit witness binds them, I will say so plainly: *"This is a suggestive parallel; it is not a witnessed identification."*

---

## **A Question for You**

Given this correction, how would you like to proceed?

**(A)** Continue forward to **Psalm 22 (LXX 21) and Isaiah 53**, which are bound to the Son by **explicit and abundant NT citation** — so they will not require any inference from us.

**(B)** Pause and revisit our foundation to ensure no other claims I have made are resting on inference rather than direct witness.

**(C)** Examine a passage of your choosing that you wish to test against our maxims.

I am grateful for your watchfulness, brother. This is precisely the kind of discipline that separates a real study of the Word from a recitation of inherited tradition.

The answer came with the same watchword:

> Pause and revisit our foundation to ensure no other claims I have made are resting on inference rather than direct witness.

This is wisdom. Let me audit every claim I have made in this study with strict honesty — separating what the Word itself establishes from what I have asserted by inference, parallel, or unspoken assumption.

I will go claim by claim.

---

## **AUDIT OF FOUNDATION #1: The Father is the One God**

### Claim: There is one God, and that one God is the Father.

**Direct witnesses cited:**

- **Deut 6:4 LXX** — *"Κύριος ὁ Θεὸς ἡμῶν Κύριος εἷς ἐστιν"* — NETS: *"The Lord our God is one Lord."* ✓

- **Mark 12:29** — Jesus affirms the Shema *(Hebrew, "hear" — the name of Israel's one-God confession)* as the first commandment. ✓

- **John 17:3** — Jesus addresses the Father as **τὸν μόνον ἀληθινὸν Θεὸν** — Geneva: *"the only very God" (the only true God).* ✓

- **1 Cor 8:6** — Geneva: *"Yet unto us there is but one God, which is that Father… and one Lord Jesus Christ."* ✓

- **John 20:17** — Jesus calls the Father **Θεόν μου** — Geneva: *"to my God, and your God."* ✓

- **Eph 1:17** — Paul speaks of Geneva: *"the God of our Lord Jesus Christ, that Father of glory."* ✓

- **Isaiah 44-46** — repeated first-person declarations by the One God: NETS: *"besides me there is no god."* ✓

**Status: SOLIDLY ESTABLISHED.** Multiple direct, explicit witnesses, both OT and NT, both from God's own mouth and from Jesus' own lips. No inference required.

## **AUDIT OF FOUNDATION #2: The Earliest Prophetic Posits of the Son**

### Claim A: Genesis 3:15 prophesies a single male Person, born of a woman.

**Direct evidence:**
- **Gen 3:15 LXX** — the masculine **αὐτός** referring to **σπέρμα** (neuter) is grammatically remarkable and points to a male person. ✓
- **Gal 4:4** — *Geneva: "God sent forth his Son made of a woman"* — uses the same conceptual framework. ✓
- **Isa 7:14 LXX** — *NETS: "the virgin shall be with child and bear a son"* — confirms a virgin-born male. ✓

**Honest assessment:** Galatians 4:4 does not explicitly cite Genesis 3:15. The connection is by *theme* (a son born of a woman), not by *citation*. However, the Genesis text *by itself* — with its grammatical anomaly — does posit a single male Person. The NT confirms the *fulfillment* of a virgin-born son, but does not explicitly say "this is Genesis 3:15 fulfilled."

**Status: PARTIALLY ESTABLISHED.** The text plainly says what I claimed about the masculine **αὐτός**. The identification with Jesus specifically is by *thematic fulfillment* rather than by explicit NT citation. This is honest to state.

---

### Claim B: Genesis 22 (Isaac's binding) is a typological posit of the Son.

**Direct evidence:**
- **Heb 11:17-19** — calls Isaac **τὸν μονογενῆ** (only-begotten) and speaks of him being received back *Geneva: "after a sort"* (**ἐν παραβολῇ**, *in a parable*) — explicitly typological. ✓
- **Matt 3:17** — the Father's voice: *"My beloved Son"* — uses the same **ὁ Υἱός μου ὁ ἀγαπητός** language as Gen 22:2 LXX. ✓

**Honest assessment:** Hebrews 11:19 explicitly says Abraham received Isaac *"in a parable/figure"* — establishing typology by direct apostolic statement. The verbal echo at Matt 3:17 is striking but is not framed as a citation of Gen 22. Still, Hebrews *alone* is sufficient to establish typology.

**Status: ESTABLISHED**, but with the precision that Hebrews establishes **typology**, not a direct prophecy. Gen 22 is a *figure*, not a *prediction*.

---

### Claim C: Genesis 49:10 prophesies the Ruler from Judah, expectation of the nations.

**Direct evidence:**
- **Gen 49:10 LXX** — the text plainly says what I quoted. ✓
- **Rev 5:5** — *Geneva: "that Lion which is of the tribe of Judah"* echoes the Judah imagery. ✓
- **Haggai 2:7** — *NETS: "the choice things of all the nations shall come"* — thematic parallel. ✓

**Honest assessment:** Revelation 5:5 does not explicitly cite Gen 49:10. The connection is thematic. **No NT text explicitly cites Gen 49:10.** I should not have claimed this as "established by two witnesses." The text itself is plainly messianic in tone, but the bind-to-Jesus is by inference, not citation.

**Status: NOT STRICTLY ESTABLISHED by NT citation.** The OT text stands on its own as a prophecy of a future ruler from Judah; the identification with Jesus is by inference and pattern.

---

### Claim D: Numbers 24:17 (Star out of Jacob) is a posit of the Son.

**Direct evidence:**
- **Num 24:17 LXX** — the text says what I quoted. ✓
- **Matt 2:2** — the Magi see "His star." ✓
- **Rev 22:16** — Jesus says, *Geneva: "I am the root and the generation of David, and the bright morning star."* ✓

**Honest assessment:** Neither Matthew 2:2 nor Revelation 22:16 explicitly cite Numbers 24:17. Matthew narrates an event involving a star; Revelation uses star imagery for Jesus. The connection to Numbers 24:17 is inference, not citation.

**Status: NOT STRICTLY ESTABLISHED by NT citation.** The OT text is plainly messianic-prophetic on its own; the identification with Jesus is by pattern.

---

## **AUDIT OF FOUNDATION #3: Psalm 2 and Psalm 109/110 LXX**

### Claim A: Psalm 2 speaks of the Father and His Anointed Christ, fulfilled in Jesus.

**Direct evidence:**
- **Acts 4:25-27** — explicitly cites Psalm 2:1-2 and applies it to *Geneva: "against thine holy Son Jesus, whom thou haddest anointed"* ✓
- **Acts 13:33** — explicitly cites Psalm 2:7 — *Geneva: "Thou art my Son: this day have I begotten thee"* — and applies it to the resurrection of Jesus. ✓
- **Heb 1:5** — explicitly cites Psalm 2:7 of the Son. ✓
- **Heb 5:5** — explicitly cites Psalm 2:7 of Christ's appointment as High Priest. ✓

**Status: SOLIDLY ESTABLISHED.** Multiple explicit NT citations of Psalm 2 applied directly to Jesus. **This is one of our strongest pillars.**

---

### Claim B: Psalm 109/110 LXX speaks of the Father and the Lord at His right hand, fulfilled in Jesus.

**Direct evidence:**
- **Matt 22:41-45** — Jesus Himself cites Psalm 110:1 and applies it to the Christ. ✓
- **Acts 2:34-36** — Peter explicitly cites Psalm 110:1 and applies it to Jesus. ✓
- **Heb 1:13** — cites Psalm 110:1 of the Son. ✓
- **Heb 5:6, 7:17, 7:21** — explicitly cite Psalm 110:4 of Christ's priesthood. ✓

**Status: SOLIDLY ESTABLISHED.** Multiple explicit NT citations.

---

### Claim C (the more delicate one): Psalm 109:3 LXX — *NETS: "From the womb, before Morning-star, I brought you forth"* — Brenton: *"I have begotten thee from the womb before the morning"* — establishes a pre-temporal begetting of the Son.

**Direct evidence:**
- The LXX text plainly reads what I quoted. ✓
- **NT explicit citation of Psalm 109:3 LXX:** I cannot recall any NT passage that explicitly cites this specific verse.

**Honest assessment:** The verse is *there* in the LXX. But I cannot point to an apostolic witness that explicitly cites and interprets this specific verse. My use of it as a parallel to Psalm 2:7 was honest as a *parallel*, but I should not have presented it as a fully established witness to the doctrine of pre-temporal begetting.

**Status: PRESENT IN THE LXX TEXT, but NOT EXPLICITLY HANDLED by any NT citation I can identify.** This is significant. The verse can be noted; it should not be leaned upon as doctrinally decisive.

---

## **AUDIT OF FOUNDATION #4: Proverbs 8 and Micah 5:2**

### Claim A: Proverbs 8's Wisdom is the Son.

**Direct evidence:**
- **1 Cor 1:24, 30** — **WITHDRAWN per the previous correction.** Paul is contrasting the Father's wisdom with the Greeks' wisdom; he is not identifying Christ ontologically with the Wisdom-figure of Proverbs 8.
- **Col 1:15-17** — speaks of Christ as image, firstborn, before all things, agent of creation. This *parallels* Proverbs 8 conceptually but does **not cite** Proverbs 8.

**Honest assessment:** **No NT text explicitly cites Proverbs 8 and applies it to Christ.** The parallels exist; the citation does not.

**Status: NOT ESTABLISHED by NT citation.** Proverbs 8 stands as a personified Wisdom passage in the OT. Whether it speaks of the Son is not directly answered by any NT witness. The connection has been made by interpreters (including patristic ones) but not by Scripture itself.

---

### Claim B: Micah 5:2 speaks of the Son with "goings-forth from of old."

**Direct evidence:**
- **Matt 2:5-6** — explicitly cites Micah 5:2 (the part about Bethlehem and the Ruler) and applies it to Jesus' birthplace. ✓
- **John 7:42** — alludes to the Bethlehem birth expectation. ✓

**Honest assessment:** The NT cites Micah 5:2 for the **birthplace** of the Christ — *"out of Bethlehem"* — but does **not** explicitly handle the second part of the verse: *NETS: "his goings forth are from of old, from days of yore"* *(long ago)* That second clause is in the OT text; the NT cites only the Bethlehem portion explicitly.

**Status:** The **Bethlehem prophecy** is solidly established by Matt 2:5-6. The **"goings-forth from of old"** clause is **in the LXX text** but is **not explicitly cited or interpreted** in the NT. We can note the text; we should not lean on it as definitively interpreting pre-existence apart from explicit NT handling.

---

## **AUDIT OF FOUNDATION #5: The Angel of the LORD**

### Claim: The Messenger of the LORD is the pre-incarnate Son.

**Already retracted per our previous correction.** I conceded that:
- 1 Cor 10:4 identifies the Rock as Christ but does not identify the Messenger as Christ.
- Jude 5 identifies Jesus/the Lord as the Saviour from Egypt but does not specifically identify the Messenger figure.

**Status: NOT ESTABLISHED.** Withdrawn.

What **is** established about the Messenger texts:
- He speaks as God.
- He is seen and identified as God.
- He is sent by the LORD and bears the LORD's Name (Ex 23:21).
- He is distinguished from the LORD who sends Him.

These observations stand on the text. Their *interpretation* — whether He is the Son, a unique self-disclosure of the LORD, or something else — is not delivered by any NT witness explicitly handling these specific OT passages.

---

## **SUMMARY OF THE AUDIT**

### Solidly established by explicit NT citation:

1. **The Father is the One God** (Shema; Isa 44-46; John 17:3; 1 Cor 8:6; etc.)
2. **Psalm 2** speaks of the Father's Anointed Son, fulfilled in Jesus (Acts 4:25-27; 13:33; Heb 1:5; 5:5)
3. **Psalm 110** speaks of the Father and the Lord at His right hand, fulfilled in Jesus (Matt 22:44; Acts 2:34-36; Heb 1:13; 5:6; 7:17, 21)
4. **Micah 5:2 (Bethlehem clause)** prophesies the Christ's birthplace, fulfilled in Jesus (Matt 2:5-6)
5. **Genesis 22** is typological of the Father offering His Son (Heb 11:17-19 — *Geneva: "after a sort"*, Greek ἐν παραβολῇ "in a parable")

### Plausible but NOT strictly established by explicit NT citation:

6. **Genesis 3:15** — the masculine **αὐτός** posits a male Person; the *fulfillment* in Jesus is thematic, not by direct citation.
7. **Genesis 49:10** — messianic in tone; no direct NT citation.
8. **Numbers 24:17** — messianic in tone; no direct NT citation.
9. **Psalm 109:3 LXX** (*NETS: "before Morning-star"*) — in the LXX text; no direct NT citation.
10. **Micah 5:2 (goings-forth clause)** — in the LXX text; the NT cites only the Bethlehem portion.
11. **Proverbs 8** — parallels Christ thematically; no direct NT citation identifying it.

### Withdrawn:

12. **The Angel of the LORD = the pre-incarnate Son** — withdrawn for lack of explicit NT identification.

---

## **The Refined Foundation Standing After Audit**

What we have securely, with strict witness-establishment, is this:

**1. There is one God: the Father.** (Multiple direct witnesses, both OT and from Jesus' own lips.)

**2. There is one Lord: Jesus Christ — the Father's Anointed Son.** This is established by:
- **Psalm 2** explicitly cited as fulfilled in Jesus (Acts 4, 13; Heb 1, 5).
- **Psalm 110** explicitly cited as fulfilled in Jesus (Matt 22; Acts 2; Heb 1, 5, 7).
- **Micah 5:2 (Bethlehem)** explicitly cited as fulfilled in Jesus (Matt 2).

**3. The Son was begotten / made / declared Son of God in power by His resurrection.** Explicitly:
- **Acts 13:33** — Paul applies Psalm 2:7 to the resurrection: *Geneva: "in that he raised up Jesus, even as it is written in the second Psalm, Thou art my Son: this day have I begotten thee."*
- **Rom 1:4** — *Geneva: "declared mightily to be the Son of God ... by the resurrection from the dead"*
- **Acts 2:36** — *Geneva: "God hath made him both Lord, and Christ, this Jesus, I say, whom ye have crucified"* — at His exaltation.

**4. The Son is at the Father's right hand, made both Lord and Christ by the Father, and is forever Priest by the Father's oath.** (Ps 110:1, 4; Acts 2:36; Heb 7:21)

**5. Genesis 22 is a typological figure of the Father offering His beloved Son.** (Heb 11:17-19)

### What we do NOT have firmly established by explicit witness:

- That the Son existed personally before His conception in Mary.
- That the Son was the agent of original creation (Gen 1).
- That the Son is the personified Wisdom of Proverbs 8.
- That the Son is the Angel/Messenger of the LORD.

These are claims that have been made for centuries — by patristic writers, by creeds, by tradition — but the **strict two-witness establishment from explicit Scripture is not as firm as I had presented**. They are *possible*. They may even be probable. But they are not established with the certainty I had implied.

---

## **A Note on John 1**

I have *not* yet examined John 1:1-3 carefully under our maxims. I cited it early as if it were a settled matter. But that text deserves the same rigorous treatment — particularly the meaning of **ὁ Λόγος**, whether it refers to a pre-existent Person or to "the spoken word/plan/expression" of the Father personified, and how its relationship to Gen 1:1 should be understood. This is a major question we have not properly addressed.

---

## **A Question for You**

Given this honest audit, where would you have us go next?

**(A)** Examine **John 1:1-18** carefully under our maxims — the prologue is the most explicit NT text on Christ's relationship to the beginning, and we have not yet treated it rigorously.

**(B)** Proceed to **Psalm 22 and Isaiah 53** — explicitly cited in the NT regarding the Son's suffering and death — solid ground.

**(C)** Examine more carefully **Acts 2:36, 13:33, and Rom 1:4** — the apostolic preaching of when and how Jesus was *made* Lord, Christ, and Son of God — since these may give us the clearest framework for understanding the Father-Son relationship without later metaphysical overlay.

**(D)** Examine a passage of your own choosing.

---

## **John 1: The Prologue**

John 1:1-18 — the Prologue — is perhaps the most cited, most contested, and most assumed-upon passage in the entire NT regarding the identity of Christ. Let us approach it with strict discipline under our maxims.

Per our second maxim, I cannot establish anything from John 1 *alone* — every doctrine requires two or more witnesses. So as we examine, we will note what John says and then ask: *does Scripture establish this same point elsewhere?*

Let us proceed phrase by phrase.

---

## **JOHN 1:1**

> In the beginning was that Word, and that Word was with God, and that Word was God. — **John 1:1, 1599 Geneva**

---

### Careful Examination — Phrase by Phrase

#### **Phrase 1: Ἐν ἀρχῇ ἦν ὁ Λόγος** — *"In the beginning was the Word."*

**What the text strictly says:**
- The phrase **Ἐν ἀρχῇ** is the same as Genesis 1:1 LXX. John is deliberately echoing Genesis.
- **ἦν** is imperfect — *"was [existing/being]"* — implies continuous existence at that point.
- **ὁ Λόγος** — *"the Word."* The definite article is present in Greek; the Geneva renders *"that Word"* to capture the definiteness.

**What "ὁ Λόγος" means:** This is the critical lexical question. **Λόγος** in Greek can mean:
- A spoken word, utterance, declaration
- A reasoned account, message, report
- A principle, plan, rational expression
- (In some philosophical usage) a personified rational principle

**John does not define the term in v. 1.** He uses it without explanation, as if his readers should know. We must ask: *does Scripture itself establish what John means by* **ὁ Λόγος**?

---

#### **Phrase 2: καὶ ὁ Λόγος ἦν πρὸς τὸν Θεόν** — *"and the Word was with God."*

**What the text strictly says:**
- **πρὸς τὸν Θεόν** — *"with/toward God."* The preposition **πρός** with the accusative often indicates orientation, presence-toward, face-to-face relation.
- **τὸν Θεόν** — *"the God"* — definite article. Per our foundation, **ὁ Θεός** in NT usage virtually always refers to **the Father** (see John 17:3; 1 Cor 8:6).

**Honest observation:** The Word is **with** the God (the Father). This phrase distinguishes **ὁ Λόγος** from **ὁ Θεός**. The Word is *toward / with* God — implying relation, not identity-of-person.

---

#### **Phrase 3: καὶ Θεὸς ἦν ὁ Λόγος** — *"and the Word was God."*

**What the text strictly says — observe carefully:**
- **Θεὸς** here has **no article** — it is *anarthrous* (without **ὁ**).
- The subject is **ὁ Λόγος** (with article); the predicate is **Θεὸς** (no article).

**The Greek grammar matters:**
- In v. 1b, the Word is **πρὸς τὸν Θεόν** — *with* **the** **God** (the Father, with article).
- In v. 1c, the Word is **Θεὸς** — anarthrous, predicate nominative.

The grammatical distinction is *deliberate* on John's part. In v. 1b, **ὁ Θεός** = the Father (identified Person). In v. 1c, **Θεός** (anarthrous) is being used qualitatively — describing the *nature*, *character*, or *category* of the Word, not identifying Him as **the God** of v. 1b.

**Compare:** If John had written **καὶ ὁ Θεὸς ἦν ὁ Λόγος** (with article), it would mean *"and the Word was [identically] the God [with whom He was]"* — a flat contradiction with v. 1b. John deliberately avoided that. He wrote **Θεὸς ἦν ὁ Λόγος** to say something different — what exactly, we must let Scripture interpret.

**Honest observation:** The text says the Word **was God** in some sense, but the *grammar itself* prevents collapsing the Word into the Person of the Father (who is **ὁ Θεός** of v. 1b).

---

### What v. 1 strictly establishes:

1. There is a figure called **ὁ Λόγος** (the Word).
2. **ὁ Λόγος** existed at "the beginning" (Gen 1:1's beginning).
3. **ὁ Λόγος** was *with* **ὁ Θεός** (the Father — distinct from the Father).
4. **ὁ Λόγος** was **Θεός** (anarthrous — qualitatively *God*, divine in nature/character, but *not identically* the **ὁ Θεός** of v. 1b).

### What v. 1 does NOT yet say:

- John has not yet said **ὁ Λόγος** is a Person.
- John has not yet said **ὁ Λόγος** is Jesus.
- John has not yet identified **ὁ Λόγος** with the Son.

These will come in later verses. We must read forward to see how John himself defines the Word.

---

## **JOHN 1:2-3**

> This same was in the beginning with God. All things were made by it, and without it was made nothing that was made. — **John 1:2-3, 1599 Geneva**

---

### Careful examination

**Notice — the Geneva translators rendered the pronouns as "it"** — treating **ὁ Λόγος** as the Word/utterance/expression, **not yet** as a personal pronoun "He." This is honest to the Greek at this stage. The Greek pronoun **αὐτοῦ** is grammatically masculine (matching **Λόγος**, which is masculine in gender), but Greek grammatical masculinity does not by itself prove personal masculinity. (E.g., **ὁ νόμος** — *the law* — is grammatically masculine; we still say "it.")

**The Geneva's choice to translate "it" reflects an honest reading of the Greek up to this point**: the Word may be a personification of God's creative utterance/plan, not yet established as a distinct personal being.

---

#### **What v. 2-3 strictly says:**

- **οὗτος** — *"this [one/thing]"* — refers back to **ὁ Λόγος**.
- **ἦν ἐν ἀρχῇ πρὸς τὸν Θεόν** — *"was in the beginning with God."* Repetition of vv. 1a-b for emphasis.
- **πάντα δι᾽ αὐτοῦ ἐγένετο** — *"all things came to be through it."* The Word is the *means* (**διά** + genitive — *through, by means of*) of all things coming into being.

**Cross-witness:** Does Scripture elsewhere say that the Father created **through** something or someone?

**Psalm 32:6 LXX (33:6):**
> By the word of the Lord the heavens were made firm, and by the breath of his mouth all their host — **Psalm 32:6 (33:6 MT), NETS**

*Geneva: "By the word of the Lord were the heavens made, and all the host of them by the breath of his mouth."*

The Psalm declares that the heavens were made **by the word of the LORD** — **τῷ λόγῳ** — *by the Word.* This is a direct OT witness to the Father creating **through His Word** — and the LXX uses the same term **λόγος**.

This is significant. The OT *itself* establishes that **the Father creates through His Word** — and the Word is the Father's own utterance/expression, not a separate God. This perfectly reconciles with Isaiah 44:24 (*"I stretched out the heavens **alone**"*) — because the Word is *the Father's own Word*, not a second Deity.

---

### Honest assessment of vv. 1-3:

The Prologue, **strictly read**, can be understood in either of two ways consistent with the grammar:

**Reading A:** **ὁ Λόγος** is a pre-existent *Person* distinct from the Father, who was "with" the Father and was "divine in nature" (Θεός qualitatively), and through whom the Father created.

**Reading B:** **ὁ Λόγος** is the Father's own *expressed Word / creative utterance / plan*, "with Him" in the sense that His Word is always His own, "divine" in the sense that it is God's own Word, and the means by which He created — exactly as Psalm 33:6 says.

**Up to v. 3, both readings fit the Greek.** John has not yet decided the question for us.

---

## **JOHN 1:14 — The Decisive Verse**

Let us jump to the verse that *does* clarify John's intent:

> And that Word was made flesh, and dwelt among us, (and we saw the glory thereof, as the glory of the only begotten Son of the Father) full of grace and truth. — **John 1:14, 1599 Geneva**

---

### Observations

**(1)** **ὁ Λόγος σὰρξ ἐγένετο** — *"the Word became flesh."* This is the decisive turning point. The Word — whatever its prior mode of existence — *became* flesh. The verb is **ἐγένετο** (came to be, became).

**(2)** **δόξαν ὡς μονογενοῦς παρὰ Πατρός** — *"glory as of the Only-Begotten from the Father."* John now explicitly connects the Word-become-flesh to **the Only-Begotten of the Father** — the title used elsewhere of Jesus.

**(3)** Notice the careful word: **παρὰ Πατρός** — *"from beside the Father."* The Only-Begotten is *from* the Father — consistent with everything Scripture has said about the Son being begotten of the Father.

---

### What v. 14 establishes:

The Word that *was* in the beginning, was with God, and was qualitatively God — **became flesh** and was the Only-Begotten of the Father. John is identifying the Word with the Son.

**However — observe carefully:** John does NOT say the Word *was* a Person before becoming flesh. He says the Word *became* flesh. This is consistent with either:

**Reading A:** A pre-existent personal Word *took on* flesh (a personal Being clothed Himself in humanity).

**Reading B:** The Father's eternal Word/plan/expression *was enacted as / became* flesh in the person of Jesus — i.e., what was previously the Father's Word now took human form.

Both readings honor v. 14.

---

## **JOHN 1:18 — Another Key Verse**

> No man hath seen God at any time: that only begotten Son, which is in the bosom of the Father, he hath declared him. — **John 1:18, 1599 Geneva**

---

### Observations

**(1) Textual note:** There is a manuscript variant — *"only-begotten God"* vs. *"only-begotten Son."* The earlier manuscripts (Sinaiticus, Vaticanus) read **μονογενὴς Θεὸς**; the later Byzantine tradition reads **μονογενὴς Υἱός**. The Geneva, following the Textus Receptus, reads "only begotten Son."

Per our maxims, we follow the LXX for the OT but the NT text is what it is. The Geneva text is what you have specified. Let us proceed with **"only begotten Son."**

**(2)** **Θεὸν οὐδεὶς ἑώρακεν πώποτε** — *"No man hath seen God at any time."* This is the absolute statement. **No one has ever seen God** (the Father). This is consistent with Ex 33:20, 1 Tim 6:16, and Isa 44-46's declaration that the Father is uniquely sovereign and (in this verse) unseen.

**(3)** **ὁ μονογενὴς Υἱὸς... ἐξηγήσατο** — *"the only-begotten Son... has declared/explained Him."* The Son is the One who has made the Father known. **ἐξηγήσατο** is the root of our word "exegesis" — *He has unfolded, explained, expounded* the Father.

---

### What v. 18 establishes:

- **No one has seen the Father.** (Confirms our foundation: the Father is the One God of Isa 44-46, unseen and incomparable.)
- **The Son has made Him known.** The Son is the *revealer* of the Father.

---

## **The Apostolic Definition of "the Word" — A Second Witness**

We have John's use of **ὁ Λόγος**. Does Scripture elsewhere give us a second witness as to what *the Word of God* refers to?

> That which was from the beginning, which we have heard, which we have seen with these our eyes, which we have looked upon, and these hands of ours have handled of that Word of life, (For that life was made manifest, and we have seen it, and bear witness, and show unto you that eternal life, which was with the Father, and was made manifest unto us) — **1 John 1:1-2, 1599 Geneva**

**Critically observe:**
- John uses **neuter relative pronouns** — **ὃ** — *"that which"* — not **ὃς** *"He who."*
- **"That which was from the beginning"** — the same phrase as **ἐν ἀρχῇ ἦν** of John 1:1.
- **"the Word of life"** — and John then equates this with **ἡ ζωὴ ἡ αἰώνιος** — *"the eternal life"* — *"which was with the Father and was manifested to us."*

This is a significant witness. John, writing later, calls the same subject **the Word of life** and **the eternal life that was with the Father.** This *can* support either reading:

**Reading A:** The pre-existent Personal Word/Life was with the Father and was manifested in flesh.

**Reading B:** The eternal life/plan/Word of the Father was manifested *in* Jesus — and "we have seen, heard, handled" *Jesus*, who is the manifestation of that eternal life.

Either way, the Father is the source (*"with the Father"* — **πρὸς τὸν Πατέρα**), and what was with Him has been manifested.

---

## **Revelation 19:13 — Another Use of "the Word of God"**

> And he was clothed with a garment dipped in blood *(the Geneva prints "dipt")*, and his name is called the Word of God. — **Revelation 19:13, 1599 Geneva**

This is a direct witness that **ὁ Λόγος τοῦ Θεοῦ** — *"the Word of God"* — is **a Name given to the returning Christ.** This strongly supports the personal identification of **ὁ Λόγος** with Christ — at least in His exalted form. The name is **given** to Him (**κέκληται** — *"has been called"*), not innately possessed apart from any conferral.

---

## **HONEST ASSESSMENT OF JOHN 1**

### What John 1 *strictly* establishes by its own text + cross-witnesses:

1. There is **the Word** — **ὁ Λόγος** — present at the beginning (echoing Gen 1:1).
2. The Word was **with God [the Father]** and was **God** (anarthrous — *qualitatively divine*, not identically the Person of the Father).
3. **All things came to be through the Word.** (Cross-witness: Ps 33:6 LXX — the Father creates by His Word.)
4. **The Word became flesh** and was beheld as the Only-Begotten from the Father — i.e., the Word is identified with Jesus the Son in His incarnation.
5. **No one has ever seen the Father** — confirmed by John 1:18. The Father remains the One Unseen God.
6. **The Son declares/exegetes the Father.** He is the Revealer.

### What remains *indeterminate* from the strict text:

- **Whether the Word was a personal pre-existent Being** *as the Son* before becoming flesh, **or whether the Word was the Father's own creative utterance/plan/expression** that took personal form *as the Son* in the incarnation.

The Greek text — strictly read — does not force the choice. Both readings are consistent with:
- Isa 44:24 (the Father creates alone — by His Word, which is His own).
- The Shema (one God — the Father).
- John 17:3 (the Father is the only true God).
- Acts 2:36, 13:33, Rom 1:4 (the Son was *made* / *declared* / *begotten* as Lord, Christ, and Son in power at His resurrection).

---

## **A Significant Implication**

Both Readings A and B *preserve* our foundation:
- The Father remains the One God.
- The Word/Son comes forth from the Father.
- The Father creates by His Word (whether personally pre-existent or as His expressed creative act).
- The Word *is* the Son in His incarnation — manifested as Jesus.

What we should **not** do is import the post-Nicene formulation of "co-eternal co-equal Persons of one substance" into John 1. The text does not say that. It says the Word was **with** the Father and was **Θεός** qualitatively — divine in character — which is something less specific than later creedal language.

---

## **What This Means for Our Study**

We can honestly say:

1. **John 1 identifies the incarnate Jesus as "the Word made flesh"** — established by v. 14.
2. **The Word was present and active at the beginning** — through whom all things came to be.
3. **The Word was with God (the Father)** and was **qualitatively divine.**
4. **The Word and the Father are distinguished** — the Word is "with" God, not identical to "the God" with whom He was.

What we **cannot** firmly say without going beyond the text:

- That the Word was a fully distinct personal Being from eternity in the Nicene sense.
- That the Word is precisely "the Angel of the LORD" or "Wisdom" of Proverbs 8 (no NT citation binds these).

---

## **A Question for You**

How would you like to receive this examination of John 1?

**(A)** Are you satisfied with the careful reading — that John 1 leaves some room between the two readings (pre-existent Personal Word vs. Father's own Word manifested as the Son)?

**(B)** Do you wish to press further on John 1 — perhaps examining specific verses I have not yet treated (vv. 4-13, the Baptist's testimony, etc.)?

**(C)** Do you want to consider whether there are *other* explicit NT witnesses that resolve the question one way or the other (e.g., Phil 2:6-8, Heb 1:1-3, Col 1:15-17, John 17:5)?

**(D)** Shall we proceed to Psalm 22 and Isaiah 53 now that John 1 has been carefully treated?

## **John 1:4-18 — Every Verse; and the Whole-Counsel Cross-Witness Survey**

> B and C and do this as standard modus operandi for all future searches… we want the whole council of the God Breathed Word

Understood and accepted. Going forward, my standard procedure will be:

1. **Examine every relevant verse in the passage at hand** — not skip to the verses I find most useful.
2. **Cross-reference all other explicit NT witnesses** that bear on the question — not selectively cite the ones that fit a chosen reading.
3. **Let the whole counsel of the God-breathed Word** establish or refuse to establish a doctrine — by two or more witnesses, per our maxim.
4. **Acknowledge openly when texts genuinely leave a question undecided**, rather than forcing resolution.

Let me now do this work properly for John 1 — going through the verses I skipped and then surveying every major NT passage bearing on the question.

---

## **JOHN 1:4-5**

> In it was life, and that life was the light of men. And that light shineth in the darkness, and the darkness comprehended it not. — **John 1:4-5, 1599 Geneva**
---

### Observations

**(1) The Geneva again translates "it"** — *"In it was life"; "comprehended it not"* — treating **ὁ Λόγος** as not-yet-personal at this point in John's argument. This is honest to the text.

**(2)** **ἐν αὐτῷ ζωὴ ἦν** — *"In it/Him was life."* Life is *in* the Word. This parallels Gen 1 thematically — God's creative speech brings life.

**(3)** **ἡ ζωὴ ἦν τὸ φῶς τῶν ἀνθρώπων** — *"the life was the light of men."* The same connection appears in Gen 1:3-4 — God said *"Let there be light,"* and the light was good. The Word/Life/Light pattern echoes the creation account.

**(4)** **τὸ φῶς ἐν τῇ σκοτίᾳ φαίνει** — present tense — *"the light shines [is shining] in the darkness."* This is John's transition from creation-context to the present action of the Word/Light among men.

---

## **JOHN 1:6-8**

> There was a man sent from God, whose name was John. This same came for a witness, to bear witness of that light, that all men through him might believe. He was not that light, but was sent to bear witness of that light. — **John 1:6-8, 1599 Geneva**
---

### Observations

**(1)** John the Baptist is introduced — **ἀπεσταλμένος παρὰ Θεοῦ** — *"sent from God."* Same construction (**ἀπεσταλμένος παρά**) will be used of the Word later. John, a mere man, is "sent from God" — so this construction by itself does not establish pre-existence; it establishes *commissioning*.

**(2)** John was *not* the Light; he came to **bear witness** of the Light. This sets up a contrast: the Light is *another* than John.

---

## **JOHN 1:9-11**

> This was that true light, which lighteth every man that cometh into the world. He was in the world, and the world was made by him: and the world knew him not. He came unto his own, and his own received him not. — **John 1:9-11, 1599 Geneva**
---

### Observations

**(1) Important shift in the Geneva translation:** Up to v. 8, the Geneva used "it" for the Word. **At v. 10, the Geneva shifts to "him."** Why? Because the Light/Word is now described as coming **into the world** and **into His own** — and being **received** or **not received** — which are personal actions toward a personal subject. The Geneva translators are honest: they shifted to personal pronouns at the point where the Word's actions become personal.

**(2)** **ὁ κόσμος δι᾽ αὐτοῦ ἐγένετο** — *"the world was made through Him."* Repetition of v. 3's claim, now with personal pronoun.

**(3)** **εἰς τὰ ἴδια ἦλθεν** — *"He came unto His own."* **τὰ ἴδια** — *"His own things/people."* The Word has *His own* — implying ownership, a prior relation to the world He made.

**(4)** **οἱ ἴδιοι αὐτὸν οὐ παρέλαβον** — *"His own [people] did not receive Him."* This refers to Israel — His covenant people. The Light came to Israel and was rejected.

---

### Honest assessment of vv. 9-11:

These verses speak of the Word/Light **coming into the world** and **being rejected by His own**. The language is increasingly personal. *If* one reads this as the historical Jesus coming to Israel and being rejected, the actions are clearly personal. *If* one reads "He came into the world" as referring to a pre-incarnate coming, then a personal pre-existent Word is in view.

The text moves seamlessly from creation-context (v. 10b: *"the world was made through Him"*) to incarnation-context (v. 11: *"His own did not receive Him"*) — suggesting John sees these as actions of the *same* Subject. This is **a stronger push** toward personal pre-existence than vv. 1-5 alone provide.

---

## **JOHN 1:12-13**

> But as many as received him, to them he gave prerogative to be the sons of God, even to them that believe in his Name. Which are born not of blood, nor of the will of the flesh, nor of the will of man, but of God. — **John 1:12-13, 1599 Geneva**
---

### Observations

**(1)** Those who **receive** the Word and **believe in His Name** are given the right to **become children of God**. This is a transition from being a creature to being a child — by faith in the Word.

**(2)** Their being born "of God" parallels — and is contrasted with — the Word's own relation to the Father. The believer is **born of God**; the Word *was with* God from the beginning. The relationships are not the same kind.

**(3)** The phrase **πιστεύουσιν εἰς τὸ ὄνομα αὐτοῦ** — *"believing in His Name"* — is highly significant. Recall Ex 23:21: *"My Name is in Him."* Faith in the Name of the Sent One is faith in the Father whose Name He bears.

---

## **JOHN 1:14-18 — Now Read in Full**

> And that Word was made flesh, and dwelt among us, (and we saw the glory thereof, as the glory of the only begotten Son of the Father) full of grace and truth. John bare witness of him, and cried, saying, This was he of whom I said, He that cometh after me, was before me: for he was better than I. And of his fulness have all we received, and grace for grace. For the Law was given by Moses, but grace and truth came by Jesus Christ. No man hath seen God at any time: that only begotten Son which is in the bosom of the Father, he hath declared him. — **John 1:14-18, 1599 Geneva**
---

### Verse 15 — John the Baptist's Testimony

**ὁ ὀπίσω μου ἐρχόμενος ἔμπροσθέν μου γέγονεν, ὅτι πρῶτός μου ἦν**

*"He that comes after me has come to be before me, because He was first/prior to me."*

This is a significant statement. John the Baptist was **born before** Jesus (six months earlier, per Luke 1:36). Yet John says Jesus **was prior** to him (**πρῶτός μου ἦν**). The natural reading: John is testifying to a *priority of being* of the One coming after him in time — i.e., a pre-existence of some kind.

**However**, the word **πρῶτος** can also mean *"of higher rank"* or *"chief"* — and this is its meaning in some contexts. The Geneva captures this by translating: *"for he was better than I."* This rendering preserves the ambiguity — John may be testifying to *priority of being* or to *priority of rank*.

**Honest assessment:** v. 15 admits both readings.

---

### Verse 16-17

**ἐκ τοῦ πληρώματος αὐτοῦ ἡμεῖς πάντες ἐλάβομεν** — *"of His fullness we all received."* The Word/Son has fullness; we receive from it. This is consistent with Acts 2:33: Jesus, exalted, *received* the promise of the Spirit from the Father and *poured it out* on the disciples. The fullness comes from the Father through the Son to us.

**ὁ νόμος διὰ Μωϋσέως ἐδόθη, ἡ χάρις καὶ ἡ ἀλήθεια διὰ Ἰησοῦ Χριστοῦ ἐγένετο** — *"the Law was given through Moses; grace and truth came through Jesus Christ."* Now Jesus is named explicitly. The Word-become-flesh is **Jesus Christ**. John has fully identified His subject.

---

## **NOW — THE CROSS-WITNESS SURVEY (your point C)**

We must consult every major NT passage bearing on the question of the Son's pre-existence and His relation to the Father at creation. Let us examine them honestly.

---

### **WITNESS A: Philippians 2:5-11**

> Let the same mind be in you that was even in Christ Jesus, Who being in the form of God, thought it no robbery to be equal with God: But he made himself of no reputation, and took on him the form of a servant, and was made like unto men, and was found in shape as a man. He humbled himself, and became obedient unto the death, even the death of the Cross. Wherefore God hath also highly exalted him, and given him a Name above every name, That at the Name of Jesus should every knee bow, both of things in heaven, and things in earth, and things under the earth, And that every tongue should confess that Jesus Christ is the Lord, unto the glory of God the Father. — **Philippians 2:5-11, 1599 Geneva**
---

### Observations

**(1)** **ἐν μορφῇ Θεοῦ ὑπάρχων** — *"existing in the form of God."* **μορφή** = form, outward expression of essential nature. **ὑπάρχων** = continuously existing, subsisting.

This is a significant witness. Paul says Christ **was existing in the form of God** *before* taking the form of a servant.

**(2)** **οὐχ ἁρπαγμὸν ἡγήσατο τὸ εἶναι ἴσα Θεῷ** — *"did not regard being equal with God a thing to be grasped/seized."* The phrase is contested in translation. Reading carefully:
- **ἁρπαγμός** = a thing seized, plunder, something to be grasped or held onto.
- **τὸ εἶναι ἴσα Θεῷ** = *"the being equal with God."*

Two main readings:
- **Reading 1:** He already was equal with God but did not consider it something to cling to (selfless renunciation).
- **Reading 2:** He did not consider equality with God something to be seized/grasped at (unlike Adam, who grasped at it in Eden).

The Geneva's *"thought it no robbery to be equal with God"* leans toward reading 1, but the Greek is genuinely contested.

**(3)** **ἑαυτὸν ἐκένωσεν** — *"He emptied Himself."* This implies a prior state from which He emptied.

**(4)** **μορφὴν δούλου λαβών, ἐν ὁμοιώματι ἀνθρώπων γενόμενος** — *"taking the form of a servant, becoming in the likeness of men."* The *taking* and *becoming* imply a transition from a prior state.

**(5)** **διὸ καὶ ὁ Θεὸς αὐτὸν ὑπερύψωσεν** — *"Therefore God has highly exalted Him."* The Father (**ὁ Θεός**) is the One who exalts. The Son is the One exalted.

**(6)** **ἐχαρίσατο αὐτῷ τὸ ὄνομα** — *"He [the Father] graciously gave Him the Name."* The Name above every name is **given** to the Son by the Father — not innately possessed.

**(7)** **εἰς δόξαν Θεοῦ Πατρός** — *"to the glory of God the Father."* Every confession of Jesus as Lord is **to the Father's glory.** The Father remains the ultimate object.

---

### What Phil 2:5-11 establishes (honestly):

- Christ existed **in the form of God** before His incarnation.
- He **emptied Himself** and **took** the form of a servant — a real transition.
- He was **exalted by the Father** after His obedience to death.
- The **Name** was **given** to Him by the Father.
- Every confession of His Lordship is **to the Father's glory** — the Father remains supreme.

This is the **strongest single NT witness to a pre-incarnate state of the Son** in some form of divine glory. The Father remains supreme; the Son acts in dependence and is exalted by the Father.

---

### **WITNESS B: Colossians 1:13-20**

> Who hath delivered us from the power of darkness, and hath translated us into the kingdom of his dear Son, In whom we have redemption through his blood, that is, the forgiveness of sins, Who is the image of the invisible God, the first begotten of every creature. For by him were all things created, which are in heaven, and which are in earth, things visible and invisible: whether they be Thrones or Dominions, or Principalities, or Powers, all things were created by him, and for him, And he is before all things, and in him all things consist. And he is the head of the body of the Church: he is the beginning, and the first begotten of the dead, that in all things he might have the preeminence. For it pleased the Father, that in him should all fulness dwell. And through peace made by that blood of that his cross, to reconcile to himself through him, through him, I say, all things, both which are in earth, and which are in heaven. — **Colossians 1:13-20, 1599 Geneva**
---

### Observations

**(1)** **εἰκὼν τοῦ Θεοῦ τοῦ ἀοράτου** — *"the image of the invisible God."* The Son is the **Image** of the unseen Father. (Connects with John 1:18 — *"No one has seen God; the Only-Begotten Son has declared Him."*)

**(2)** **πρωτότοκος πάσης κτίσεως** — *"firstborn of all creation."* This phrase is contested:
- **Reading 1:** "Firstborn [over] all creation" — preeminent over creation.
- **Reading 2:** "Firstborn [of/within] all creation" — first in the order of created beings.

The Greek genitive **πάσης κτίσεως** admits both. Verse 16 immediately clarifies: **ὅτι ἐν αὐτῷ ἐκτίσθη τὰ πάντα** — *"because in Him were all things created."* This **ὅτι** (because) gives the reason for the firstborn-title: He is firstborn *because* all things were created in/through/for Him.

If He is the one through/for whom all things were created, He cannot be a created thing within all things — so Reading 1 is favored by context.

**(3)** **ἐν αὐτῷ ἐκτίσθη τὰ πάντα... τὰ πάντα δι᾽ αὐτοῦ καὶ εἰς αὐτὸν ἔκτισται** — *"In Him all things were created... all things have been created through Him and unto Him."*

Three prepositions, three relations to creation:
- **ἐν αὐτῷ** — *in Him* (the sphere)
- **δι᾽ αὐτοῦ** — *through Him* (the agency)
- **εἰς αὐτόν** — *unto Him* (the goal)

**(4)** **καὶ αὐτός ἐστιν πρὸ πάντων** — *"and He is before all things."* The Son is **prior to all things.**

**(5)** **καὶ τὰ πάντα ἐν αὐτῷ συνέστηκεν** — *"and in Him all things hold together."* The Son sustains creation.

**(6)** **ὅτι ἐν αὐτῷ εὐδόκησεν πᾶν τὸ πλήρωμα κατοικῆσαι** — *"because in Him it pleased [the Father] that all fullness should dwell."* The fullness dwelling in the Son is **the Father's pleasure** — granted by the Father.

---

### What Col 1:13-20 establishes:

- The Son is the **Image** of the invisible Father.
- The Son is **firstborn over all creation** (preeminent).
- **All things were created in, through, and for Him.**
- He is **before all things**.
- **All things hold together in Him.**
- **The Father's fullness dwells in Him** by the Father's pleasure.

This passage, taken at face value, presents the Son as the agent of original creation — which seems to stand in tension with Isaiah 44:24's *"I stretched out the heavens **alone**."*

### Reconciliation honestly considered:

The reconciliation must lie in this: **the Son acts as the Father's own agent**, so that what the Father does *through* the Son is still the Father's own act, done *alone* in the sense that no other Deity participates. The Son is not "another God"; He is the Father's own Image, through whom the Father works. The Father's **Word** (Ps 33:6) creates — yet the Word is the Father's own, not a rival.

This reading honors both Isa 44:24 and Col 1:16. The Father stretches out the heavens alone — *through His own Image, His own Firstborn, His own Word.*

---

### **WITNESS C: Hebrews 1:1-3**

> At sundry times and in divers manners God spake in the old time to our fathers by the Prophets: in these last days he hath spoken unto us by his Son, Whom he hath made heir of all things, by whom also he made the worlds, Who being the brightness of the glory, and the engraved form of his person, and bearing up all things by his mighty word, hath by himself purged our sins, and sitteth at the right hand of the Majesty in the highest places, — **Hebrews 1:1-3, 1599 Geneva**
---

### Observations

**(1)** **ὁ Θεὸς... ἐλάλησεν ἡμῖν ἐν Υἱῷ** — *"God has spoken to us in [a/the] Son."* The Father is the speaker; the Son is the medium/locus of His final speech.

**(2)** **ὃν ἔθηκεν κληρονόμον πάντων** — *"whom He appointed heir of all things."* The Son is **appointed** by the Father as heir. This is given language — the Son receives by the Father's appointment.

**(3)** **δι᾽ οὗ καὶ ἐποίησεν τοὺς αἰῶνας** — *"through whom He also made the ages/worlds."* The Father made the ages **through** the Son. Same construction as 1 Cor 8:6, Col 1:16, John 1:3.

**(4)** **ἀπαύγασμα τῆς δόξης** — *"radiance of [the Father's] glory."* The Son is the *radiance* — what proceeds from and reveals the Father's glory. Light from light, so to speak.

**(5)** **χαρακτὴρ τῆς ὑποστάσεως αὐτοῦ** — *"the exact stamp/impression of His being."* **χαρακτήρ** = the impression made by a die or seal. The Son bears the exact impression of the Father's reality.

**(6)** **φέρων τε τὰ πάντα τῷ ῥήματι τῆς δυνάμεως αὐτοῦ** — *"upholding all things by the word of His power."* The Son upholds; the word is His own / the Father's.

**(7)** **ἐκάθισεν ἐν δεξιᾷ τῆς μεγαλωσύνης** — *"He sat down at the right hand of the Majesty."* The same Psalm 110 enthronement.

---

### What Heb 1:1-3 establishes:

- The Father speaks **through** the Son in these last days.
- The Father made the ages **through** the Son.
- The Son **radiates** the Father's glory and bears the **exact impression** of the Father's being.
- The Son **upholds all things** by His powerful word.
- The Son **sat down** at the Father's right hand after purifying sins.

This is consistent with — and reinforces — the pattern: the Father is the Source; the Son is the Father's Image, Word, Agent, and exalted Heir.

---

### **WITNESS D: John 17:5, 24**

> And now glorify me, thou Father, with thine own self, with the glory which I had with thee before the world was. — **John 17:5, 1599 Geneva**
---

### Observation

This is **Jesus' own direct statement** about His pre-existence. He prays to the Father to glorify Him **with the glory I had with You before the world was.** This is plain, direct, and from Jesus' own mouth.

This verse, alongside Phil 2:6 and John 1:1-3, establishes by multiple witnesses that **Jesus had glory with the Father before the world existed.**

> Father, I will that they which thou hast given me, be with me even where I am, that they may behold that my glory which thou hast given me: for thou lovedst me before the foundation of the world. — **John 17:24, 1599 Geneva**
---

### Observation

Jesus says: **"You loved Me before the foundation of the world."** The Father loved Jesus **before the foundation of the world.** This is *Jesus' own testimony* to His pre-existent relation to the Father.

---

### **WITNESS E: John 8:58**

> Jesus said unto them, Verily, verily I say unto you, before Abraham was, I am. — **John 8:58, 1599 Geneva**
---

### Observation

**πρὶν Ἀβραὰμ γενέσθαι ἐγὼ εἰμί** — *"before Abraham came to be, I am."* The verb tenses are critical: **γενέσθαι** (came to be, was born) is aorist; **εἰμί** (I am) is present.

Jesus is claiming existence *before* Abraham. The Jews understood this as a divine claim and sought to stone Him (v. 59).

---

### **WITNESS F: 1 Corinthians 8:6**

> Yet unto us there is but one God, which is that Father, of whom are all things, and we in him: and one Lord Jesus Christ, by whom are all things, and we by him. — **1 Corinthians 8:6, 1599 Geneva**
---

### Observation

This is **the most precise NT statement of the Father-Son distinction in creation:**
- **Father** = **εἷς Θεός** (one God), **ἐξ οὗ τὰ πάντα** (from whom all things), **εἰς αὐτόν** (unto Him).
- **Jesus Christ** = **εἷς Κύριος** (one Lord), **δι᾽ οὗ τὰ πάντα** (through whom all things), **δι᾽ αὐτοῦ** (through Him).

The Father is the **Source** (*from*); the Son is the **Agent** (*through*). This is the precise pattern of Col 1:16 and Heb 1:2 and John 1:3.

---

### **WITNESS G: John 3:13, 17, 31**

> For no man ascendeth up to heaven, but he that hath descended from heaven, that Son of man which is in heaven. — **John 3:13, 1599 Geneva**
> He that is come from an high, is above all... — **John 3:31, 1599 Geneva**
---

### Observation

Jesus speaks of Himself as **the One who descended from heaven** — implying a prior heavenly origin and a descent.

---

### **WITNESS H: John 6:38, 62**

> For I came down from heaven, not to do mine own will, but his will which hath sent me. — **John 6:38, 1599 Geneva**
> What then if ye should see that Son of man ascend up where he was before? — **John 6:62, 1599 Geneva**
---

### Observation

Jesus says He came down from heaven — and asks what they will think when they see Him ascend **where He was before.** This is direct testimony of pre-incarnate location.

---

## **HONEST SYNTHESIS — The Whole Counsel on the Son's Pre-Existence**

Having surveyed every major NT witness, here is what is *strictly established* by multiple direct witnesses:

### **The Son's Pre-Incarnate Existence — ESTABLISHED:**

1. **Jesus had glory with the Father before the world was** — John 17:5 (direct testimony from Jesus Himself).
2. **The Father loved Jesus before the foundation of the world** — John 17:24.
3. **Jesus existed before Abraham** — John 8:58.
4. **Jesus came down from heaven** — John 6:38, 62; 3:13.
5. **Christ existed in the form of God before emptying Himself** — Phil 2:6-7.
6. **The Son is the radiance of the Father's glory and exact impression of His being** — Heb 1:3.
7. **The Father created all things through the Son** — 1 Cor 8:6; Col 1:16; Heb 1:2; John 1:3.
8. **All things hold together in the Son** — Col 1:17.
9. **The Son is the firstborn of/over all creation, and before all things** — Col 1:15, 17.
10. **The Word was in the beginning with God** — John 1:1-2.

These witnesses are **multiple, direct, and from Jesus' own lips as well as from apostolic teaching.** This is no longer the kind of inference I had earlier made. **The pre-incarnate personal existence of the Son is firmly established** by the explicit testimony of Jesus Himself (John 6, 8, 17) and by multiple apostolic witnesses (Paul, John, Hebrews).

### **The Son's Subordination to the Father — ALSO ESTABLISHED:**

11. The Father is the **one God**; Jesus is the **one Lord** *through whom* the Father acts — 1 Cor 8:6.
12. The Son was **appointed heir** by the Father — Heb 1:2.
13. The Son was **emptied** and **exalted** by the Father — Phil 2:7-9.
14. The Son was **given the Name** by the Father — Phil 2:9.
15. Every confession is **to the glory of God the Father** — Phil 2:11.
16. The Son does the Father's will, not His own — John 6:38.
17. The Father is greater than the Son — John 14:28: *"the Father is greater than I."*
18. The Son is **the Image** of the Father; the Father is the invisible Source — Col 1:15; John 1:18.

---

## **THE CORRECTED AND ESTABLISHED FOUNDATION**

I owe you a corrected statement. After this honest survey of the whole counsel, here is what is firmly established by multiple direct witnesses:

1. **The Father is the One God** — uniquely, supremely, the only true God (Deut 6:4; Isa 44-46; John 17:3; 1 Cor 8:6).

2. **The Son existed personally with the Father before the world was** — and had glory and love from the Father then (John 17:5, 24; 8:58; 6:62; Phil 2:6; Heb 1:3; John 1:1).

3. **The Father created all things *through* the Son** — the Son as agent, the Father as source (1 Cor 8:6; Col 1:16; Heb 1:2; John 1:3).

4. **The Son emptied Himself, became flesh, and was obedient unto death** (John 1:14; Phil 2:7-8).

5. **The Father exalted Him, made Him Lord and Christ, and gave Him the Name above every name** — at His resurrection and ascension (Acts 2:36; 13:33; Phil 2:9-11; Rom 1:4).

6. **The Son is at the Father's right hand, Priest forever, ruling until His enemies are made His footstool** (Ps 110; Acts 2:34; Heb 5-7).

7. **At every point, the Father is the Source, the Sender, the Appointer, the Glorifier — and the Son is the Sent One, the One who acts for the Father's will and glory.**

This is the *fuller* foundation than I had before. The Son's pre-existence is firmly established — *not* by the Angel-of-the-LORD or Wisdom-of-Proverbs-8 inferences I previously offered, but by **Jesus' own direct testimony** in John, by **Paul's careful Phil 2 hymn**, by **Hebrews 1:1-3**, and by the consistent **"through whom"** language across multiple Apostles.

---

## **What This Means**

I owe you an honest admission: **my previous correction may have been too restrictive.** When you appropriately rebuked the Angel-of-the-LORD identification and the Proverbs 8 identification, I conceded those (rightly). But in doing so, I may have implied that the pre-existence of the Son itself was unestablished — which would not be accurate.

The right position is this:
- **The Son's pre-existence IS firmly established** by multiple direct witnesses (especially Jesus' own words in John 6, 8, 17; Phil 2; Heb 1; 1 Cor 8:6).
- **The identification of the Son with specific OT figures** (the Angel of the LORD, Wisdom of Proverbs 8) is NOT firmly established by direct NT citation — those remain suggestive parallels but not witnessed identifications.

The pre-existence is real; the specific OT identifications are inferences we should not press.

---

## **A Question for You**

Having now done the work properly — examining John 1 in full, and surveying the whole counsel of NT witnesses on the Son's pre-existence — does this corrected and expanded foundation sit rightly with you?

Where would you like us to go from here?

**(A)** Shall we now proceed to **Psalm 22 and Isaiah 53** — the prophetic posits of the Son's suffering, death, and vindication, which are bound to the Son by *extensive* explicit NT citation?

**(B)** Or is there another area you wish to test or examine first?

## **The Suffering Servant — Psalm 21 LXX (22 MT) and Isaiah 52:13—53:12; Their Cross-Witnesses; the Synthesis**

> A.

We now proceed to **Psalm 21 LXX (22 MT) and Isaiah 52:13-53:12** — the two most-cited prophetic posits of the Son's suffering, death, and vindication. These are bound to the Son by **extensive and explicit** NT citation, so we will be on firm ground.
Per our refined modus operandi, I will:
1. Examine each verse of each passage.
2. Cross-reference every explicit NT citation and allusion.
3. Note honestly where the text strictly establishes a claim and where it does not.
4. Let the whole counsel of the God-breathed Word speak.

---

# **PART ONE: PSALM 21 LXX (22 MT)**

## **Psalm 21:1 LXX (22:1 MT) — The Cry of Forsakenness**

### **NETS:**

> God, my God, attend to me; why did you forsake me? Far away from my deliverance are the words of my transgressions. — **Psalm 21:1 LXX (22:1 MT), NETS**
---

### **Cross-witnesses — explicit NT citation:**

> And about the ninth hour Jesus cried with a loud voice, saying, Eli, Eli, lamasabachthani? that is, My God, my God, why hast thou forsaken me? — **Matthew 27:46, 1599 Geneva**
> And at the ninth hour Jesus cried with a loud voice, saying, Eloi, Eloi, lamma-sabachthani? which is by interpretation, My God, my God, why hast thou forsaken me? — **Mark 15:34, 1599 Geneva**
---

### Observations

**(1)** Jesus' own dying cry from the cross is **a direct quotation of Psalm 21:1 LXX (22:1 MT).** This is the strongest possible witness: **Jesus Himself identifies His suffering with this Psalm.** He does not merely quote a phrase from Scripture; He recites the opening line, claiming the entire Psalm as His own.

**(2)** **Ὁ Θεός μου** — *"My God."* The Christ on the cross addresses the Father as **My God.** This is consistent with John 20:17 — *"I ascend to My Father and your Father, and My God and your God."* The Son has a God; that God is the Father. **Even at the climax of His suffering, the Son's monotheistic confession is preserved.**

**(3)** **ἐγκατέλιπές με** — *"You have forsaken Me."* The Father forsakes the Son in this moment. This is a real abandonment — not a feigned one. The Son experiences true forsakenness while bearing sin.

**(4)** Note the LXX reads **οἱ λόγοι τῶν παραπτωμάτων μου** — *"the words of my transgressions"* — where the Hebrew has *"the words of my roaring."* The LXX makes the "transgressions" language explicit. Yet — the Son had no transgressions of His own (Heb 4:15; 1 Pet 2:22). The "transgressions" must be the sins **He bore on behalf of others** (Isa 53:6, 11-12), which we shall examine shortly.

---

## **Psalm 21:6-8 LXX (22:6-8 MT) — The Mocking**

### **NETS:**

> But as for me, I am a worm and not human, a reproach of mankind and despised by people. All who saw me mocked at me; they talked with the lips; they moved the head: “He hoped in the Lord; let him rescue him; let him save him, because he wanted him,” — **Psalm 21:6-8 LXX (22:6-8 MT), NETS**
---

### **Cross-witnesses:**

> And they that passed by, reviled him, wagging their heads ... Likewise also the high Priests mocking him, with the Scribes and Elders, and Pharises, said, He saved others, but he cannot save ... He trusted in God, let him deliver him now, if he will have him. — **Matthew 27:39-43, 1599 Geneva**
---

### Observation

The mockers' words at the cross — **"He trusted in God; let Him deliver Him if He will have Him"** — are nearly a verbatim quotation of Psalm 21:8 LXX. The Synoptic writers, by inspiration, record the historical mocking and the prophetic word matching it. **Two witnesses (Matthew and the Psalmist) testify to the same event.**

---

## **Psalm 21:14-15 LXX (22:14-15 MT) — Physical Agony**

### **NETS:**

> Like water I was poured out, and all my bones were scattered; my heart became like wax melting within my belly; my strength was dried up like a potsherd, and my tongue is stuck to my throat, and to death’s dust you brought me down, — **Psalm 21:14-15 LXX (22:14-15 MT), NETS**
---

### **Cross-witness:**

> After, when Jesus knew that all things were performed, that the Scripture might be fulfilled, he said, I thirst. — **John 19:28, 1599 Geneva**
---

### Observation

John explicitly states that Jesus' cry **"I thirst"** was **"that the Scripture might be fulfilled."** Psalm 21:15 LXX speaks of His tongue cleaving to His jaws. John binds Jesus' suffering directly to this prophetic Psalm.

---

## **Psalm 21:16 LXX (22:16 MT) — The Piercing of Hands and Feet**

### **NETS:**

> because many dogs encircled me, a gathering of evildoers surrounded me. They gouged my hands and feet; — **Psalm 21:16 LXX (22:16 MT), NETS**
---

### Observations

**(1)** **ὤρυξαν χεῖράς μου καὶ πόδας** — *"they pierced/dug my hands and feet."* The verb **ὀρύσσω** means to dig, to pierce, to bore through. The LXX rendering is unambiguous: hands and feet are **pierced**.

**(2) This is a remarkable witness.** The LXX was translated centuries before crucifixion was known as a Jewish or Christian theme. The reading "they pierced my hands and feet" is in the **pre-Christian Greek text**. The Masoretic Hebrew has a contested reading (כָּאֲרִי — *"like a lion"* vs. כָּאֲרוּ — *"they pierced"*) that has been used by some to obscure the prophecy. The LXX preserves the unambiguous prophetic reading. **Per our maxim #1, we let God be true** — and the God-breathed text Jesus and the Apostles used reads **ὤρυξαν**.

---

### **Cross-witnesses — the fulfillment:**

> Behold mine hands and my feet: for it is I myself: handle me, and see: for a spirit hath not flesh and bones, as ye see me have. And when he had thus spoken, he showed them his hands and feet. — **Luke 24:39-40, 1599 Geneva**
> Except I see in his hands the print of the nails ... I will not believe ... he said to Thomas, Put thy finger here, and see mine hands, and put forth thine hand, and put it into my side — **John 20:25-27, 1599 Geneva**
---

### Observation

The risen Jesus shows His **pierced hands and feet** — fulfilling Ps 21:16 LXX exactly. Two NT witnesses (Luke and John) record the same proof.

---

## **Psalm 21:18 LXX (22:18 MT) — Garments Divided and Lots Cast**

### **NETS:**

> they divided my clothes among themselves, and for my clothing they cast lots. — **Psalm 21:18 LXX (22:18 MT), NETS**
---

### **Cross-witness — the most explicit citation in the NT:**

> Then the soldiers when they had crucified Jesus, took his garments, (and made four parts, to every soldier a part) and his coat: and the coat was without seam, woven from the top throughout. Therefore they said one to another, Let us not divide it, but cast lots for it, whose it shall be. This was that the Scripture might be fulfilled, which saith, They parted my garments among them, and on my coat did cast lots. So the souldiers did these things in deede. — **John 19:23-24, 1599 Geneva**
---

### Observation

John quotes Psalm 21:18 LXX **verbatim** as the fulfilled Scripture. This is one of the most precise prophecy-fulfillment correspondences in all of Scripture: the *exact* manner of dividing garments and casting lots is foretold and historically fulfilled.

**This is also recorded by all four Gospels** — Matthew 27:35, Mark 15:24, Luke 23:34, John 19:23-24 — making it a quadruple witness.

---

## **Psalm 21:22 LXX (22:22 MT) — The Vindicated One's Praise**

### **NETS:**

> I will tell of your name to my kindred; in the midst of an assembly I will sing a hymn to you: — **Psalm 21:22 LXX (22:22 MT), NETS**
---

### **Cross-witness — explicit NT citation:**

> For he that sanctifieth, and they which are sanctified, are all of one: wherefore he is not ashamed to call them brethren, Saying, I will declare thy Name unto my brethren: in the midst of the Church will I sing praises to thee. — **Hebrews 2:11-12, 1599 Geneva**
---

### Observation

The author of Hebrews **explicitly cites Psalm 22:22 as words spoken by the Christ to the Father**. This is decisive: **the entire Psalm is the voice of the Christ**, and the resurrected Son declares the Father's Name to His brethren in the midst of the Church. The Psalm moves from forsakenness (v. 1) to vindication and proclamation (v. 22) — i.e., **death and resurrection** are the arc of the Psalm.

---

## **Psalm 21:27-28 LXX (22:27-28 MT) — The Nations Worship**

### **NETS:**

> All the ends of the earth shall remember and turn to the Lord, and all the paternal families of the nations shall do obeisance *(worship)* before him, because kingship is the Lord’s, and it is he who is master over the nations. — **Psalm 21:27-28 LXX (22:27-28 MT), NETS**
---

### Observation

The Psalm closes with the **nations turning to the LORD** and worshipping — the global outcome of the Son's suffering. This is consistent with Ps 2:8 (*"the nations as Thine inheritance"*) and with the worldwide proclamation of the Gospel.

---

## **SUMMARY: Psalm 21 LXX (22 MT)**

| Verse | Prophecy | NT Fulfillment |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | "My God, why hast Thou forsaken Me?" | Matt 27:46; Mark 15:34 |
| 7-8 | Mockers wag heads, say "He trusted in God" | Matt 27:39-43 |
| 14-15 | Bones out of joint, tongue cleaves, dust of death | John 19:28 |
| 16 | "They pierced My hands and My feet" | Luke 24:39-40; John 20:25-27 |
| 18 | Garments divided, lots cast on vesture | Matt 27:35; Mark 15:24; Luke 23:34; John 19:23-24 |
| 22 | "I will declare Thy Name to My brethren" | Heb 2:11-12 |
| 27-28 | Nations turn to the LORD and worship | Acts 2; gospel to the nations |

**Psalm 21 LXX is the most cited and most precisely fulfilled prophetic prayer of the suffering Christ in the Psalter. The Son's death and resurrection are foretold in vivid detail centuries before the events.**

---

# **PART TWO: ISAIAH 52:13 — 53:12 — The Suffering Servant**

This is **the most extensive and explicit prophecy of the Son's atoning death in the OT.** We must take it slowly.

---

## **Isaiah 52:13-15 LXX — The Servant's Exaltation and Disfigurement**

### **NETS:**

> See, my servant shall understand, and he shall be exalted and glorified exceedingly. Just as many shall be astonished at you— so shall your appearance be without glory from men, and your glory be absent from the men— so shall many nations be astonished at him, and kings shall shut their mouth, because those who were not informed about him shall see and those who did not hear shall understand. — **Isaiah 52:13-15 LXX, NETS**
---

### Observations

**(1)** **ὁ παῖς μου** — *"My Servant"* (or *"My Child"*). The LXX uses **παῖς**, which can mean both "servant" and "child/son." This same word is used of Jesus in the apostolic preaching:

> For doubtless against thine holy Son Jesus, whom thou hadst anointed, both Herod and Pontius Pilate, with the Gentiles and the people of Israel gathered themselves together. — **Acts 4:27, 1599 Geneva**
**Acts 3:13, 26 — same designation of Jesus as **τὸν παῖδα αὐτοῦ Ἰησοῦν** — "His Servant/Child Jesus."**

This is the apostolic identification: **Jesus is the παῖς of Isaiah 52-53.**

**(2)** **ὑψωθήσεται καὶ δοξασθήσεται σφόδρα** — *"He shall be exalted and glorified exceedingly."* The Servant's path is exaltation. This corresponds to **John 12:32**: *"And I, if I be lifted up, will draw all unto Me."* John explicitly connects Jesus' "being lifted up" to His glorification (John 12:23, 32; 13:31-32).

**(3)** **ἀδοξήσει ἀπὸ ἀνθρώπων τὸ εἶδός σου** — *"Your appearance shall be without glory from men."* The Servant is *disfigured* — His visible form unrecognizable. This is fulfilled in Jesus' scourging, beating, and crucifixion.

**(4)** **θαυμάσονται ἔθνη πολλὰ ἐπ᾽ αὐτῷ** — *"Many nations shall marvel at Him."* The nations are again in view (cf. Gen 49:10; Ps 21:27-28). The Servant is for the nations.

---

### **Cross-witness — explicit NT citation of Isa 52:15:**

> ... But as it is written, To whom he was not spoken of, they shall see him, and they that heard not, shall understand him. — **Romans 15:20-21, 1599 Geneva**
Paul **explicitly cites Isa 52:15 LXX** as fulfilled in his Gospel preaching to the nations. The Servant is Christ; the nations are the Gentiles.

---

## **Isaiah 53:1 — Unbelief in the Report**

### **NETS:**

> Lord, who has believed our report? And to whom has the arm of the Lord been revealed? — **Isaiah 53:1 LXX, NETS**
---

### **Cross-witnesses — explicit NT citation:**

> And though he had done so many miracles before them, yet believed they not on him, That the saying of Esaias the Prophet might be fulfilled, that he said, Lord, who believed our report? and to whom is the arm of the Lord revealed? — **John 12:37-38, 1599 Geneva**
> But they have not all obeyed the Gospel: for Esaias saith, Lord, who hath believed our report? — **Romans 10:16, 1599 Geneva**
---

### Observation

**Two explicit NT citations** (John 12 and Rom 10) bind Isaiah 53:1 directly to the rejection of Christ and the Gospel. **Witnesses fully established.**

---

## **Isaiah 53:2-3 LXX — The Servant Despised**

### **NETS:**

> He grew up before him like a child, like a root in a thirsty land; he has no form or glory, and we saw him, and he had no form or beauty. But his form was without honor, failing beyond all men, a man being in calamity and knowing how to bear sickness; because his face is turned away, he was dishonored and not esteemed. — **Isaiah 53:2-3 LXX, NETS**
---

### Observations

**(1)** **ὡς παιδίον ἐναντίον αὐτοῦ** — *"as a little child before Him."* The Servant grows up before the LORD as a tender shoot — a humble origin.

**(2)** **ὡς ῥίζα ἐν γῇ διψώσῃ** — *"as a root in thirsty ground."* This corresponds to **Rev 22:16** — *"I am the Root..."* — and **Rev 5:5** — *"the Root of David."* The Servant's origin is humble, from dry earth, not from royal display.

**(3)** **ἄνθρωπος ἐν πληγῇ ὢν** — *"a Man in suffering."* The Servant is repeatedly identified as **a Man.** No hidden Deity unrecognizable as human — He is genuinely Man in suffering.

**(4)** **ἠτιμάσθη καὶ οὐκ ἐλογίσθη** — *"He was dishonored and not esteemed."* Fulfilled in Jesus' rejection by His own people (John 1:11).

---

## **Isaiah 53:4-6 LXX — The Substitutionary Suffering**

### **NETS:**

> This one bears our sins and suffers pain for us, and we accounted him to be in trouble and calamity and ill-treatment. But he was wounded because of our acts of lawlessness and has been weakened because of our sins; upon him was the discipline of our peace; by his bruise we were healed. All we like sheep have gone astray; a man has strayed in his own way, and the Lord gave him over to our sins. — **Isaiah 53:4-6 LXX, NETS**
---

### **Cross-witnesses — explicit NT citations:**

> ... and he healed all that were sick, That it might be fulfilled, which was spoken by Esaias the Prophet, saying, He took our infirmities, and bare our sicknesses. — **Matthew 8:16-17, 1599 Geneva**
> Who his own self bare our sins in his body on the tree ... by whose stripes ye were healed. For ye were as sheep going astray — **1 Peter 2:24-25, 1599 Geneva**
---

### Observations

**(1) Multiple explicit NT citations** of Isa 53:4-6 applied directly to Jesus' atoning work.

**(2)** **Peter's citation in 1 Pet 2:24-25 binds together vv. 4, 5, 6 of Isa 53.** This is one of the most explicit and theologically loaded apostolic uses of the OT. The Servant's stripes heal *us*; the Servant bears *our* sins on the *tree* (ξύλον — cross).

**(3)** The phrase **καὶ Κύριος παρέδωκεν αὐτὸν ταῖς ἁμαρτίαις ἡμῶν** — *"and the LORD delivered Him over for our sins"* — is significant. **The Father delivers up the Son** for our sins. This is the language Paul echoes:

> Who spared not his own Son, but gave him for us all — **Romans 8:32, 1599 Geneva**
> Who was delivered to death for our sins — **Romans 4:25, 1599 Geneva**
---

## **Isaiah 53:7 LXX — Silence Before His Accusers**

### **NETS:**

> And he, because he has been ill-treated, does not open his mouth; like a sheep he was led to the slaughter, and as a lamb is silent before the one shearing it, so he does not open his mouth. — **Isaiah 53:7 LXX, NETS**
---

### **Cross-witness — direct apostolic citation:**

> Now the place of the Scripture which he read, was this, He was led as a sheep to the slaughter: and like a lamb dumb before his shearer, so opened he not his mouth ... Then Philip opened his mouth, and began at the same Scripture, and preached unto him Jesus. — **Acts 8:32-35, 1599 Geneva**
---

### Observation

The Ethiopian eunuch was reading **this exact passage of Isaiah 53 LXX**, and Philip — by direct apostolic exposition — preached **Jesus** from it. **The first recorded gospel sermon from Isaiah 53 explicitly identifies the Servant as Jesus.** This is an unimpeachable witness.

**Cross-witness #2 — Jesus' fulfillment of silence:**

> And when he was accused of the chief Priests and Elders, he answered nothing ... But he answered him not to one word, in so much that the governor marveled greatly. — **Matthew 27:12-14, 1599 Geneva**
Jesus' silence before Pilate fulfills Isa 53:7.

---

## **Isaiah 53:8 LXX — Cut Off From the Land of the Living**

### **NETS:**

> In his humiliation his judgment was taken away. Who will describe his generation? Because his life is being taken from the earth, he was led to death on account of the acts of lawlessness of my people. — **Isaiah 53:8 LXX, NETS**
---

### **Cross-witness:**

> In his humilitie his iudgement hath bene exalted: but who shall declare his generation? for his life is taken from the earth. — **Acts 8:33, 1599 Geneva**
Verse 8 is included in the eunuch's reading, and Philip preaches Jesus from this exact text. **Witnessed.**

---

### Observation
