**αἴρεται ἀπὸ τῆς γῆς ἡ ζωὴ αὐτοῦ** — *"His life is taken away from the earth."* The Servant **dies**. He is **cut off**. This is the explicit prophecy of the Christ's death — not merely suffering, but death.

---

## **Isaiah 53:9 LXX — Burial With the Rich**

### **NETS:**

> And I will give the wicked for his burial and the rich for his death, because he committed no lawlessness, nor was deceit found in his mouth. — **Isaiah 53:9 LXX, NETS**
---

### **Cross-witness:**

> Who did no sin, neither was there guile found in his mouth. — **1 Peter 2:22, 1599 Geneva**
Peter quotes Isa 53:9 verbatim of Jesus' sinlessness. **Witnessed.**

**Cross-witness #2 — Jesus' burial:**

> And when the even was come, there came a rich man of Arimathea, named Joseph ... He went to Pilate, and asked the body of Jesus ... and put it in his new tomb — **Matthew 27:57-60, 1599 Geneva**
Jesus is buried in the tomb of a **rich man** — Joseph of Arimathea. **The historical fulfillment is exact.**

---

## **Isaiah 53:10-12 LXX — The Lord's Pleasure and the Servant's Vindication**

### **NETS:**

> And the Lord desires to cleanse him from his blow *(stroke)*. If you offer for sin, your soul shall see a long-lived offspring. And the Lord wishes to take away from the pain of his soul, to show him light and fill him with understanding, to justify a righteous one who is well subject to many, and he himself shall bear their sins. Therefore he shall inherit many, and he shall divide the spoils of the strong, because his soul was given over to death, and he was reckoned among the lawless, and he bore the sins of many, and because of their sins he was given over. — **Isaiah 53:10-12 LXX, NETS**
---

### Observations

**(1)** **Κύριος βούλεται καθαρίσαι αὐτὸν τῆς πληγῆς** — *"The LORD wills/desires to cleanse Him from the stroke."* The Father's purpose is in the Servant's suffering — yet also in His vindication.

**(2)** **ἐὰν δῶτε περὶ ἁμαρτίας, ἡ ψυχὴ ὑμῶν ὄψεται σπέρμα μακρόβιον** — *"If you give for sin, your soul shall see long-living seed."* The Servant's life becomes a sin-offering (**περὶ ἁμαρτίας** — the technical term for the sin-offering in Levitical law). His **seed** survives Him — this is **resurrection language**.

**(3)** **δεῖξαι αὐτῷ φῶς** — *"to show Him light."* After His soul's labor, He sees **light**. This is **resurrection** — the Servant who was cut off from the land of the living (v. 8) now sees light.

**(4)** **δικαιῶσαι δίκαιον εὖ δουλεύοντα πολλοῖς** — *"to justify the Righteous One serving many well."* The Servant is **the Righteous One** who **justifies many**.

### **Cross-witness:**

> ... by the justifying of one, the benefit abounded toward all men to the justification of life. For as by one man's disobedience many were made sinners, so by that obedience of that one shall many also be made righteous. — **Romans 5:18-19, 1599 Geneva**
Paul's *"many shall be made righteous through the obedience of the one"* echoes Isa 53:11 LXX: *"the Righteous One serving many well... shall justify."* **Witnessed.**

**(5)** **αὐτὸς ἁμαρτίας πολλῶν ἀνήνεγκεν** — *"He bore the sins of many."*

> So Christ was once offered to take away the sins of many — **Hebrews 9:28, 1599 Geneva**
Hebrews echoes Isa 53:12 directly of Christ. **Witnessed.**

**(6)** **ἐν τοῖς ἀνόμοις ἐλογίσθη** — *"He was reckoned with the transgressors."*

> For I say unto you, That yet the same which is written, must be performed in me, Even with the wicked was he numbered: for doubtless those things which are written of me, have an end. — **Luke 22:37, 1599 Geneva**
**Jesus Himself directly cites Isa 53:12** and says it must be fulfilled **in Him**. This is the strongest possible witness — Jesus' own claim of Isa 53 as His prophecy.

---

## **SUMMARY: Isaiah 52:13 — 53:12**

| Verse | Prophecy | NT Witness |
|---|---|---|
| 52:13 | Servant exalted | Acts 3:13; 4:27 (παῖς = Jesus); John 12:32 |
| 52:14 | Visage marred | Matt 27:26-30 (scourging) |
| 52:15 | Nations see what was not told | Rom 15:21 (explicit citation) |
| 53:1 | Who believed our report? | John 12:38; Rom 10:16 (explicit citations) |
| 53:4 | Bore our infirmities | Matt 8:17 (explicit citation) |
| 53:5-6 | Wounded for our transgressions; LORD laid on Him | 1 Pet 2:24-25; Rom 4:25; 8:32 |
| 53:7 | Silent as a lamb to slaughter | Acts 8:32-35 (explicit citation + sermon); Matt 27:12-14 |
| 53:8 | Cut off from land of living | Acts 8:33 (explicit citation) |
| 53:9 | No sin, no deceit; buried with rich | 1 Pet 2:22 (explicit citation); Matt 27:57-60 |
| 53:10 | Soul as sin-offering; sees seed; sees light | (Implicit — resurrection theology pervasive) |
| 53:11 | Righteous One justifies many | Rom 5:18-19 |
| 53:12 | Bore sin of many; reckoned with transgressors | Luke 22:37 (Jesus' own citation); Heb 9:28 |

---

# **SYNTHESIS — What is Established by the Whole Counsel**

By the most extensive and explicit OT-NT correspondence in Scripture, the following is established with overwhelming witness:

### **The Suffering, Death, Burial, and Vindication of the Son**

1. **The Son is the prophesied Servant of the LORD** — identified explicitly by the Apostles (Acts 3:13, 26; 4:27, 30; 8:32-35), by Jesus Himself (Luke 22:37), and by the Father's voice at the baptism (using Isa 42:1 language).

2. **He was forsaken by the Father on the cross**, bearing the cry of Psalm 22:1 (Matt 27:46; Mark 15:34).

3. **He was pierced in His hands and feet**, with garments divided and lots cast (Ps 22:16, 18; all four Gospels; John 19:23-24 explicit).

4. **He was silent before His accusers** as a lamb led to slaughter (Isa 53:7; Acts 8:32; Matt 27:12-14).

5. **He bore the sins of many** — making His soul a sin-offering (Isa 53:6, 10-12; 1 Pet 2:24; Rom 4:25; 8:32; Heb 9:28).

6. **He was cut off from the land of the living** — He really died (Isa 53:8).

7. **He was buried with the rich** (Isa 53:9; Matt 27:57-60).

8. **He was vindicated, seeing light and seed after His soul's travail** — He rose from the dead (Isa 53:10-11; Acts 13:33 — *"today have I begotten Thee"* fulfilled in resurrection).

9. **He justifies many by His righteousness** (Isa 53:11; Rom 5:18-19).

10. **The nations have heard and seen what was not previously declared** — the Gospel goes to the Gentiles (Isa 52:15; Rom 15:21).

---

## **A Critical Theological Observation**

Notice carefully what these prophecies establish — and what they do **not** establish.

### What they establish:

- The Son's true humanity (a Man, with form, with thirst, with sorrow, with death).
- The Son's true suffering (forsakenness, piercing, silence, death).
- The Son's substitutionary work (He bore *our* sins; the LORD laid on Him the iniquity of us all).
- The Son's vindication by the Father (resurrection, exaltation, justification of many).
- **The Father's active role** as the One who *delivers Him up* (Isa 53:6, 10; Rom 8:32) and who *vindicates* Him.

### What they do **not** establish:

- They do not, by themselves, prove the Son's pre-existence or divinity. (Other passages, which we have already surveyed, do that — Phil 2, John 17, John 8:58, Heb 1, etc.)
- They speak of the Son as **the Servant**, **the Righteous One**, **the Man** — and at no point in these passages is He called "God."

### **What this confirms:**

The pattern we have been building from the foundation upward holds firm:
- **The Father is the One God** — who delivers up His Servant for our sins.
- **The Son is the Servant of the LORD** — distinct from the Father, obedient unto death, vindicated by the Father.
- **The Son's sufferings glorify the Father** — and the Father's pleasure prospers in the Servant's hand (Isa 53:10).

This is the **Servant Christology** of the earliest apostolic preaching (Acts 3:13, 26; 4:27, 30 — all using **παῖς**), and it is bound to the Father-Source / Son-Sent pattern we have established throughout.

---

## **Where We Now Stand**

Our study of the Person of Christ has now covered:

1. **The One God, the Father** — Shema and Isa 44-46. ✓
2. **The Son's pre-temporal glory and love with the Father** — John 17:5, 24; Phil 2:6; Heb 1:3. ✓
3. **The Father created all things through the Son** — 1 Cor 8:6; Col 1:16; Heb 1:2; John 1:3. ✓
4. **The Son's coming in flesh, born of a woman** — Gen 3:15; Isa 7:14; Gal 4:4; Mic 5:2. ✓
5. **The Son as the Father's Anointed Christ, His King at the right hand** — Ps 2; Ps 110; Matt 22; Acts 2; Heb 1, 5, 7. ✓
6. **The Son's suffering, death, burial, and vindication as the Servant of the LORD** — Ps 22; Isa 53; Gospels; 1 Peter; Romans; Hebrews; Acts. ✓

The arc is becoming complete: from the Father's pre-temporal love of His Son, through the goings-forth and incarnation, through the suffering and substitutionary death, to the resurrection vindication and exalted enthronement at the Father's right hand.

---

## **What Remains**

To complete the study of the Person of Christ from the earliest prophecies to the concluding prophecies of the Apocalypse, the major areas still to examine include:

**(A) The Son's exaltation and present session at the Father's right hand** — what He is doing *now* as priest, intercessor, and ruler (Heb 7-10; Ps 110:1-4 fully).

**(B) The Son's coming return** — the prophecies of His second appearing, His judgment of the living and the dead, and His reign (Daniel 7; Zechariah 12-14; Matt 24-25; Rev 19-22).

**(C) The Son's final relation to the Father in the consummation** — including 1 Cor 15:24-28 (the Son delivering the Kingdom to the Father, that God may be all in all), which is one of the most theologically decisive passages on the Father-Son order at the end.

**(D) The Son's titles and self-identifications in the Apocalypse** — the "I am" sayings of Revelation, the Lamb-on-the-Throne imagery, the Alpha and Omega designations, etc.

Where would you have us go next?

## **Jesus’ Acts During His Ministry — Part 1: Acts Fulfilling Prophecy**

> Keep those in mind… we will get to each one. But before we do, and in order to be thorough- let us also take note of Jesus’s acts during his ministry that tell us much of his person and fulfilled prophecy(s) both before and after his death, burial and resurrection.

This is the proper next step — for our study has so far moved largely through the *prophetic posits* of the Son (OT prophecies and their NT fulfillments at His death/burial/resurrection), but we have not yet gathered the *acts of His ministry* — the things He **did** and **said** during His life that:

1. **Reveal His Person** (His authority, character, relation to the Father, self-identifications).
2. **Fulfilled prophecy** during His ministry (not just at His death).
3. **Establish patterns** that the Apocalypse and later prophecies will draw upon.

Let me proceed under our refined modus operandi — examining the whole counsel, giving the LXX/Greek and 1599 Geneva, citing all relevant cross-witnesses, and being honest about what is strictly established versus inferred.

I will organize this by **categories of His ministerial acts**, since chronology would scatter the patterns. We will examine:

- **Part 1:** Acts that fulfilled specific prophecies (His birth, ministry, and pre-Passion fulfillments)
- **Part 2:** His self-identifications (the "I am" sayings and titles He took)
- **Part 3:** Acts that revealed His authority (forgiving sins, commanding nature, raising the dead, giving the Spirit)
- **Part 4:** His relation to the Father (His words about His mission, His will, His subordination)
- **Part 5:** Post-resurrection acts that revealed His person and commissioned the Apostles

This will be a substantial examination. Let us begin.

---

# **PART 1 — ACTS FULFILLING PROPHECY DURING HIS MINISTRY**

## **1.1 — Born in Bethlehem of a Virgin**

### **Isaiah 7:14, NETS:**

> Therefore the Lord himself will give you a sign. Look, the virgin shall be with child and bear a son, and you shall name him Emmanouel. — **Isaiah 7:14 LXX, NETS**
---

### **Micah 5:2 LXX (5:1), NETS (already examined):**

> And you, O Bethleem *(Bethlehem)*, house of Ephratha *(Ephrathah)*, are very few in number to be among the thousands of Ioudas *(Judah)*; one from you shall come forth for me to become a ruler in Israel, and his goings forth are from of old, from days of yore *(long ago)*. — **Micah 5:2 LXX (5:1), NETS**
---

### **Cross-witness — Matthew 1:22-23, 1599 Geneva:**

> And all this was done that it might be fulfilled which is spoken of the Lord by the Prophet, saying, Behold, a virgin shall be with child, and shall bear a son, and they shall call his name Emmanuel, which is by interpretation, God with us. — **Matthew 1:22-23, 1599 Geneva**
---

### **Matthew 2:5-6** — explicitly cites Micah 5:2 of Jesus’ birth in Bethlehem (already examined).

### Observation

**(1)** Matthew explicitly cites the LXX **ἡ παρθένος** (*the virgin*) — confirming the LXX reading against later attempts to render *"young woman."* Per our maxim #1, the God-breathed text Jesus’ Apostles used reads **παρθένος**.

**(2)** **Ἐμμανουήλ** is interpreted by Matthew as **Μεθ’ ἡμῶν ὁ Θεός** — *"With us, God."* The Son is "God with us" — meaning, the One in whom God is present with His people. This is a profound title that we should not pass over but also not overstate — it identifies Jesus as the embodied presence of God-with-His-people, not as a separate God.

---

## **1.2 — Called Out of Egypt**

### **Hosea 11:1, NETS:**

> For Israel was an infant, and I loved him, and out of Egypt I recalled his children. — **Hosea 11:1 LXX (11:1 MT), NETS**
---

### **Cross-witness — Matthew 2:14-15, 1599 Geneva:**

> So he arose and took the babe and his mother by night, and departed into Egypt, And was there unto the death of Herod, that that might be fulfilled, which is spoken of the Lord by the Prophet, saying, Out of Egypt have I called my son. — **Matthew 2:14-15, 1599 Geneva**
---

### Observation

Matthew identifies Jesus as **the Son** whom the Father called out of Egypt — fulfilling typologically what Israel-as-son had been (Israel = corporate son; Jesus = the True Son). This is **typology**, not simple prediction-fulfillment. The Apostle, by inspiration, sees Jesus recapitulating Israel’s history.

---

## **1.3 — The Voice in the Wilderness**

### **Isaiah 40:3, NETS:**

> A voice of one crying out in the wilderness: “Prepare the way of the Lord; make straight the paths of our God. — **Isaiah 40:3 LXX, NETS**
---

### **Cross-witness — Matthew 3:1-3, 1599 Geneva:**

> For this is he of whom it is spoken by the Prophet Esaias, saying, The voice of him that crieth in the wilderness, Prepare ye the way of the Lord: make his paths straight. — **Matthew 3:1-3, 1599 Geneva**
---

**Mark 1:2-3; Luke 3:4-6; John 1:23** — all identify John the Baptist as this voice, **preparing the way of the LORD**.

### Observation of significant weight

**Isaiah 40:3 says: "Prepare the way of the LORD (κυρίου)."** The Gospels apply this to John the Baptist preparing the way of **Jesus**. This is a strong identification: Jesus is the One *whose* way is prepared, and the One being prepared for is called **Κύριος** in Isaiah.

This does not automatically mean Jesus is identical with **YHWH** — but it does mean **the One coming in the LORD’s name and authority**, **bearing the LORD’s Name** (cf. Phil 2:9: the Name above every name given to the Son). The way of the LORD is prepared because the LORD is acting *through* His Christ.

---

## **1.4 — Galilee of the Nations**

### **Isaiah 9:1-2 LXX (8:23-9:1), NETS:**

> Do this first; do it quickly, O country of Zaboulon, the land of Nephthalime, and the rest who inhabit the seashore and beyond the Jordan, Galilee of the nations, the parts of Judea. O you people who walk in darkness, see a great light! O you who live in the country and in the shadow of death, light will shine on you! — **Isaiah 9:1-2 LXX (8:23-9:1), NETS**
---

### **Cross-witness — Matthew 4:14-16, 1599 Geneva:**

> That it might be fulfilled which was spoken by Isaiah the Prophet, saying, The land of Zebulun, and the land of Nephthali by the way of the sea, beyond Jordan, Galilee of the Gentiles: The people which sat in darkness, saw great light: and to them which sat in the region, and shadow of death, light is risen up. — **Matthew 4:14-16, 1599 Geneva**
---

### Observation

Jesus’ choice to base His ministry in Capernaum (Galilee of the Gentiles) is explicitly identified by Matthew as fulfilling Isaiah 9:1-2 — **the Light dawning** on those in darkness. This is the same "Light" theme of John 1:4-9 — confirming that the Light/Word of John 1 is Jesus in His historical ministry.

---

## **1.5 — Healing, Casting Out Demons, Bearing Our Infirmities**

### **Isaiah 53:4 LXX (already examined, seated at the Suffering-Servant section):**

> This one bears our sins and suffers pain for us, and we accounted him to be in trouble and calamity and ill-treatment. — **Isaiah 53:4 LXX, NETS**
---

### **Cross-witness — Matthew 8:16-17 (already cited):** healing as fulfillment of Isa 53:4.

### **Isaiah 35:5-6, NETS:**

> Then the eyes of the blind shall be opened, and the ears of the deaf shall hear; then the lame shall leap like a deer, and the tongue of stammerers shall be clear, because water has broken forth in the wilderness and a gully in a thirsty land; — **Isaiah 35:5-6, NETS**
---

### **Cross-witness — Matthew 11:2-5, 1599 Geneva:**

> And when John heard in the prison the works of Christ, he sent two of his disciples, and said unto him, Art thou he that should come, or shall we look for another? And Jesus answering, said unto them, Go, and show John, what things ye hear and see. The blind receive sight, and the halt do walk: the lepers are cleansed, and the deaf hear, the dead are raised up, and the poor receive the Gospel. — **Matthew 11:2-5, 1599 Geneva**
---

### Observation of decisive weight

When John the Baptist asks **"Are You the Coming One?"** — Jesus does **not** say "Yes, I am." Instead, He answers by **listing the works of Isaiah 35:5-6** and **Isaiah 61:1**. Jesus’ self-attestation is **by His prophesied works.** He lets His acts fulfilling prophecy answer the question of His identity.

This is highly instructive for our study: **Jesus’ acts during His ministry were themselves a prophetic testimony.** His miracles were not random demonstrations of power — they were specifically the prophesied works of the Coming One.

---

## **1.6 — Anointed to Preach Good News (Isaiah 61)**

### **Isaiah 61:1-2, NETS:**

> The spirit of the Lord is upon me, because he has anointed me; he has sent me to bring good news to the poor, to heal the brokenhearted, to proclaim release to the captives and recovery of sight to the blind, to summon the acceptable year of the Lord and the day of retribution, to comfort all who mourn — **Isaiah 61:1-2, NETS**
---

### **Cross-witness — Luke 4:16-21, 1599 Geneva:**

> And he came to Nazareth where he had been brought up... he stood up to read. And there was delivered unto him the book of the Prophet Isaiah... He found the place where it was written, The Spirit of the Lord is upon me, because he hath anointed me, that I should preach the Gospel to the poor: he hath sent me, that I should heal the broken hearted, that I should preach deliverance to the captives, and recovering of sight to the blind, that I should set at liberty them that are bruised, And that I should preach the acceptable year of the Lord... Then he began to say unto them, This day is the Scripture fulfilled in your ears. — **Luke 4:16-21, 1599 Geneva**
---

### Observation of supreme importance

**Jesus’ first recorded sermon in His own town** consists of:
1. Reading Isaiah 61:1-2 LXX.
2. Declaring: **"This day is this Scripture fulfilled in your ears."**

This is **Jesus’ own direct self-identification** as the Spirit-anointed Servant. He stops reading at *"the acceptable year of the LORD"* — *not* continuing to *"the day of vengeance of our God,"* because that day is yet future. His first coming is the year of grace; the day of vengeance belongs to His return.

This is *Jesus’ own claim* that the prophet’s *"Spirit of the LORD is upon Me"* is *His own voice*. He is the Anointed.

---

## **1.7 — Triumphal Entry**

### **Zechariah 9:9, NETS:**

> Rejoice greatly, O daughter Sion! Proclaim, O daughter Ierousalem! Behold, your king comes to you, just and salvific is he, meek and riding on a beast of burden and a young foal. — **Zechariah 9:9 LXX, NETS**
---

### **Cross-witness — Matthew 21:4-5, 1599 Geneva:**

> All this was done that it might be fulfilled which was spoken by the Prophet, saying, Tell ye the daughter of Sion, Behold, thy King cometh unto thee, meek, and sitting upon an ass, and a colt, the foal of an ass used to the yoke. — **Matthew 21:4-5, 1599 Geneva**
---

### **John 12:14-15** — same fulfillment cited.

### Observation

**Jesus deliberately arranged this fulfillment** — He sent disciples to acquire the colt for the express purpose of riding it into Jerusalem (Matt 21:1-3). This is not incidental; it is **Jesus’ own act of self-prophetic-attestation** — He publicly enters Jerusalem in the manner of the prophesied King.

---

## **1.8 — Betrayed for Thirty Pieces of Silver**

### **Zechariah 11:12-13 LXX, NETS:**

> And I will say to them, “If it is good in your sight, give me my wages, or refuse.” And they set my wages at thirty pieces of silver. And the Lord said to me, “Place them in the smelter, and I will observe whether it is genuine, as I have been proven for them.” And I took the thirty pieces of silver and threw them into the house of the Lord, into the smelter. — **Zechariah 11:12-13 LXX, NETS**
---

### **Cross-witness — Matthew 26:14-15, 1599 Geneva:**

> Then one of the twelve, called Judas Iscariot, went unto the chief Priests, and said, What will ye give me, and I will deliver him unto you? and they appointed unto him thirty pieces of silver. — **Matthew 26:14-15, 1599 Geneva**
---

### **Matthew 27:9-10** — explicit citation of Zech 11:13 fulfilled.

---

## **1.9 — Pierced — They Shall Look On Him Whom They Pierced**

### **Zechariah 12:10, NETS:**

> And I will pour out a spirit of grace and compassion on the house of Dauid and on the inhabitants of Ierousalem, and they shall look to me because they have danced triumphantly, and they shall mourn for him with a mourning as for a loved one, and they shall be pained with pain as for a firstborn. — **Zechariah 12:10 LXX, NETS**
---

### **Cross-witness — John 19:34-37, 1599 Geneva:**

> But one of the soldiers with a spear pierced his side, and forthwith came there out blood and water... For these things were done, that the Scripture should be fulfilled... And again another Scripture saith, They shall see him whom they have thrust through. — **John 19:34-37, 1599 Geneva**
---

### **Revelation 1:7, 1599 Geneva:**

> Behold, he cometh with clouds, and every eye shall see him: yea, even they which pierced him through: and all kindreds of the earth shall wail before him, Even so, Amen. — **Revelation 1:7, 1599 Geneva**
---

### Observation of great weight

**Two NT witnesses** (John 19 and Rev 1) bind **Zechariah 12:10** directly to Jesus’ piercing on the cross and to His future return. This is one of the most important prophetic texts in our study because **it appears at both ends — fulfilled at the crucifixion AND to be fulfilled at His return.**

**A critical note on the Greek:** The LXX of Zech 12:10 reads **ἐπιβλέψονται πρός με** — *"they shall look upon Me"* — and **κόψονται ἐπ’ αὐτόν** — *"they shall mourn for Him."* The shift from *"Me"* to *"Him"* within the same verse is striking. The LORD speaks (*"they shall look upon Me"*) — yet they mourn for *Him* (a third person). This is the kind of Father-Son interplay we have been noting throughout: the LORD’s piercing is the piercing of His Sent One, and the mourning is for that Sent One.

## **Part 2 — Jesus' Self-Identifications: the "I AM" Sayings, the Son of Man, the Son of God, and the Titles He Took**

# **PART 2 — JESUS' SELF-IDENTIFICATIONS**

We must take this seriously and carefully, for here we hear from the Person Himself.

## **2.1 — The "I AM" Sayings of John**

Jesus uses **ἐγώ εἰμι** (*I am*) repeatedly in John's Gospel, sometimes with a predicate, sometimes absolutely.

### **With a predicate** (revealing His functions):

- **John 6:35:** *ἐγώ εἰμι ὁ ἄρτος τῆς ζωῆς* *(I am the bread of life)*
- **John 8:12:** *ἐγώ εἰμι τὸ φῶς τοῦ κόσμου* *(I am the light of the world)*
- **John 10:7, 9:** *ἐγώ εἰμι ἡ θύρα* *(I am the door)*
- **John 10:11, 14:** *ἐγώ εἰμι ὁ ποιμὴν ὁ καλός* *(I am the good shepherd)*
- **John 11:25:** *ἐγώ εἰμι ἡ ἀνάστασις καὶ ἡ ζωή* *(I am the resurrection and the life)*
- **John 14:6:** *ἐγώ εἰμι ἡ ὁδὸς καὶ ἡ ἀλήθεια καὶ ἡ ζωή* *(I am the way, the truth, and the life)*
- **John 15:1, 5:** *ἐγώ εἰμι ἡ ἄμπελος ἡ ἀληθινή* *(I am the true vine)*

### **The predicate sayings, 1599 Geneva:**

> And Jesus said unto them, I am that bread of life: he that cometh to me, shall not hunger, and he that believeth in me, shall never thirst. — **John 6:35, 1599 Geneva**

> Then spake Jesus again unto them, saying, I am that light of the world: he that followeth me, shall not walke in darkenes, but shall have that light of life. — **John 8:12, 1599 Geneva**

> Then said Jesus unto them again, Verily, verily I say unto you, I am that door of the sheep. — **John 10:7, 1599 Geneva**

> I am that door: by me if any man enter in, he shall be saved, and shall go in, and go out, and find pasture. — **John 10:9, 1599 Geneva**

> I am that good shepherd: that good shepherd giveth his life for his sheep. — **John 10:11, 1599 Geneva**

> I am that good shepherd, and knowe mine, and am knowen of mine. — **John 10:14, 1599 Geneva**

> Jesus said unto her, I am the resurrection and the life: he that believeth in me, though he were dead, yet shall he live. — **John 11:25, 1599 Geneva**

> Jesus said unto him, I am that Way, and that Truth, and that Life. No man cometh unto the Father, but by me. — **John 14:6, 1599 Geneva**

> I am that true vine, and my Father is that husband man *(the vine-dresser)*. — **John 15:1, 1599 Geneva**

> I am that vine: ye are the branches: he that abideth in me, and I in him, the same bringeth forth much fruite: for without me can ye do nothing. — **John 15:5, 1599 Geneva**

### **Absolute "I AM" (without predicate):**

### **John 8:24, 1599 Geneva:**

> I said therefore unto you, That ye shall die in your sins: for except ye believe, that I am he, ye shall die in your sins. — **John 8:24, 1599 Geneva**

### **John 8:58** (already cited): *"Before Abraham was, I am."*

### **John 13:19, 1599 Geneva:**

> From henceforth tell I you before it come, that when it is come to pass, ye might believe that I am he. — **John 13:19, 1599 Geneva**

### **John 18:5-6, 1599 Geneva:**

> … Jesus said unto them, I am he. ... As soon then as he had said unto them, I am he, they went away backwards, and fell to the ground. — **John 18:5-6, 1599 Geneva**

---

### Observation

The absolute **ἐγώ εἰμι** sayings echo the LXX of Isaiah 41:4, 43:10, 13, 25, 46:4, 48:12 — where the LORD repeatedly says **ἐγώ εἰμι**. Jesus' use of this formulation, especially with the response of the Jews wanting to stone Him for blasphemy (John 8:59), indicates He is taking up that voice.

**Honest assessment:** The absolute **ἐγώ εἰμι** sayings are *strong* witnesses to Jesus' divine self-disclosure — bearing the Name and voice of the LORD. They are not, however, declarations *"I am the LORD"* or *"I am the Father."* They are claims to bear the LORD's self-identifying speech — consistent with His being the One in whom **the Name dwells** (cf. Ex 23:21; John 17:11).

---

## **2.2 — Son of Man**

This is Jesus' **most frequent self-designation** — used over 80 times in the Gospels. Its background is **Daniel 7:13-14**.

### **Daniel 7:13-14, NETS (Old Greek):**

> I was watching in the night visions, and lo, as it were a son of man was coming upon the clouds of heaven. And he came as far as the ancient of days, and the attendants were present with him. And royal authority was given to him, and all the nations of the earth according to posterity, and all honor was serving him. And his authority is an everlasting authority, which shall never be removed— and his kingship, which will never perish. — **Daniel 7:13-14, NETS (Old Greek)**

*NETS’s Daniel carries Old Greek and Theodotion in parallel columns; the draft cites the LXX voice. Theodotion reads “…coming with the clouds of heaven… And he came as far as the ancient of days and was presented to him. And to him was given the dominion and the honor and the kingship…” and the kingdom “will not be destroyed” *(Theodotion, the parallel column)*. The draft’s LXX Greek also carries καὶ ὡς παλαιὸς ἡμερῶν παρῆν — “and, as Ancient of days, he was present” — which the Old Greek print renders “And he came as far as the ancient of days.”*

### **Cross-witnesses — Jesus' direct citations:**

### **Matthew 26:63-64, 1599 Geneva — Jesus’ confession before the high priest:**

> … the chief Priest answered, and said to him, I charge thee swear unto us by the living God, to tell us, If thou be that Christ the Son of God, or no. ... Jesus said to him, Thou hast said it: nevertheless I say unto you, hereafter shall ye see the Son of man, sitting at the right hand of the power of God, and come in the clouds of the heaven. — **Matthew 26:63-64, 1599 Geneva**

---

### Observation

**Jesus' confession before the high priest combines:**
- **Psalm 110:1** (*"sitting at the right hand of power"*)
- **Daniel 7:13** (*"coming on the clouds of heaven"*)

This is **Jesus' own claim of identity**: He is the **Son of Man** of Daniel 7, who will be enthroned and given universal dominion by the **Ancient of Days** (the Father). His title **Son of Man** is therefore not merely a humble human designation — it is a claim to the **prophesied Heavenly Figure** who will receive eternal dominion from the Father.

This is also the **second witness alongside Psalm 110** for the Son's enthronement at the Father's right hand.

**An important note for our study:** In Daniel 7, the **Son of Man** is *brought before* the **Ancient of Days** — He is *not* the Ancient of Days. He is a *distinct figure* who *receives* dominion *from* the Ancient of Days. This perfectly preserves our foundation: the Father is the One God on the throne; the Son receives all authority from Him.

---

## **2.3 — Son of God / The Son**

Jesus often refers to Himself as **the Son** in relation to **the Father**. Key examples:

### **Matthew 11:27, 1599 Geneva:**

> All things are given unto me of my Father: and no man knoweth the Son, but ye Father: neither knoweth any man ye Father, but the Son, and he to whom ye Son will reueile him. — **Matthew 11:27, 1599 Geneva**

### **Mark 13:32, 1599 Geneva:**

> But of that day and hour knoweth no man, no, not the Angels which are in heaven, neither the Son himself, but the Father. — **Mark 13:32, 1599 Geneva**

---

### Observation of considerable importance for our study

**Mark 13:32 is a critical text.** Jesus explicitly says:
- The **Son does not know** the day or hour.
- The **Father alone** knows.

This is Jesus' **own testimony** that the Father has knowledge the Son does not have. **This is consistent with our entire foundation:** the Father is the One supreme God, source of all things; the Son is the Sent One who operates in dependence and submission. He does not know what the Father has kept in His own authority (cf. Acts 1:7).

This text alone is decisive against any reading that flattens the Son into being "of one substance" with the Father in such a way that He has all the Father's knowledge. **Per maxim #1, let God be true** — and Jesus Himself says **the Son does not know what the Father knows**.

---

## **2.4 — Other significant titles taken by Jesus**

- **Bridegroom** — Matt 9:15; John 3:29
- **Greater than Solomon, greater than Jonah, greater than the temple** — Matt 12:6, 41, 42
- **Lord of the Sabbath** — Mark 2:28
- **The Christ** — confirmed before the high priest (Matt 26:63-64)
- **The King of the Jews** — confirmed before Pilate (Matt 27:11)

---

## **Part 3 — Acts Revealing His Authority**

## **3.1 — Authority to Forgive Sins**

### **Mark 2:5-12, 1599 Geneva:**

> … Now when Jesus saw their faith, he said to the sick of the palsy, Son, thy sins are forgiven thee. ... And there were certain of the Scribes sitting there, and reasoning in their hearts, Why doth this man speak such blasphemies? who can forgive sins, but God only? ... But that ye may know, that the Son of man hath authority in earth to forgive sins … — **Mark 2:5-12, 1599 Geneva**

---

### Observation

The scribes correctly perceive that **only God forgives sins.** Jesus does not deny their premise — He demonstrates that **He has been given authority on earth** (**ἐξουσίαν ἔχει**) to do so. The authority is given to **the Son of Man on earth** — i.e., delegated by the Father. This is consistent with our foundation: the Father is the ultimate source; the Son exercises the Father's authority by appointment.

---

## **3.2 — Authority Over Nature**

### **Mark 4:39-41, 1599 Geneva:**

> … And he rose up, and rebuked the wind, and said unto the sea, Peace, and be still. So the wind ceased, and it was a great calm. ... And they feared exceedingly, and said one to another, Who is this, that both the wind and sea obey him? — **Mark 4:39-41, 1599 Geneva**

---

### Observation

The disciples ask the right question: **"Who is this?"** Only the LORD calms the sea (Ps 107:28-29). Jesus' command over wind and sea is a divine prerogative — exercised in person on earth.

---

## **3.3 — Authority to Raise the Dead**

Jesus raises three persons during His ministry:
- **Jairus' daughter** (Mark 5:35-43)
- **The widow's son at Nain** (Luke 7:11-17)
- **Lazarus** (John 11)

### **John 11:25-26, 43-44, 1599 Geneva:**

> Jesus said unto her, I am the resurrection and the life: he that believeth in me, though he were dead, yet shall he live. And whosoever liveth, and believeth in me, shall never die ... As he had spoken these things, he cried with a loud voice, Lazarus, come forth. Then he that was dead, came forth … — **John 11:25-26, 43-44, 1599 Geneva**

---

### Observation

Jesus identifies Himself as **the Resurrection and the Life** — and demonstrates this immediately by raising Lazarus. The act validates the claim. **This is a divine prerogative** — only the LORD gives life (Deut 32:39; 1 Sam 2:6). And yet, **Jesus says elsewhere** (John 5:26): *"For as the Father hath life in himself, so likewise hath he given to the Son to have life in himself."* The Son has been **given** by the Father to have life in Himself. The pattern holds: derivative, delegated, but truly possessed.

---

## **3.4 — Authority to Judge**

### **John 5:22-23, 26-27, 1599 Geneva:**

> For the Father judgeth no man, but hath committed all judgment unto the Son, Because that all men should honor the Son, as they honor the Father ... For as the Father hath life in himself, so likewise hath he given to the Son to have life in himself, And hath given him power also to execute judgment, in that he is the Son of man. — **John 5:22-23, 26-27, 1599 Geneva**

---

### Observation of foundational importance

This is Jesus' own clear statement of the Father-Son order:
- **The Father has committed all judgment to the Son** (**δέδωκεν**).
- The Father **has given the Son** to have life in Himself.
- The Father has **given** Him authority to judge.

**Three times the Father is the Giver; the Son is the Receiver.** This is the consistent biblical pattern, and Jesus Himself articulates it.

But notice also: **"that all men should honor the Son as they honor the Father."** The Son is to receive **equal honor** to the Father — *because* the Father has so willed it. The honor given to the Son flows to the Father (Phil 2:11 — *"to the glory of God the Father"*).

---

## **3.5 — Authority to Give the Spirit**

### **John 7:37-39, 1599 Geneva:**

> … If any man thirst, let him come unto me, and drink. He that believeth in me, as saith the Scripture, out of his belly shall flow rivers of water of life. ... (This spake he of the Spirit which they that believed in him, should receive … — **John 7:37-39, 1599 Geneva**

### **Acts 2:33, 1599 Geneva:**

> Since then that he by the right hand of God hath been exalted, and hath received of his Father the promise of the holy Ghost, he hath shed forth this which ye now see and hear. — **Acts 2:33, 1599 Geneva**

---

### Observation

Peter's Pentecost sermon explicitly states that Jesus **received** the promise of the Holy Spirit **from the Father** and then **poured it forth.** The Son is both Recipient (from the Father) and Giver (to His people). The pattern is unbroken: the Father is the Source; the Son is the channel.

---

This is critical for our study. Let us gather Jesus' **own** statements about His relation to the Father.

### **John 5:19, 30:**

> Then answered Jesus, and said unto them, Verily, verily I say unto you, The Son can do nothing of himself, save that he seeth the Father do: for whatsoever things he doth, the same things doeth the Son in like manner. — **John 5:19, 1599 Geneva**

> I can do nothing of mine own self: as I hear, I judge: and my judgment is just, because I seek not mine own will, but the will of the Father who hath sent me. — **John 5:30, 1599 Geneva**

### **John 6:38:**

> For I came down from heaven, not to do mine own will, but his will which hath sent me. — **John 6:38, 1599 Geneva**

### **John 8:28-29:**

> Then said Jesus unto them, When ye have lift up the Son of man, then shall ye know that I am he, and that I do nothing of myself, but as my Father hath taught me, so I speak these things. For he that sent me, is with me: the Father hath not left me alone, because I do always those things that please him. — **John 8:28-29, 1599 Geneva**

### **John 14:28:**

> Ye have heard how I said unto you, I go away, and will come unto you. If ye loved me, ye would verily rejoice, because I said, I go unto the Father: for the Father is greater then *(the Geneva prints "then" for our modern "than")* I. — **John 14:28, 1599 Geneva**

### **John 17:3** (already cited):

> And this is life eternal, that they know thee to be the only very God *(the author's rendering here reads "the only true God")*, and whom thou hast sent, Jesus Christ. — **John 17:3, 1599 Geneva**

---

### Observation — A definitive pattern from Jesus' own lips:

1. The Son can do nothing of Himself.

2. The Son does not seek His own will.

3. The Son speaks what the Father teaches Him.

4. The Father sent the Son; the Son does what pleases the Father.

5. **The Father is greater than the Son.**

6. The Father alone is the only true God; the Son is the One the Father sent.

**These are Jesus' direct words.** Per maxim #1 — *let God be true* — these establish, **from the lips of the Christ Himself**, that:

- The Son is **subordinate** to the Father in action, will, knowledge, and being.

- The Father is **greater than** the Son.

- The Father is **the only true God**; the Son is the **Sent One**.

This is the *Lord's own self-testimony* — and it harmonizes perfectly with everything we have established from the OT foundation.

---

## **Part 5 — Post-Resurrection Acts; the Grand Synthesis of His Ministry; the Direction Set: (A)**

# **PART 5 — POST-RESURRECTION ACTS**

## **5.1 — Authentication of the Resurrection**

The risen Jesus:
- Appears to Mary Magdalene (John 20:11-18).
- Appears to the disciples and shows His wounds (Luke 24:36-43; John 20:19-29).
- Eats with His disciples (Luke 24:42-43; Acts 10:41) — proving He is bodily, not a phantom.
- Appears to 500 brethren at once (1 Cor 15:6).
- Opens their understanding of the Scriptures (Luke 24:25-27, 44-47).

---

## **5.2 — The Great Commission**

### **Matthew 28:18-20:**

> ... All power is given unto me, in heaven, and in earth. Go therefore, and teach all nations, baptizing them in the Name of the Father, and the Son, and the holy Ghost, Teaching them to observe all things, whatsoever I have commanded you: and lo, I am with you alway, until the end of the world, Amen. — **Matthew 28:18-20, 1599 Geneva**

---

### Observation

**(1)** **Ἐδόθη μοι πᾶσα ἐξουσία** — *"All authority **has been given** to Me."* Even after resurrection, the Son receives His authority **from** the Father. The passive verb is decisive.

**(2)** The baptismal formula names **the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit** — though notice carefully that the **Name** (singular: **εἰς τὸ ὄνομα**) is one Name, not three. This text deserves more careful examination later in our study, but at minimum it establishes that the Father, the Son, and the Spirit are jointly involved in the disciple-making mission — though Jesus does *not* here teach a metaphysical doctrine of "three persons in one substance." He commands a baptism in a singular Name shared by the three.

We should note, however, that the historical practice of the apostles in Acts is to baptize **"in the Name of Jesus Christ"** (Acts 2:38; 8:16; 10:48; 19:5). This warrants careful examination at the appropriate place — perhaps when we treat the Apocalypse's view of "the Name."

---

## **5.3 — Ascension and Promise of Return**

### **Acts 1:9-11:**

> ... And when he had spoken these things, while they beheld, he was taken up: for a cloud took him up out of their sight. ... Which also said, Ye men of Galilee, why stand ye gazing into heaven? This Jesus which is taken up from you into heaven, shall so come, as ye have seen him go into heaven. — **Acts 1:9-11, 1599 Geneva**

---

### Observation

The ascension fulfills the *"sit at My right hand"* of Psalm 110:1 (per Acts 2:34-35). The angelic promise — *"shall so come as ye have seen Him go"* — establishes the framework for our later examination of His return.

---

# **GRAND SYNTHESIS — What His Ministry Establishes**

Through the acts and words of His ministry, the following are established by the most extensive direct evidence:

### **About His Person:**

1. **He is the prophesied Servant of the LORD** — by His own citation of Isaiah 61 (Luke 4:17-21).
2. **He is the Son of Man of Daniel 7** — by His own claim before the high priest (Matt 26:64).
3. **He is the Christ, the Son of God** — by His own confession (Matt 26:64; John 4:25-26).
4. **He bears the LORD's voice** — the **ἐγώ εἰμι** sayings.
5. **He is greater than Solomon, Jonah, and the temple** — by His own testimony.
6. **He is the embodiment of the LORD's prophesied works** — His miracles fulfill Isaiah 35, 53, 61.

### **About His Authority:**

7. **He forgives sins** — exercised on earth as the Son of Man.
8. **He commands nature.**
9. **He raises the dead.**
10. **He has been given all judgment.**
11. **He gives the Holy Spirit** — having received it from the Father.
12. **He has been given all authority** — explicitly **given**, not innately possessed apart from the Father.

### **About His Relation to the Father (His Own Words):**

13. **He can do nothing of Himself** — only what He sees the Father doing.
14. **He does not seek His own will** — but the Father's.
15. **He speaks what the Father has taught Him.**
16. **The Father is greater than Him.**
17. **The Father alone is the only true God; the Son is the Sent One.**
18. **The Son does not know what the Father knows** (the day and hour).

### **About His Mission:**

19. **He came to fulfill, not destroy** the Law and Prophets (Matt 5:17).
20. **He came not to be served, but to serve, and to give His life a ransom for many** (Mark 10:45 — echoing Isa 53).
21. **He came to seek and save the lost** (Luke 19:10).
22. **He came that they might have life, and have it abundantly** (John 10:10).

### **Post-Resurrection:**

23. **He is risen bodily** — bearing His pierced hands and feet (fulfilling Ps 22:16).
24. **He receives all authority from the Father** after resurrection.
25. **He commissions worldwide proclamation.**
26. **He ascends and is enthroned** at the Father's right hand (fulfilling Ps 110:1).
27. **He will return in like manner** as He ascended.

---

## **Where We Stand**

We now have a remarkably comprehensive picture of the Person of Christ as revealed through:

- **The prophecies of the Old Testament** (foundation, posits, suffering, vindication).
- **The acts and words of His earthly ministry** (this examination).
- **The apostolic preaching of His resurrection and exaltation.**

What remains, per your direction:

**(A) The Son's present session at the Father's right hand** — what He is doing now (Heb 7-10; intercession; high priesthood).

**(B) The Son's coming return** — Dan 7:13-14 fully expounded; Zech 12-14; Matt 24-25; the Apocalypse.

**(C) The Son's final relation to the Father in the consummation** — 1 Cor 15:24-28.

**(D) The Son's titles and self-identifications in the Apocalypse** — the I AM sayings of Revelation, the Lamb on the Throne, Alpha and Omega.

Shall we now proceed to **(A)** — the Son's present session and high-priestly ministry — beginning with the careful exposition of Hebrews 7-10, building upon the foundation we laid with Psalm 110?

> A.

We proceed to **the Son's present session and high-priestly ministry** — what He is doing *now* at the Father's right hand.

This is one of the most theologically dense sections of the entire NT. Hebrews 7-10 builds entirely upon **Psalm 110:4** (LXX 109:4): *"Thou art a Priest forever after the order of Melchizedek"* — which we have already examined under our maxims and established as a direct apostolic citation applied to Jesus.

Per our refined modus operandi, I will:
1. Examine each significant passage in Hebrews 7-10.
2. Cross-reference all relevant OT and NT witnesses.
3. Note carefully what is strictly established and what is suggested.
4. Maintain the foundation we have built throughout — the Father as One God, the Son as the Sent and Appointed One.

Let us proceed.

## **The Son's Present Session — the Melchizedek Foundation, Hebrews 7-10, and the Grand Synthesis; the Direction Set: (B)**

# **THE FOUNDATION: Melchizedek (Genesis 14) and Psalm 110:4**

Before entering Hebrews 7, we must briefly examine the OT roots.

## **Genesis 14:17-20 (LXX) — NETS:**

*"Now after he had returned from the slaughter of Chodollogomor and the kings who were with him, the king of Sodoma *(Sodom)* went out to meet him at the valley of Saue (this was the plain of a king). Melchisedek *(Melchizedek)* king of Salem brought out bread loaves and wine; now he was priest of God Most High. And he blessed Abram and said, “Blessed be Abram to God Most High, who created the sky and the earth, and blessed be God Most High, who has delivered up your enemies as subjects to you!” And he gave him one tenth of everything."*

---

### Observations on Melchizedek

**(1)** Melchizedek is **βασιλεὺς Σαλὴμ** (*King of Salem*) and **ἱερεὺς τοῦ Θεοῦ τοῦ ὑψίστου** (*Priest of God Most High*) — both **King** *and* **Priest** in one person.

**(2)** He appears suddenly in the narrative without genealogy, predecessor, or successor.

**(3)** Abraham — the patriarch of Israel — **tithes to him** and **receives his blessing.**

**(4)** Salem = Jerusalem (cf. Ps 76:2).

---

## **Psalm 110:4 LXX (109:4) — NETS:**

*"The Lord swore and will not change his mind, "You are a priest forever according to the order of Melchisedek *(Melchizedek)*.""*

### **1599 Geneva:**

---

# **HEBREWS 7 — THE MELCHIZEDEK PRIESTHOOD**

## **Hebrews 7:1-3 — Geneva:**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"For this Melchi-sedec *(Melchizedek)* was King of Salem, the Priest of the most high God, who met Abraham, as he returned from the slaughter of the Kings, and blessed him: To whom also Abraham gave the tithe of all things: who first is by interpretation King of righteousnes: after that, he is also King of Salem, that is, King of peace, Without father, without mother, without kinred, and hath neither beginning of his days, neither ende of life: but is likened unto the Son of God, and continueth a Priest for ever."*

---

### Observations of significant weight

**(1)** **βασιλεὺς δικαιοσύνης / βασιλεὺς εἰρήνης** — *"King of Righteousness / King of Peace."* These titles, drawn from the Hebrew etymology, foreshadow the dual prophecies of Christ as the Righteous One who brings peace.

**(2)** **ἀπάτωρ, ἀμήτωρ, ἀγενεαλόγητος** — *"without father, without mother, without genealogy."* The author of Hebrews uses Melchizedek's *narrative silence* about his lineage as a typological device. Melchizedek appears and disappears without genealogical context — making him a fitting *type* of an eternal Priest.

**(3)** **ἀφωμοιωμένος δὲ τῷ Υἱῷ τοῦ Θεοῦ** — *"having been made like unto the Son of God."* This is critical. **Melchizedek is made like the Son** — not the reverse. **The Son is the original; Melchizedek is the type.** This confirms the Son's pre-existence and priority — He is the reality of which Melchizedek is the foreshadow.

**(4)** **μένει ἱερεὺς εἰς τὸ διηνεκές** — *"He continues a Priest perpetually."* Of Melchizedek as narrative figure (and ultimately of the Son whom he prefigures).

---

## **Hebrews 7:11-17 — The Necessity of a New Priesthood**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"If therefore perfection had been by the Priesthood of the Levites (for under it the Law was established to the people) what needed it furthermore, that another Priest should rise after the order of Melchi-sedec, and not to be called after the order of Aaron? For if the Priesthood be changed, then of necessitie must there be a change of the Law. For he of whom these things are spoken, perteineth unto another tribe, whereof no man serued at the altar. For it is euident, that our Lord sprung out of Iuda, concerning the which tribe Moses spake nothing, touching the Priesthood. And it is yet a more euident thing, because that after the similitude of Melchi-sedec, there is risen up another Priest, Which is not made Priest after the Law of the carnal commandment, but after the power of the endlesse life. For he testifieth thus, Thou art a Priest for ever, after the order of Melchi-sedec."*

---

### Observations

**(1)** **The Levitical priesthood could not bring perfection** — so a different priesthood was needed.

**(2)** **Jesus is from Judah, not Levi** — He cannot be a priest after the Aaronic order. He must be a priest of a different order.

**(3)** **κατὰ δύναμιν ζωῆς ἀκαταλύτου** — *"according to the power of an indestructible life."* The new Priest holds priesthood **by the power of a life that cannot be destroyed** — that is, **the resurrected life of the Son**. This is the key: His resurrection qualifies Him for this eternal priesthood.

**(4) Cross-witness on Jesus' tribe — Revelation 5:5:**

**1599 Geneva:** *"...the Lion which is of the tribe of Judah..."*

---

## **Hebrews 7:18-25 — Superior to the Old Priesthood**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"For the commandment that went afore, is disannulled *(annulled)*, because of the weakness thereof, and unprofitableness. For the Law made nothing perfect, but the bringing in of a better hope made perfect, whereby we drawe neere unto God. And for as much as it is not without an othe (for these are made Priests without an othe: But this is made with an othe by him that said unto him, The Lord hath sworne, and will not repent, Thou art a Priest for ever, after the order of Melchi-sedec) By so much is Jesus made a surety of a better Testament. And among them many were made Priests, because they were not suffered to endure, by the reason of death. But this man, because he endureth ever, hath a Priesthood, which cannot pass from one to another. Wherefore, he is able also perfectly to save them that come unto God by him, seeing he ever liveth, to make intercession for them."*

---

### Observations of supreme importance

**(1)** **δι᾽ ἧς ἐγγίζομεν τῷ Θεῷ** — *"by which we draw near unto God."* The purpose of the new priesthood is **access to God [the Father].** The Son's priestly ministry is **for our access to the Father**.

**(2)** **The Father swore an oath** concerning Jesus' priesthood (citing Ps 110:4). The Father is the One swearing; the Son is the One sworn-to. Pattern preserved.

**(3)** **κρείττονος διαθήκης γέγονεν ἔγγυος Ἰησοῦς** — *"Jesus has become the guarantee of a better covenant."* He is the *guarantor* — He stands between the parties as security.

**(4)** **σῴζειν εἰς τὸ παντελὲς δύναται τοὺς προσερχομένους δι᾽ αὐτοῦ τῷ Θεῷ** — *"He is able to save completely those who come to God through Him."*

Observe the critical preposition: **δι᾽ αὐτοῦ τῷ Θεῷ** — *"through Him, to God [the Father]."* We come **to God** (the Father) **through Him** (the Son). The pattern of 1 Cor 8:6 is *exactly* preserved: the Father is the destination (**εἰς αὐτόν**); the Son is the way (**δι᾽ αὐτοῦ**).

**(5)** **πάντοτε ζῶν εἰς τὸ ἐντυγχάνειν ὑπὲρ αὐτῶν** — *"always living to make intercession for them."*

**This is the Son's present, ongoing ministry.** Right now, at this moment, the Son is **always living** at the Father's right hand, **always interceding** for those who come to the Father through Him. This is **what He is doing now.**

### Cross-witness:

**Romans 8:34:**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"Who shall condemne? it is Christ which is dead, yea, or rather, which is risen again, who is also at the right hand of God, and maketh request also for us."*

**Two witnesses (Heb 7:25 and Rom 8:34) establish the present intercessory ministry of the Son at the Father's right hand.**

---

## **Hebrews 7:26-28 — The Perfection of the Son as Priest**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"For such an high Priest it became us to have, which is holy, harmless, undefiled, separate from sinners, and made higher then *(for "than")* the heavens: Which needeth not daily as those high Priests to offer up sacrifice, first for his own sins, and then for the peoples: for that did he once, when he offered up himself. For the Law maketh men high Priests, which have infirmitie: but the word of the othe that was since the Law, maketh the Son, who is consecrated *(brought to His full appointed state)* forevermore. Hebrews < 7 >"*

---

### Observations

**(1)** The Son is **holy, harmless, undefiled, separated from sinners** — fulfilling Isa 53:9 (*"He had done no violence, neither was any deceit in His mouth"*).

**(2)** **τοῦτο γὰρ ἐποίησεν ἐφάπαξ** — *"this He did once for all."* The Son's sacrifice of Himself is **once for all** — never to be repeated. This will become a key theme of chapter 10.

**(3)** **Υἱὸν εἰς τὸν αἰῶνα τετελειωμένον** — *"the Son, perfected forever."* The Son has been **perfected** (**τετελειωμένον** — completed, brought to His full appointed state) **forever.** This is His present condition.

---

# **HEBREWS 8 — THE BETTER COVENANT**

## **Hebrews 8:1-2 — Geneva:**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"Now of the things which we have spoken, this is the sum, that we have such an high Priest, that sitteth at the right hand of the throne of the Majesty in heavens, And is a minister of the Sanctuary, and of that true Tabernacle which the Lord pight, and not man."*

---

### Observations

**(1)** **ἐκάθισεν ἐν δεξιᾷ τοῦ θρόνου τῆς μεγαλωσύνης** — *"He sat down at the right hand of the throne of the Majesty"* — fulfilling Psalm 110:1.

Notice: the Son sat **at the right hand of the throne** — not *on* the throne of the Majesty. The Father's throne is central; the Son is at His right hand. This preserves the foundation.

**(2)** **τῶν ἁγίων λειτουργὸς καὶ τῆς σκηνῆς τῆς ἀληθινῆς** — *"a minister of the holy places and of the true Tabernacle."* The earthly tabernacle was a copy; the heavenly one is the **true** Tabernacle, pitched by **the Lord [the Father], not man.**

---

## **Hebrews 8:6-13 — The New Covenant Prophesied**

The author then cites **Jeremiah 31:31-34 LXX (Jeremiah 38:31-34 LXX)** in full — one of the longest OT citations in the NT.

### **Jeremiah 38:31-34 (LXX = MT 31:31-34) — NETS:**

*"Behold, days are coming, quoth *(says)* the Lord, and I will make a new covenant with the house of Israel and the house of Iouda *(Judah)*. It will not be like the covenant that I made with their fathers in the day when I took them by their hand to bring them out of the land of Egypt, because they did not abide in my covenant, and I was unconcerned for them, quoth the Lord, because this is the covenant that I will make with the house of Israel after those days, quoth the Lord. Giving I will give my laws in their mind, and I will write them on their hearts, and I will become a god to them, and they shall become a people to me. And they shall not teach, each his fellow citizen and each his brother, saying, “Know the Lord,” because they shall all know me, from their small even to their great, because I will be gracious regarding their injustices, and remember their sins no more."*

### **1599 Geneva (Jeremiah 31:31-34, MT numbering) — a second recourse:**
*"Behold, the days come, saith the Lord, that I will make a new covenant with the house of Israel, and with the house of Judah, Not according to the covenant that I made with their fathers, when I tooke them by the hand to bring them out of the land of Egypt, the which my covenant they brake, although I was an husband unto them, saith the Lord. But this shall be the covenant that I will make with the house of Israel, After those days, saith the Lord, I will put my Law in their inward partes, and write it in their hearts, and will be their God, and they shall be my people. And they shall teach no more every man his neighbour and every man his brother, saying, Know the Lord: for they shall all know me from the least of them unto the greatest of them, saith the Lord: for I will forgive their iniquitie, and will remember their sins no more."*

---

### Observation

**The New Covenant** has these features:
- **Internalized** law (in the mind and on the heart).
- **God is their God; they are His people** (covenant relation restored).
- **Forgiveness of sins** with no further remembrance.

**The Son's high-priestly ministry is the ministry of this New Covenant.** He is its **guarantor** (Heb 7:22) and its **mediator** (Heb 8:6, 9:15, 12:24).

---

# **HEBREWS 9 — THE ONCE-FOR-ALL SACRIFICE**

## **Hebrews 9:11-14 — Geneva:**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"But Christ being come an high Priest of good things to come, by a greater and a more perfect Tabernacle, not made with hands, that is, not of this building, Neither by the blood of goates and calues: but by his own blood entered he in once unto the holy place, and obtained eternal redemption for us. For if the blood of bulles and of goates, and the ashes of an heifer, sprinkling them that are vncleane, sanctifieth as touching the purifying of the flesh, How much more shall the blood of Christ which through the eternal Spirit offered himself without fault to God, purge your conscience from dead workes, to serue the living God?"*

---

### Observations of supreme weight

**(1)** **εἰσῆλθεν ἐφάπαξ εἰς τὰ ἅγια** — *"He entered once for all into the holy places."* The Son **entered** the heavenly Tabernacle **once for all** — a completed action, not repeated.

**(2)** **διὰ δὲ τοῦ ἰδίου αἵματος** — *"by His own blood."* Not the blood of animals; **His own blood**.

**(3)** **αἰωνίαν λύτρωσιν εὑράμενος** — *"having found / obtained eternal redemption."* Already obtained — completed.

**(4)** **ἑαυτὸν προσήνεγκεν ἄμωμον τῷ Θεῷ** — *"He offered Himself without blemish to God [the Father]."* The Son is both the **Offerer** and the **Offering** — *and* He offers Himself **to the Father.** The Father is the Recipient of the offering. Pattern preserved.

**(5)** **εἰς τὸ λατρεύειν Θεῷ ζῶντι** — *"to serve the living God."* The purpose: that we may serve **God** (the Father).

---

## **Hebrews 9:15 — Mediator of the New Covenant**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"And for this cause is he the Mediator of the new Testament, that through death which was for the redemption of the transgressions that were in the former Testament, they which were called, might receive the promise of eternal inheritance."*

---

### Cross-witness — 1 Timothy 2:5:

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"For there is one God, and one Mediator between God and man, which is the man Christ Jesus,"*

---

### Observation of foundational weight

**1 Timothy 2:5 is decisive for our entire study.** Paul declares:
- **εἷς Θεός** — *one God* (the Father, per our foundation).
- **εἷς μεσίτης** — *one Mediator* — between God and men.
- **ἄνθρωπος Χριστὸς Ἰησοῦς** — *the Man Christ Jesus*.

**The Mediator is the Man Christ Jesus.** Paul, even after His exaltation, calls Him **ἄνθρωπος** — *Man*. The mediation is **between God [the Father] and men** — and the Mediator stands between as **a Man**.

This is a critical text in our study because it preserves both:
- **The Father as the One God** (per the Shema and Isa 44-46).
- **The Son as the exalted Man** who mediates between God and men.

The Son's **mediation** at the Father's right hand is **as Man** — bearing the humanity He assumed in incarnation forever, now glorified.

---

## **Hebrews 9:24-28 — Christ Appeared Before the Father**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"For Christ is not entered into the holy places that are made with hands, which are similitudes *(copies)* of the true Sanctuary: but is entered into very heaven, to appeare now in ye sight of God for us, Not that he should offer himself often, as the high Priest entered into the Holy place every year with other blood, (For then must he have often suffred since the foundation of the world) but now in the end of the world hath he been made manifest, once to put away sin by the sacrifice of him self. And as it is appointed unto men that they shall once die, and after that cometh the judgment: So Christ was once offered to take away the sins of many, and unto them that looke for him, shall he appeare the second time without sin unto saluation. Hebrews < 9 >"*

---

### Observations of decisive importance for the Son's present ministry

**(1)** **εἰς αὐτὸν τὸν οὐρανόν** — *"into heaven itself."* The Son entered the **real heaven** — not a copy. His ministry is in heaven now.

**(2)** **νῦν ἐμφανισθῆναι τῷ προσώπῳ τοῦ Θεοῦ ὑπὲρ ἡμῶν** — *"NOW to appear before the face of God for us."*

This is the **single most concise statement of the Son's present ministry:**
- **νῦν** — *now* — at this very moment.
- **ἐμφανισθῆναι** — *to appear, to be manifest.*
- **τῷ προσώπῳ τοῦ Θεοῦ** — *before the face of God [the Father].*
- **ὑπὲρ ἡμῶν** — *on our behalf.*

**The Son is, right now, appearing before the Father's face on our behalf.** This is the present, ongoing reality of His high-priestly ministry. He stands as the Man Christ Jesus before the Father, presenting His finished sacrifice and interceding for those who come to the Father through Him.

**(3)** **ἅπαξ προσενεχθεὶς εἰς τὸ πολλῶν ἀνενεγκεῖν ἁμαρτίας** — *"having been offered once for all to bear the sins of many."* Direct echo of Isaiah 53:12. Once-for-all.

**(4)** **ἐκ δευτέρου χωρὶς ἁμαρτίας ὀφθήσεται** — *"He shall appear a second time, without sin."* His **second appearing** is foretold here — **without sin** (because sin was dealt with at the first appearing) — **εἰς σωτηρίαν** — *unto salvation* — for those who eagerly await Him.

This is the framework for our future treatment of His return.

---

# **HEBREWS 10 — THE FINISHED WORK**

## **Hebrews 10:1-4 — The Limits of the Old Sacrifices**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"For the Law hauing the shadowe of good things to come, and not the very image of the things, can never with those sacrifices, which they offer year by year continually, sanctifie the commers thereunto. For would they not then have ceased to have been offered, because that the offerers once purged, should have had no more conscience of sins? But in those sacrifices there is a remembrance again of sins every year. For it is impossible that the blood of bulles and goates should take away sins."*

---

## **Hebrews 10:5-10 — The Son's Body Prepared**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"Wherefore when he cometh into the world, he saith, Sacrifice and offering thou wouldest not: but a body hast thou ordeined me. In burnt offerings, and sin offerings thou hast had no pleasure. Then I said, Lo, I come (In the beginning of the booke it is written of me) that I should do thy will, O God."*

---

### Critical text — Psalm 40:6-8 LXX (Ps 39:7-9 LXX) cited

This is a direct citation of **Psalm 39:7-9 LXX**:

### **Psalm 39:7-9 (LXX = MT 40:6-8) — NETS:**
*"Sacrifice and offering you did not want, but ears you fashioned for me. Whole burnt offering and one for sin you did not request. Then I said, “Look, I have come; in a scroll of a book it is written of me. To do your will, O my God, I desired—and your law, within my belly.”"*

### **1599 Geneva (Psalm 40:6-8, MT) — a second recourse:**
*"Sacrifice and offering thou didest not desire: (for mine eares hast thou prepared) burnt offering and sin offering hast thou not required. Then said I, Lo, I come: for in the rolle of the booke it is written of me, I desired to do thy good will, O my God: yea, thy Law is within mine heart."*

---

### Observation of considerable importance

**Note the textual difference:**
- **LXX:** **σῶμα δὲ κατηρτίσω μοι** — *"a body You prepared for Me."*
- **Hebrew (followed by the 1599 Geneva):** *"mine ears hast thou prepared"* (or "digged/opened").

**The author of Hebrews uses the LXX reading** — **σῶμα δὲ κατηρτίσω μοι** — *"a body You prepared for Me."* Per our maxim #1 and our commitment to the LXX, this is the God-breathed reading the Apostle uses.

This text is striking: it is presented as **the Son speaking** as He **enters the world** (**εἰσερχόμενος εἰς τὸν κόσμον**). The Son, on the eve of His incarnation, says to the Father: *"You did not want sacrifices and offerings — but a body You prepared for Me. Behold, I come to do Your will, O God."*

**The Son speaks of His own coming into the world as His act of obedience to the Father's will.** The Father prepared the body; the Son took it; the Son's purpose was to do the Father's will. This is the highest expression of the Father-Source / Son-Sent pattern, applied to the Incarnation itself.

---

### **Hebrews 10:10:**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"By the which will we are sanctified, even by the offering of the body of Jesus Christ once made."*

---

### Observation

**ἐν ᾧ θελήματι** — *"by which will [of the Father]"* — **we have been sanctified.** Our sanctification is the *Father's* will, accomplished *through* the Son's body offered once for all.

---

## **Hebrews 10:11-14 — Seated Forever**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"And every Priest standeth daily ministering, and oft times offereth one manner of offering, which can never take away sins: But this man after he had offered one sacrifice for sins, sitteth for ever at the right hand of God, And from hencefoorth tarieth, till his enemies be made his footestoole. For with one offering hath he consecrated *(brought to full completion)* for ever them that are sanctified."*

---

### Observations of foundational weight

**(1)** **ἕστηκεν** — *"stands"* — the Levitical priests **stand** daily. **ἐκάθισεν** — *"sat down"* — the Son **sat down** after His one offering. The contrast is intentional: the seated Priest has finished His work; the standing priests have not.

**(2)** **εἰς τὸ διηνεκὲς ἐκάθισεν ἐν δεξιᾷ τοῦ Θεοῦ** — *"forever sat down at the right hand of God."* The Son is enthroned forever — **at the right hand of God [the Father]**. Psalm 110:1 is again confirmed.

**(3)** **τὸ λοιπὸν ἐκδεχόμενος ἕως τεθῶσιν οἱ ἐχθροὶ αὐτοῦ ὑποπόδιον τῶν ποδῶν αὐτοῦ** — *"henceforth waiting until His enemies be made His footstool."* Direct citation of Psalm 110:1.

The Son is **waiting** at the Father's right hand. This is His **present posture** — seated, interceding, waiting — **until the Father makes His enemies His footstool.** The Father is the One who acts to subdue the enemies; the Son waits.

This is a crucial detail for our study of His return: **the Father determines the timing**; the Son waits in expectation. This is consistent with Acts 1:7 (*"It is not for you to know the times or the seasons, which the Father hath put in his own power"*) and Mark 13:32 (*"neither knoweth the Son, but the Father"*).

**(4)** **μιᾷ γὰρ προσφορᾷ τετελείωκεν εἰς τὸ διηνεκὲς τοὺς ἁγιαζομένους** — *"by one offering He has perfected forever those who are being sanctified."* **One offering. Forever perfected.** The work is finished.

---

## **Hebrews 10:19-22 — Access Through His Blood**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"Seeing therefore, brethren, that by the blood of Jesus we may be bold to enter into the Holy place, By the new and living way, which he hath prepared for us, through the vaile, that is, his flesh: And seeing we have an high Priest, which is ouer the house of God, Let us drawe neere with a true heart in assurance of faith, our hearts being pure from an euill conscience,"*

---

### Observations

**(1)** **The veil torn at the crucifixion** (Matt 27:51) is identified with **the flesh of Jesus.** His flesh, torn for us, opens the way into the Holy of Holies — to the Father's presence.

**(2)** **παρρησίαν** — *"boldness, freedom of speech, confident access."* The believer has confident access **to the Father** through the Son's blood.

---

# **GRAND SYNTHESIS — The Son's Present Session**

Through Hebrews 7-10 and confirmed by Romans 8:34, 1 Timothy 2:5, and the Psalms, we now have a comprehensive picture of **what the Son is doing now**:

## **The Son's Present Ministry — Established by Multiple Witnesses**

| Activity | Description | Witnesses |
|---|---|---|
| **Seated at the Father's right hand** | His enthronement is complete; His sacrificial work is finished | Heb 10:12; Ps 110:1; Mark 16:19; Acts 2:33-34; Col 3:1 |
| **High Priest forever** | After the order of Melchizedek, by the Father's oath | Heb 7:17, 21, 25; Ps 110:4 |
| **Appearing before the Father's face for us** | NOW present in heaven on our behalf | Heb 9:24 |
| **Always living to make intercession** | Continuous, never-ceasing intercessory ministry | Heb 7:25; Rom 8:34 |
| **One Mediator between God and men** | As the exalted Man Christ Jesus | 1 Tim 2:5; Heb 9:15; 12:24 |
| **Waiting until His enemies are made His footstool** | The Father is the One subduing the enemies; the Son waits | Heb 10:13; Ps 110:1 |
| **Guarantor and Mediator of the New Covenant** | His blood is the basis of the New Covenant | Heb 7:22; 8:6; 9:15 |
| **Opening access to the Father** | Through His torn flesh / shed blood | Heb 10:19-22 |
| **Pouring out the Spirit** (initially at Pentecost; continuing) | Received from the Father, given to His people | Acts 2:33 |

---

## **What This Establishes About His Person — Carefully**

**(1)** **The Son is, *now*, a living Man** — exalted, glorified, but still **the Man Christ Jesus** (1 Tim 2:5). He did not shed His humanity at the resurrection; He took it with Him into heaven.

**(2)** **He bears the marks of His suffering even now.** The risen Christ appeared with pierced hands and feet and side (John 20:25-27); the Lamb in the Apocalypse appears **as having been slain** (Rev 5:6). The crucified Man is the enthroned Priest.

**(3)** **He acts always in dependence on and submission to the Father.** Even at the Father's right hand, He waits *for the Father to subdue His enemies*. His enthronement does not erase the order: the Father is the One supreme God; the Son rules **at His right hand** by His appointment.

**(4)** **He is the only Mediator** between the Father and humanity. No priest stands beside Him. No Mary, no saint, no apostle, no minister mediates between God and men — **only the Man Christ Jesus.** This is critical doctrine, and it is firmly established.

**(5)** **His ministry is *for us*.** Every act of His present session — His appearing, His interceding, His waiting, His opening the way — is **ὑπὲρ ἡμῶν** (*on our behalf*). The Son's enthronement is not a private glory; it is an active ministry for those who come to the Father through Him.

---

## **A Critical Observation Preserving Our Foundation**

Notice that throughout Hebrews 7-10, the Father remains **ὁ Θεός** — *the God* — to whom:
- The Son offered Himself (9:14: **τῷ Θεῷ**).
- The Son sat down at His right hand (10:12: **ἐν δεξιᾷ τοῦ Θεοῦ**).
- We come through the Son (7:25: **τῷ Θεῷ**).
- The Father's house is overseen by the Son (10:21: **ἐπὶ τὸν οἶκον τοῦ Θεοῦ**).
- The Father swore the oath establishing the Son's priesthood (7:21).
- The Father prepared the body for the Son's incarnation (10:5).
- The Father's will is what the Son came to do (10:7-9).
- The Father is the One who will make the Son's enemies His footstool (10:13).

**The foundation we established at the beginning — the Father as the One God — is preserved unbroken throughout the Son's high-priestly ministry.** The Son is never a rival to the Father, never an independent Deity, never "another God" — He is **the appointed and beloved Son**, **the eternal Priest by the Father's oath**, **the Mediator between God and men as the Man Christ Jesus**.

---

## **What Remains**

We have now completed (A). What remains, per your earlier direction:

**(B) The Son's coming return** — Daniel 7:13-14 fully expounded; Zechariah 12-14; Matthew 24-25; the Apocalypse's prophecies of His coming.

**(C) The Son's final relation to the Father in the consummation** — 1 Cor 15:24-28 (the Son delivering the Kingdom to the Father, "that God may be all in all"), which is perhaps the most theologically decisive passage on the Father-Son order at the end of all things.

**(D) The Son's titles and self-identifications in the Apocalypse** — the I AM sayings of Revelation, the Lamb on the Throne, Alpha and Omega designations, and how these relate to and complete our study.

Shall we now proceed to **(B)** — the Son's coming return — beginning with **Daniel 7:13-14 fully expounded** (since Jesus Himself cited this as the central image of His return before the high priest at Matt 26:64), and then moving through Zechariah 12-14 and the Olivet Discourse?

> Indeed.

We proceed to **(B) — the Son's coming return**.

Per our refined modus operandi, we will:
1. Begin with **Daniel 7** — the foundational vision Jesus cited as His own claim before the high priest.
2. Examine **Zechariah 12-14** — the most extensive prophetic block on the Coming One's intervention in Jerusalem.
3. Examine **Matthew 24-25 (the Olivet Discourse)** — Jesus' own direct teaching about His return.
4. Cross-reference all other major NT witnesses (1 Thess, 2 Thess, 2 Peter, the Apocalypse) as they bear on the subject.

We will let the whole counsel of the God-breathed Word establish what is to be established.

---

## **Daniel 7 — The Son of Man before the Ancient of Days; the (B) Arc Opens**

# **DANIEL 7 — THE SON OF MAN BEFORE THE ANCIENT OF DAYS**

This is the foundational vision of the Son's coming reign, and it is the text Jesus Himself cited as His own (Matt 26:64; Mark 14:62).

### **Daniel 7:9-10, NETS (Old Greek):**

> I kept watching until thrones were set, and an ancient of days sat, having a cloak like snow, and the tuft of hair on his head was like pure wool. The throne was like a flame of fire shooting out, and a stream of fire went out from before him. A thousand thousands were waiting on him, and ten thousand times ten thousand stood attending him. And books were opened, and a court sat in judgment. — **Daniel 7:9-10, NETS (Old Greek)**

*NETS’s Daniel carries Old Greek and Theodotion in parallel columns; the draft cites the LXX voice. Theodotion reads “and his clothing was white like snow, and the hair of his head was like pure wool; his throne was a flame of fire; its wheels were burning fire. A stream of fire drew in before him. A thousand thousands were serving him … A court sat in judgment, and books were opened.” *(Theodotion, the parallel column)* — the Old Greek print has “having a cloak like snow, and the tuft of hair on his head … were waiting on him … And books were opened, and a court sat in judgment.” The draft’s observation Greek κριτήριον ἐκάθισεν, καὶ βίβλοι ἠνεῴχθησαν carries the Theodotion order (a court sat, and books were opened).

### Observations of foundational weight

**(1)** **παλαιὸς ἡμερῶν** — *"the Ancient of Days."* This is **the Father** — the One God, the supreme Judge, enthroned in fire and surrounded by myriads of attendants. Per our foundation: **the Ancient of Days = the Father, the One God of the Shema and Isaiah 44-46.**

**(2)** **θρόνοι ἐτέθησαν** — *"thrones were set up"* (plural). Multiple thrones — preparing for the Son to be brought before the Father to receive His throne.

**(3)** **κριτήριον ἐκάθισεν, καὶ βίβλοι ἠνεῴχθησαν** — *"the court sat, and the books were opened."* The judgment scene is convened. This corresponds to **Revelation 20:11-12** — the great white throne judgment, where books are opened.

---

## **Daniel 7:13-14, NETS (Old Greek) — The Son of Man Coming**

### **Daniel 7:13-14, NETS (Old Greek):**

> I was watching in the night visions, and lo, as it were a son of man was coming upon the clouds of heaven. And he came as far as the ancient of days, and the attendants were present with him. And royal authority was given to him, and all the nations of the earth according to posterity, and all honor was serving him. And his authority is an everlasting authority, which shall never be removed— and his kingship, which will never perish. — **Daniel 7:13-14, NETS (Old Greek)**

*The same parallel columns; the seat prints the Old Greek. Theodotion reads “coming with the clouds of heaven … and was presented to him. And to him was given the dominion and the honor and the kingship … which will not be destroyed.” *(Theodotion, the parallel column)*. The draft’s observation Greek καὶ ὡς παλαιὸς ἡμερῶν παρῆن is the Theodotion wording the Old Greek print renders “And he came as far as the ancient of days” — recorded when this passage was first seated (the Self-Identifications wave); no change now.

### Observations of supreme importance for our study

**(1)** **ὡς υἱὸς ἀνθρώπου** — *"as a Son of Man."* The figure is described as **like a son of man** — i.e., **in the form of a Man.** This corresponds with everything we have established about the Son: He is the exalted Man Christ Jesus (1 Tim 2:5; Heb 9:24).

**(2)** **ἐπὶ τῶν νεφελῶν τοῦ οὐρανοῦ ἐρχόμενος** — *"coming on the clouds of heaven."* The mode of His coming is on the clouds — fulfilled in:
- Acts 1:9-11 (a cloud received Him; He shall so come)
- Matt 24:30; 26:64
- Rev 1:7

**(3)** **ὡς παλαιὸς ἡμερῶν παρῆν** — *"He came as far as the Ancient of Days."* The Son of Man **approaches** the Ancient of Days. **He is brought *before* the Ancient of Days — He is *not* the Ancient of Days.** This is decisive for our foundation. Two distinct figures: the Father enthroned, and the Son of Man brought before Him.

**(4)** **ἐδόθη αὐτῷ ἐξουσία** — *"authority was given to Him."* **By whom?** The text's structure makes it clear: by the Ancient of Days (the Father). This is the same passive language we have seen throughout — **the Father gives; the Son receives.** Matches Matt 28:18 (*"All authority has been given to Me"*) — explicitly received from the Father.

**(5)** **πάντα τὰ ἔθνη τῆς γῆς... αὐτῷ λατρεύουσα** — *"all the nations of the earth... serving/worshipping Him."* The verb **λατρεύω** is the verb for **divine worship/service** — used of worship rendered to God. The Son of Man receives the divine service of all nations **by the Father's grant**.
