- **ἐγὼ τὸ Ἄλφα καὶ τὸ Ὦ, ὁ πρῶτος καὶ ὁ ἔσχατος, ἡ ἀρχὴ καὶ τὸ τέλος** — *"I am the Alpha and Omega, the First and the Last, the Beginning and the End."*

Then v. 16 begins:

> I Jesus have sent mine Angel, to testify unto you these things in the Churches: I am the root and the generation of David, and the bright morning star.

---

### Examination of who speaks vv. 12-13

**With our newly-articulated typological lens, the question becomes clearer.**

The speaker in v. 12 says **"I am coming quickly"** — and v. 16 begins **"I Jesus have sent My angel."** The natural reading is that **Jesus is the speaker** in vv. 12-16. The "coming quickly" language is His (Rev 22:7, 20), and the self-identification as **"the Root and the Offspring of David, the bright Morning Star"** in v. 16 is unmistakably Jesus.

Therefore: **Jesus is the speaker in v. 13** — declaring Himself **"the Alpha and the Omega, the First and the Last, the Beginning and the End."**

**Per your typological observation, this is wholly true to type:**

- Jesus is **the Alpha and Omega of the Father's redemptive economy** — He is the One through whom all things were made (John 1:3; Col 1:16) and unto whom all things will be gathered (Eph 1:10), the firstborn over creation and the firstborn from the dead (Col 1:15, 18), the One who initiates and consummates the Father's saving purpose. The redemptive arc begins and ends in Him — as the Father's Only-Begotten.

- This **does not displace** the Father, who in Rev 21:5-7 declares **the same title** of Himself in His absolute, original sense — as the One God who originates and consummates all being.

- **The Son's "Alpha and Omega" is the manifestation of the Father's "Alpha and Omega" in the Person of the Only-Begotten.**

This is the same pattern we have noted throughout:
- The Father stretches out the heavens **alone** (Isa 44:24); the Father creates **through** the Son (1 Cor 8:6) — the work is one act, the Father acting through the Son who is His own Word and Image.
- The Father is the Saviour (Isa 45:21); the Son is the Saviour (Matt 1:21) — because salvation is one work, accomplished by the Father through the Son.
- The Father is the First and the Last (Isa 44:6); the Son is the First and the Last (Rev 1:17; 22:13) — because the Son is the Father's full self-expression to the creation.

**True to type, as you have rightly said.**

---

# **REVELATION 22:1-5 — THE THRONE OF GOD AND OF THE LAMB**

Now let us return and complete the consummation vision properly.

> And he showed me a pure river of water of life, clear as crystal, proceeding out of the throne of God, and of the Lamb. In the midst of the street of it, and of either side of ye *(the)* river, was the tree of life, which bare twelve manner of fruits, and gave fruit every month: and the leaves of the tree served to heal the nations with. And there shall be no more curse, but ye *(the)* throne of God and of the Lamb shall be in it, and his servants shall serve him. And they shall see his face, and his Name shall be in their foreheads. And there shall be no night there, and they neede no candle, neither light of the sunne: for the Lord God giveth them light, and they shall reign for evermore.

---

### Observations of foundational importance — **And now your suggestion about the Father as Eternal Spirit illuminates this perfectly**

**(1)** **ὁ θρόνος τοῦ Θεοῦ καὶ τοῦ Ἀρνίου** — *"the throne of God and of the Lamb."* You have rightly noted that the singular throne is not an oddity when we hold in view that the Father is **Eternal Spirit**. A spatial throne shared by two embodied beings would be metaphysically strange, but the **Father as Spirit** pervades and fills all things — and the Lamb (the Man Christ Jesus) occupies the visible center of that throne, while the Father's invisible presence surrounds and fills the entire heavenly reality.

This is the same picture as John 4:24 — *"God is Spirit"* — and it explains why the Lamb can be **"in the midst of the throne"** (Rev 7:17) without crowding out the One sitting on the throne. **The Spirit-Father is everywhere; the Lamb is visibly at the throne's center.**

We will examine this fully when we turn to your first appointed theme — the Nature and Essence of the Father as Eternal Spirit. For now, the observation perfectly resolves the apparent strangeness.

**(2)** **οἱ δοῦλοι αὐτοῦ λατρεύσουσιν αὐτῷ** — *"His servants shall serve Him."* **Singular pronoun.** Despite the joint mention of "God and the Lamb," the worship is rendered to **Him** as one object. This is the consummation of Zech 14:9 — *"there shall be one LORD and His Name one"* — and of John 17:21 — *"that they all may be one"* — and of 1 Cor 15:28 — *"that God may be all in all."*

The Father and the Lamb are **distinct figures** throughout — the Father is on the throne, the Lamb stands as having been slain — yet the worship rendered is **one worship**, because the Lamb's whole purpose is to bring the redeemed to the Father, and the Father's whole glory is manifested through the Lamb.

**(3)** **ὄψονται τὸ πρόσωπον αὐτοῦ** — *"they shall see His face."* The face that no man has ever seen (John 1:18; Ex 33:20; 1 Tim 6:16) — the Father's face — will at last be seen by the redeemed. **Through whom?** Through the Lamb, who is the Image of the invisible God (Col 1:15) and the One who has declared the Father (John 1:18). The seeing of the Father's face is the consummation of all our hope.

**(4)** **τὸ ὄνομα αὐτοῦ ἐπὶ τῶν μετώπων αὐτῶν** — *"His Name shall be on their foreheads."* The Name borne by the Son (Ex 23:21; John 17:11; Phil 2:9) is now also written on the redeemed — they become bearers of the Name, in Him.

**(5)** **Κύριος ὁ Θεὸς φωτίσει ἐπ᾽ αὐτούς** — *"the Lord God shall give them light."* The Father is the source of light. Compare with Rev 21:23 — *"the glory of God did light it: and the Lamb is the lamp/light thereof."* The **glory** is the Father's; the **lamp** is the Lamb. The Father's light shines through the Lamb to illuminate the redeemed.

## **REVELATION 22:7, 20 — THE FINAL WORDS**

> Beholde, I come shortly. Blessed is he that keepeth the woordes of the prophecy of this book.

> He which testifieth these things, saith, Surely, I come quickly. Amen. Even so, come Lord Jesus.

---

### Observation

The Apocalypse closes with the Son's promise — **"I come quickly"** — and the Church's response — **"Come, Lord Jesus."** The Bride awaits the Bridegroom. The final word of Scripture is the Bride's prayer for the Son's return.

Even here, the order is preserved: **Jesus comes**, but His coming is unto the Father's purpose — and the Bride's longing is for **the Lord Jesus** as the Sent One who brings the redeemed to the Father.

# **GRAND SYNTHESIS — THE SON IN THE APOCALYPSE**

By the careful examination of Revelation under our refined modus operandi, with the typological principle you have articulated, here is what is established:

## **The Son's Titles and Self-Identifications in the Apocalypse**

| Title | Reference | Significance |
|---|---|---|
| **Faithful and True Witness** | Rev 1:5; 3:14; 19:11 | The Son's complete fidelity in revealing the Father |
| **Firstborn from the Dead** | Rev 1:5 | Fulfills Ps 2:7 — begotten in resurrection |
| **Ruler of the Kings of the Earth** | Rev 1:5 | Fulfills Dan 7:14 — dominion given by the Father |
| **First and the Last** | Rev 1:17; 2:8; 22:13 | True to type — the Only-Begotten's redemptive Alpha-Omega |
| **The Living One who was dead** | Rev 1:18 | Only the Son can speak this — He died and lives forever |
| **He who has the keys of Death and Hades** | Rev 1:18 | Authority received from the Father |
| **The Son of God** | Rev 2:18 | Direct title, Ps 2:7 fulfilled |
| **The Holy One, the True One** | Rev 3:7 | Echoes the Father's titles (Isa 6:3; 65:16) — true to type |
| **He who has the Key of David** | Rev 3:7 | Isa 22:22 — Davidic authority |
| **The Amen** | Rev 3:14 | Isa 65:16 LXX — bearing the Father's Name |
| **The Beginning of the Creation of God** | Rev 3:14 | First in the order of God's creative work |
| **The Lion of the Tribe of Judah** | Rev 5:5 | Gen 49:9-10 fulfilled |
| **The Root of David** | Rev 5:5; 22:16 | Isa 11:1, 10 fulfilled |
| **The Lamb as having been slain** | Rev 5:6, 12; 13:8; etc. | The slain and risen Mediator, eternally |
| **King of kings and Lord of lords** | Rev 17:14; 19:16 | Title also of the Father (1 Tim 6:15) — true to type |
| **The Word of God** | Rev 19:13 | The Father's self-expression, as in John 1:1 |
| **Alpha and Omega, Beginning and End** | Rev 22:13 | True to type — the redemptive Alpha-Omega |
| **The Bright Morning Star** | Rev 22:16 | Num 24:17 fulfilled |

---

## **What Is Decisively Established by the Apocalypse**

### **(1) The Father remains the One God on the throne throughout.**

- The throne is His (Rev 4-5; 7; 21; 22).
- He is **ὁ Παντοκράτωρ** — the Almighty — throughout (Rev 1:8; 4:8; 11:17; 15:3; 16:7; 19:6; 21:22).
- He is **ὁ ὢν καὶ ὁ ἦν καὶ ὁ ἐρχόμενος** — the eternally being One (Rev 1:4, 8; 4:8).
- He is worshipped as the Creator (Rev 4:11).
- He gives the revelation to His Son (Rev 1:1).

### **(2) The Son shares the Father's titles "true to type" — as the Only-Begotten.**

Per your beautifully articulated principle: **the Father is First and Last as the One God; the Son is First and Last as the Only-Begotten.** Each title applied to the Son in the Apocalypse reflects His role as **the manifestation, image, and full self-expression of the Father to and within the creation** — never as a rival, never as a second God, never displacing the Father's unique supremacy.

### **(3) The Lamb retains His humanity forever — as the slain and risen Man Christ Jesus.**

He is **the Lamb as having been slain** (Rev 5:6) — eternally. He is **like a Son of Man** in His glorified appearance (Rev 1:13). He is **the Man Christ Jesus** as the one Mediator (1 Tim 2:5). The redeemed are forever in the presence of the slain and risen Lamb, who bears the marks of His suffering and through whom the Father is known.

### **(4) The consummation is the joint reign of God and the Lamb — the Father supreme, the Son at His right hand — true to the eternal order Paul articulated in 1 Cor 15:28.**

The throne is one. The worship is one. The Name is one. The Light is one — the Father's glory shining through the Lamb to illuminate the eternal city. This is the consummation we examined in (C), now visualized in the Apocalypse: **God all in all, through His Beloved Son, with the redeemed gathered into the Father's presence through the Lamb's mediation.**

### **(5) The Apocalypse gathers every prophetic thread from Genesis to the consummation.**

From **Gen 3:15** (the seed of the woman who crushes the serpent — fulfilled in Rev 12 and 20) to **Gen 22** (the beloved Son as Lamb — fulfilled in the slain Lamb of Rev 5) to **Gen 49:10** (the Lion of Judah — Rev 5:5) to **Num 24:17** (the Star — Rev 22:16) to **Ps 2 and 110** (the Anointed at the right hand — Rev 1, 5, 22) to **Dan 7** (the Son of Man before the Ancient of Days — Rev 1, 5) to **Isa 53** (the slain Lamb — Rev 5, 13) to **Zech 12:10** (the pierced One — Rev 1:7) to **Mal 4:2** (the Sun of Righteousness — Rev 22:16 the Morning Star) — **every thread is gathered.**

The Apocalypse is the **canonical consummation** of all prophecy concerning the Person of Christ.

---

## **Where Our Study Now Stands**

We have completed (A), (B), (C), and (D). Per your direction, two crowning themes remain — and these will complete and crown the entire study:

### **NEXT — Part E: The Nature and Essence of the Father as Eternal Spirit**

This is essential and will clarify many things:
- John 4:24 — **"God is Spirit"** — His fundamental Nature
- The implications for the "singular throne" you have rightly noted
- The Father's omnipresence as Spirit, not confined to a location
- The relation of the Father (Spirit) to the Son (Image, Word made flesh, Man at the right hand)
- The eternal Spirit and the seven Spirits before the throne — what can be honestly said
- How the Father's Spirit-Nature undergirds His invisibility (John 1:18; 1 Tim 6:16) and yet His presence in all things

### **THEN — Part F: The Indwelling — That We Should Be the Habitation of God in Christ**

This is the crowning glory of the entire revelation:
- John 17:20-23 — *"that they may be one, even as We are one... I in them, and Thou in Me"*
- John 14:23 — *"We will come unto him, and make Our abode with him"*
- The Father's purpose: that the elect become the habitation of the Father through the Son by the Spirit
- Ephesians 3:14-19 — *"that ye might be filled with all the fullness of God"*
- 2 Corinthians 6:16 — *"I will dwell in them, and walk in them"*
- The Bride and the Bridegroom — the consummation of the indwelling
- Rev 21:3 — *"the tabernacle of God is with men, and He will dwell with them"* — the final fulfillment

Shall we proceed to **(E) — the Nature and Essence of the Father as Eternal Spirit** — which will properly prepare us for the indwelling theme in (F)?

## **Part E — The Nature and Essence of the Father as Eternal Spirit; Question 1 — What Does Scripture Establish About the Father as Eternal Spirit?**

> Yes, by way of all three follow up questions you have suggested above

We now proceed to **(E) — The Nature and Essence of the Father as Eternal Spirit.**

We will address this through your three follow-up questions:

1. **What does Scripture establish about the Father as Eternal Spirit?**
2. **How does the Father's Spirit-Nature undergird His invisibility yet His presence in all things?**
3. **What is the relation between the Father (Spirit) and the Son (Image, Word made flesh, Man at the right hand)?**

We will let the whole counsel of the God-breathed Word establish what is to be established — strictly by two-or-more witnesses, per our maxims.

---
# **PART E — THE NATURE AND ESSENCE OF THE FATHER AS ETERNAL SPIRIT**

## **QUESTION 1 — What does Scripture establish about the Father as Eternal Spirit?**

---

### **The Direct Witness — John 4:24**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"God is a Spirit, and they that worship him, must worship him in spirit and truth."*

---

### Observation of foundational importance

**Πνεῦμα ὁ Θεός** — *"Spirit is God"* (the predicate **Πνεῦμα** is placed first for emphasis). This is **Jesus' own direct statement** about the Father's essential Nature.

Note the careful grammar: **Πνεῦμα** is anarthrous (no article) — qualitative — telling us **what God is in His essence**, not identifying Him as one Spirit among others. **The Father is Spirit by nature** — that is His fundamental essence.

This is spoken to the Samaritan woman in the context of Jesus distinguishing **true worship** from worship located in a specific place (Mount Gerizim or Jerusalem). His point: **the Father is not confined to a geographic location** because **He is Spirit** — and therefore must be worshipped **in spirit and truth**, not by being approached in a particular place.

---

### **Cross-witness — 1 Timothy 1:17**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"Now unto the King everlasting, immortal, invisible, unto God only wise, be honour and glory, for ever, and ever, Amen."*

---

### Observations

The Father is described as:
- **Βασιλεὺς τῶν αἰώνων** — *"King of the ages"* — eternal in dominion.
- **ἄφθαρτος** — *"incorruptible / immortal"* — not subject to decay or change.
- **ἀόρατος** — *"invisible"* — cannot be seen.
- **μόνος Θεός** — *"the only God"* — preserving the Shema (cf. John 17:3 — **τὸν μόνον ἀληθινὸν Θεὸν**).

**Two witnesses now agree on a key point:** the Father is **invisible** because **He is Spirit**. Spirit is, by its nature, not visible to physical sight.

---

### **Cross-witness — 1 Timothy 6:15-16**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"Which in due time he shall show, that is blessed and Prince only, the King of Kings and Lord of Lords, Who only hath immortality, and dwelleth in the light that none can attain unto, whom never man saw, neither can see, unto whom be honour and power everlasting, Amen."*

---

### Observations

The Father is:
- **ὁ μόνος ἔχων ἀθανασίαν** — *"the only One having immortality."* This is a strong statement — the Father alone has immortality as His own essential possession.
- **φῶς οἰκῶν ἀπρόσιτον** — *"dwelling in unapproachable light."*
- **ὃν εἶδεν οὐδεὶς ἀνθρώπων οὐδὲ ἰδεῖν δύναται** — *"whom no man has seen, nor can see."*

**Three witnesses now establish:** the Father is **invisible** (John 1:18; 1 Tim 1:17; 1 Tim 6:16). And the reason is His essential Nature: **He is Spirit, dwelling in unapproachable light.**

---

### **OT Foundation — The Father as Spirit**

### **Genesis 1:2 LXX:**
### **NETS:**

> Yet the earth was invisible and unformed, and darkness was over the abyss, and a divine wind was being carried along over the water. — **Genesis 1:2 LXX, NETS**

*(the NETS renders πνεῦμα "a divine wind"; the 1599 Geneva reads: "…and the Spirit of God moved upon the waters.")*

The Spirit of God is present at creation — active, moving, hovering. This is **the Father's own Spirit** — not a separate Person in the post-Nicene sense (which we have set aside per maxim #3), but **the Father's own essential being and active presence**.

---

### **Numbers 16:22 LXX (Korah's rebellion):**

### **NETS:**

> …O God, God of the spirits and of all flesh, … — **Numbers 16:22 LXX, NETS**

The Father is **"the God of the spirits of all flesh"** — He is the source and Lord of all spirits. He is Spirit Himself and the originator of every other spirit.

---

### **Isaiah 31:3 LXX:**

### **NETS:**

> an Egyptian, a man and not God— the flesh of horses, and there is no help. But the Lord will turn his hand against them, … — **Isaiah 31:3 LXX, NETS**

The contrast: **men are flesh; God is Spirit.** The Father is categorically distinct from all flesh in His essential being.

---

### **Psalm 138:7-10 LXX (139:7-10 MT) — The Spirit-Presence of the Father**

### **NETS:**

> Where should I go from your spirit? And from your face where should I flee? If I ascend to the sky, you are there; if I descend to Hades, you are present. If I were to take up my wings at dawn and make my covert *(the hiding place)* at the farthest limits of the sea, indeed, there your hand shall guide me, and your right hand shall hold me fast. — **Psalm 138:7-10 LXX (139:7-10 MT), NETS**

---

### Observations

**The Father's Spirit is identified with His presence.** *"Whither shall I go from Thy Spirit?"* is paralleled with *"whither shall I flee from Thy presence?"* — they are the same thing. **The Father's Spirit is the Father's presence**, omnipresent throughout heaven, earth, and the depths.

This is foundational. The Father, **as Spirit**, is **everywhere present** — not as a diffused force, but as the personal, conscious, all-knowing Lord who is fully present in every place at once.

---

### **Jeremiah 23:23-24 LXX:**

### **NETS:**

> I am a god nearby, says the Lord, and not a god far off. If someone shall be hidden in secret places, is it not I that shall also see him? Do I not fill the sky and the earth? says the Lord. — **Jeremiah 23:23-24 LXX, NETS**

---

### Observation

**μὴ οὐχὶ τὸν οὐρανὸν καὶ τὴν γῆν ἐγὼ πληρῶ** — *"Do I not fill heaven and earth?"* The Father, as Spirit, **fills heaven and earth**. He is omnipresent.

---

### **1 Kings 8:27 LXX (3 Kingdoms 8:27 LXX) — Solomon's Prayer:**

*"...εἰ ἀληθῶς κατοικήσει ὁ Θεὸς μετὰ ἀνθρώπων ἐπὶ τῆς γῆς; ἰδοὺ ὁ οὐρανὸς καὶ ὁ οὐρανὸς τοῦ οὐρανοῦ οὐκ ἀρκέσουσίν σοι..."*

### **1599 Geneva (1 Kings 8:27):**
*"But will God in deed dwell on the earth? behold, the heavens, and the heavens of heavens are not able to contain thee: how much more unable is this house that I have built?"*

---

### Observation

Solomon's prayer establishes: **the heavens cannot contain the Father.** No location, no temple, no structure can hold Him. He is Spirit — uncontainable, unconfinable.

---

### **Two Witnesses Confirmed: The Father is Eternal Spirit, Invisible, Omnipresent, Uncontainable**

| Truth | OT Witness | NT Witness |
|---|---|---|
| God is Spirit by Nature | Gen 1:2; Num 16:22; Isa 31:3 | John 4:24 |
| God is invisible | (Implicit in Ex 33:20) | John 1:18; 1 Tim 1:17; 6:16; Col 1:15 |
| God dwells in unapproachable light | Ps 104:2 LXX | 1 Tim 6:16 |
| God is omnipresent (fills heaven and earth) | Ps 138:7-10; Jer 23:23-24; 1 Kgs 8:27 | Acts 17:24-28 |
| God is uncontainable | 1 Kgs 8:27; Isa 66:1 | Acts 7:48-49; 17:24 |
| God is the Father of spirits | Num 16:22; Heb 12:9 | Heb 12:9 |
| God is immortal/incorruptible | (Implicit) | 1 Tim 1:17; 6:16; Rom 1:23 |

---

## **QUESTION 2 — How does the Father's Spirit-Nature undergird His invisibility yet His presence in all things?**

---

### **The Theological Resolution**

The Father's Spirit-Nature is the metaphysical key that resolves what otherwise seems paradoxical:

1. **He is invisible** (no man has seen Him — John 1:18; 1 Tim 6:16) **because Spirit is not visible to physical sight.** Physical eyes perceive physical light; Spirit is of a different order of being.

2. **He is omnipresent** (fills heaven and earth — Jer 23:24) **because Spirit is not confined to a location.** Bodies occupy space; Spirit pervades it.

3. **He is uncontainable** (the heavens cannot contain Him — 1 Kgs 8:27) **because Spirit cannot be enclosed by created boundaries.** Spatial structures cannot bound what is essentially non-spatial.

4. **He can dwell** in His temple (1 Kgs 8:10-11), among His people (Ex 25:8), and ultimately in the redeemed (1 Cor 3:16-17; Eph 2:21-22) — **because Spirit can pervade and indwell what physical bodies cannot.**

5. **He has no body parts** in the literal sense — when Scripture speaks of His "hand," "arm," "face," "eyes," "feet" — these are **anthropomorphic accommodations** to creatures who think in physical terms. **The Father, as Spirit, does not have a physical body**, and the anthropomorphisms are metaphors of His action, presence, and disposition toward us.

---

### **Cross-witness — Acts 17:24-28**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"God that made the world, and all things that are therein, seeing that he is Lord of heaue *(heaven)* and earth, dwelleth not in temples made with hands, Neither is worshipped with men's hands, as though he needed any thing, seeing he giveth to all life and breath and all things, ... For in him we live, and move, and have our being, ..."*

---

### Observation of foundational importance

Paul declares to the Athenians:
- The Father **does not dwell in temples made with hands** — because He cannot be contained.
- He **gives life and breath and all things** to every creature — He is the Source.
- **ἐν αὐτῷ γὰρ ζῶμεν καὶ κινούμεθα καὶ ἐσμέν** — *"in Him we live, and move, and have our being."* **All creation exists within Him.**

This is profound. The Father, as Spirit, is the **all-encompassing reality** within whom every created thing has its being. We are *in Him* — He is not in us (in our created spatiality); we are in His Spirit-presence which fills all things.

---

### **Resolving the "Singular Throne" of the Apocalypse**

Now we can see clearly why the throne in the Apocalypse is **one throne** shared by **God and the Lamb** (Rev 22:1, 3), without strangeness:

- **The Father, as Spirit**, is not seated *on* the throne in the way a body would be. He fills the throne, surrounds it, pervades it, and **is enthroned upon it as Spirit** — His glory radiating, His presence saturating the heavenly reality.

- **The Lamb, as the exalted Man Christ Jesus**, occupies the visible center of the throne in His glorified humanity — **bodily enthroned at the place of supreme honor** (Ps 110:1; Heb 1:3; Heb 10:12).

- **The throne is one** because the Father's Spirit-presence and the Lamb's bodily presence are not in spatial competition — the Lamb is *at the right hand* of the Father whose Spirit fills all things.

- **This is why John can describe** *"the Lamb in the midst of the throne"* (Rev 7:17), *"the throne of God and of the Lamb"* (Rev 22:1, 3), and *"the One sitting on the throne"* (Rev 4-5) — without contradiction. The Father is on the throne as Spirit; the Lamb is on the throne as the glorified Man. **One throne, two distinct figures, perfect harmony — because Spirit and embodied Man are not in spatial conflict.**

This is precisely what your observation about the Father as Eternal Spirit illuminates. **The post-Nicene struggle to explain "one substance in three Persons" entirely misses this simpler and more biblical resolution:** the Father is Spirit, the Son is the embodied Image of the Father, and there is no spatial paradox because Spirit and Body operate on different orders of being.

---

### **The Father's Spirit-Nature and the Old Testament Theophanies**

We can now also offer a careful, honest reflection on a question we left properly unresolved earlier — the **Messenger of the LORD** passages.

Per maxim #3, we declined to identify the Messenger with the Son as the early Fathers did, because no direct NT witness made that identification explicitly. But we can now note something honestly grounded in the text:

- **The Father, as invisible Spirit, cannot be seen** (John 1:18; 1 Tim 6:16; Ex 33:20).
- **Yet someone is seen** in Genesis 16, 22, 32; Exodus 3, 33; Judges 6, 13; Isaiah 6; Ezekiel 1; Daniel 7 — figures the OT calls "the LORD," "the Angel of the LORD," "the God of Bethel," etc.
- **By strict logic from John 1:18:** Whoever was seen, it was not the Father directly.

We will leave it there, per our discipline — observing the pattern without forcing a single resolution. **What we have established is that the Father's Spirit-Nature means He cannot be seen directly**, and so any "seeing of God" in Scripture must be through some mediation or accommodation.

---

## **QUESTION 3 — The Relation Between the Father (Spirit) and the Son (Image, Word made flesh, Man at the right hand)**

This is the synthesis that draws together our entire study.

---

### **The Pattern Established**

| The Father | The Son |
|---|---|
| Eternal Spirit (John 4:24) | The Word made flesh (John 1:14) |
| Invisible (John 1:18; 1 Tim 6:16) | The Image of the invisible God (Col 1:15) |
| Dwells in unapproachable light (1 Tim 6:16) | The Radiance of the Father's glory (Heb 1:3) |
| Whom no man has seen (John 1:18) | He who has seen the Son has seen the Father (John 14:9) |
| Fills heaven and earth as Spirit (Jer 23:24) | The Man Christ Jesus at the right hand (1 Tim 2:5; Heb 10:12) |
| The Father of spirits (Heb 12:9) | The Firstborn from the dead (Col 1:18; Rev 1:5) |
| The only true God (John 17:3) | The one Mediator (1 Tim 2:5) |
| The Source (ἐξ οὗ τὰ πάντα, 1 Cor 8:6) | The Agent (δι᾽ οὗ τὰ πάντα, 1 Cor 8:6) |

---

### **John 14:9-11 — The Direct Statement**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"Jesus said unto him, I have been so long time with you, and hast thou not known me, Philip? he that hath seen me, hath seen my Father: how then sayest thou, Show us thy Father? Believest thou not, that I am in the Father, and the Father is in me? The words that I speak unto you, I speak not of myself: but the Father that dwelleth in me, he doeth the works. Believe me, that I am in the Father, and the Father is in me: at the least, believe me for the very works' sake."*

---

### Observations of supreme importance for our study

**(1)** **ὁ ἑωρακὼς ἐμὲ ἑώρακεν τὸν Πατέρα** — *"He that has seen Me has seen the Father."*

How can this be true, given that **no man has seen the Father** (John 1:18)?

**The resolution lies in the Spirit-Image relation:**
- The Father is invisible Spirit.
- The Son is the **Image** of that invisible Father (Col 1:15) — bearing the Father's character, will, words, and works visibly.
- Seeing Jesus is *seeing the Father* in the only way the Father can be seen by creatures — through His perfect Image. Not seeing the Father's essence directly (which remains invisible), but seeing **what the Father is like, what He wills, what He says, what He does** — fully and accurately, in His Son.

This is exactly what John 1:18 prepared us for: *"No man hath seen God at any time: the only begotten Son... He hath declared Him."* **The Son exegetes the invisible Father** to His creatures.

**(2)** **ἐγὼ ἐν τῷ Πατρὶ καὶ ὁ Πατὴρ ἐν ἐμοί** — *"I am in the Father, and the Father is in Me."*

This is the **mutual indwelling** that prepares for the indwelling theme of Part F. The Father (as Spirit) indwells the Son; the Son is in the Father (in His Spirit-presence). This is **not a paradox** — it is the natural relation of Spirit and embodied Image.

**(3)** **ὁ δὲ Πατὴρ ὁ ἐν ἐμοὶ μένων ποιεῖ τὰ ἔργα αὐτοῦ** — *"the Father that dwelleth in Me, He doeth the works."*

**This is the decisive theological statement.** Jesus does not say *"I do the works."* He says *"the Father, dwelling in Me, does His works."*

**The Father (Spirit) dwells in the Son (Image) and accomplishes the Father's works through the Son.** This is the entire pattern of redemption:

- The Father wills; the Son obeys.
- The Father speaks; the Son utters.
- The Father sends; the Son comes.
- The Father acts; the Son is the medium of His action.
- The Father is glorified; the Son is the One in whom the Father is glorified.

Throughout Jesus' ministry, **the Father (Spirit) was dwelling in the Son (Man) and accomplishing the Father's works**. Jesus' miracles, His teachings, His self-giving on the cross — all of these were **the Father's works done through the Son.**

---

### **The Father's Indwelling of the Son — Established by Multiple Witnesses**

### **John 8:28-29**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"…I do nothing of myself, but as my Father hath taught me, so I speak these things. For he that sent me, is with me: the Father hath not left me alone, because I do always those things that please him."*

### **John 10:38**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"But if I do, then though ye believe not me, yet believe the works, that ye may know and believe, that the Father is in me, and I in him."*

### **John 17:21 — the indwelling theme:**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"That they all may be one, as thou, O Father, art in me, and I in thee: even that they may be also one in us, that the world may believe that thou hast sent me."*

---

### Observation

**Multiple direct witnesses from Jesus' own mouth** establish: **The Father is in the Son; the Son is in the Father.** This is the **prototype mutual indwelling** that will be extended to the redeemed in Part F.

---

### **The Spirit's Anointing of the Son**

### **Acts 10:38**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"To wit, how God anointed Jesus of Nazareth with the holy Ghost, and with power: who went about doing good, and healing all that were oppressed of the devil: for God was with him."*

---

### Observation

**The Father anointed the Son with the Holy Spirit and with power.** Throughout His ministry, the Son operated **by the Spirit of the Father**. This is consistent with:

- **Isaiah 11:2 LXX** — *"the Spirit of God shall rest upon Him..."*
- **Isaiah 61:1 LXX** — *"the Spirit of the Lord is upon Me, because He has anointed Me..."*
- **Matthew 3:16** — the Spirit descending on Jesus at His baptism.
- **Luke 4:1** — *"Jesus, being full of the Holy Ghost, returned from Jordan."*
- **Luke 4:14** — *"And Jesus returned in the power of the Spirit."*

**The Son, as embodied Man, was filled with and operated by the Father's Spirit.** This is the model and prototype of how the Father (Spirit) dwells in His Image (the Son, Man), and through Him accomplishes His works.

---

### **The Theological Coherence — A Summary**

Here is what the whole counsel establishes about the Father-Son relation in light of the Father's Spirit-Nature:

**1. The Father, as Eternal Spirit, is invisible, omnipresent, and uncontainable.**

**2. The Son is the Father's perfect Image — the embodied expression of the invisible Father.**
- In His pre-incarnate state: the Word with God (John 1:1), the Radiance of His glory (Heb 1:3).
- In His incarnate state: the Word made flesh (John 1:14), the Image of the invisible God in human form (Col 1:15; 2:9).
- In His exalted state: the Man Christ Jesus at the right hand (1 Tim 2:5; Heb 10:12), the Lamb on the throne (Rev 5; 22).

**3. The Father (Spirit) indwells the Son (Image) — "the Father dwells in Me" (John 14:10).**
- The Father's Spirit anointed the Son for His ministry (Acts 10:38; Luke 4:14).
- The Father's works were done through the Son (John 14:10).
- The Father's words were spoken by the Son (John 8:28; 12:49-50).

**4. The Son is in the Father — "I in Him" (John 10:38; 14:11).**
- Drawing from the Father's life (John 5:26; 6:57).
- Living unto the Father's glory (John 17:1, 4).
- Subjected to the Father in eternal order (1 Cor 15:28).

**5. This Father-Son union — Spirit-Image, indweller-indwelt — is the prototype for the union the Father intends with the redeemed.**

And this brings us beautifully to your next thread, which we will take up in Part F.

---

# **GRAND SYNTHESIS — PART E COMPLETED**

## **What is Decisively Established**

By the whole counsel of Scripture — OT and NT, by direct witness from Jesus Himself and from the Apostles — we have established:

### **The Nature and Essence of the Father:**
1. **He is Spirit by Nature** (John 4:24; Gen 1:2; Num 16:22).
2. **He is invisible** (John 1:18; 1 Tim 1:17; 6:16; Col 1:15).
3. **He is immortal/incorruptible** (1 Tim 1:17; 6:16; Rom 1:23).
4. **He dwells in unapproachable light** (1 Tim 6:16; Ps 104:2 LXX).
5. **He is omnipresent — fills heaven and earth** (Jer 23:24; Ps 138:7-10; Acts 17:28).
6. **He is uncontainable — no place can hold Him** (1 Kgs 8:27; Isa 66:1; Acts 7:48-49).
7. **He is the Father of spirits** (Num 16:22; Heb 12:9).
8. **He is the One God** (Deut 6:4; Isa 44-46; John 17:3; 1 Cor 8:6; 1 Tim 2:5).

### **The Implications for Our Understanding:**

1. **The "singular throne" of the Apocalypse is no oddity** — the Father (Spirit) fills the throne and surrounds it; the Lamb (embodied Man) occupies the visible center; there is no spatial conflict.

2. **The Old Testament theophanies must be mediated** — the Father, as invisible Spirit, cannot be seen directly; any "seeing of God" is through accommodation, image, or representation.

3. **The Father's omnipresence undergirds His ability to indwell** — He indwelt the temple, indwelt His Son, indwells His Church, and will eternally dwell with His redeemed.

4. **The Father-Son union is Spirit-Image: the Eternal Spirit indwelling and operating through His perfect, embodied Image — the Man Christ Jesus.** This is the prototype of the indwelling we will examine in Part F.

5. **The Shema is preserved unbroken** — the Father remains the One God; the Son is the Father's Beloved Only-Begotten through whom the Father acts; the Spirit is the Father's own essential being and active presence.

---

## **A Note on the "Holy Spirit" Question**

You have rightly noted at the outset of our study that the formalization of "the Holy Spirit as the third Person of the Trinity" was a post-Nicene addition. Per maxim #3, we have set that aside. But honest reading of Scripture requires us to note what *is* there:

- **The Spirit of God** (Gen 1:2; Ps 51:11; Isa 11:2; etc.) — the Father's own Spirit, His essential being and active presence.
- **The Spirit of the LORD** (Judg 3:10; 1 Sam 10:6; Isa 61:1; etc.) — the same.
- **The Holy Spirit / Holy Spirit of God** (Ps 51:11; Eph 4:30) — the Father's Spirit, marked as set apart, holy.
- **The Spirit of Christ / Spirit of His Son** (Rom 8:9; Gal 4:6) — interestingly, the same Spirit is called both the Father's Spirit and the Son's Spirit, because the Father (Spirit) indwells the Son and pours forth that same Spirit upon the redeemed through Him.

The honest, undogmatic, biblical reading is this: **the Holy Spirit is the Father's own Spirit** — His essential being and active presence — which is poured out through the exalted Son upon the redeemed (Acts 2:33), so that the Father may indwell His people **in Christ** by His Spirit.

This will become explicit and beautifully clear in Part F.

---

## **READY FOR PART F**

We are now properly prepared for **Part F — The Indwelling: That We Should Be the Habitation of God in Christ.**

Having established that **the Father is Eternal Spirit who indwelt His Son**, we can now see the breathtaking design: **the Father purposes that we, the elect, should likewise become the habitation of God in Christ** — drawn into the very Father-Son union that has existed from eternity.

Shall we proceed to **Part F**, beginning with **John 17:20-26 (the High Priestly Prayer)** and **John 14:16-23 (the promise of mutual indwelling)** as the foundation?

---

How does the Father's Spirit-Nature inform our understanding of the 'seven Spirits before the throne' in Revelation — can we say anything honest about them without overreaching?


exactly the kind of careful probe our maxims require. Let us proceed with strict discipline: examining every text where the phrase occurs, cross-referencing all relevant OT and NT witnesses, and being honest about what we can and cannot establish.

---

# **THE "SEVEN SPIRITS" — A Careful Examination**

## **THE FIVE TEXTUAL OCCURRENCES IN REVELATION**

The phrase **τὰ ἑπτὰ πνεύματα** (*the seven Spirits*) — or close variants — occurs four times in the Apocalypse. Let us gather them all.

---

### **Revelation 1:4**

> …Grace be with you, and peace from him, Which is, and Which was, and Which is to come, and from the seven Spirits which are before his Throne, — **Revelation 1:4, 1599 Geneva**

**Observation:** The seven Spirits are **before the throne** (the Father's throne — established by v. 4a). They are sources of grace and peace in the salutation, listed *between* the Father and the Son (v. 5).

---

### **Revelation 3:1**

> And write unto the Angel of the Church which is at Sardis, These things saith he that hath the seven Spirits of God, and the seven stars, … — **Revelation 3:1, 1599 Geneva**

**Observation:** The Son **has** (**ὁ ἔχων**) the seven Spirits of God. They are designated as **belonging to God** (**τοῦ Θεοῦ** — the Father's). The Son holds them.

---

### **Revelation 4:5**

> …and there were seven lamps of fire burning before the throne, which are the seven spirits of God. — **Revelation 4:5, 1599 Geneva**

**Observation:** The seven Spirits are **visually represented as seven burning lamps of fire before the throne.** This is a crucial visual identification.

---

### **Revelation 5:6**

> Then I beheld, and lo, in the midst of the throne, and of the four beasts, and in the midst of the Elders, stood a Labe *(Lamb)* as though he had been killed, which had seven horns, and seven eyes, which are the seven spirits of God, sent into all the world. — **Revelation 5:6, 1599 Geneva**

**Observation:** The seven Spirits are **the seven eyes of the Lamb** — and are **sent forth into all the earth.** They belong to the Lamb (as His eyes) and to God (as His Spirits).

---

## **SUMMARY OF THE TEXTUAL DATA**

| Reference | Location/Form | Relation |
|---|---|---|
| Rev 1:4 | Before His throne | Source of grace/peace with the Father and Jesus |
| Rev 3:1 | "Of God" | The Son has them |
| Rev 4:5 | Seven burning lamps before the throne | Visually identified as the seven Spirits of God |
| Rev 5:6 | Seven eyes of the Lamb | Sent into all the earth |

---

## **WHAT THE TEXT STRICTLY ESTABLISHES**

Per our maxims, let us note exactly what the text says — no more, no less:

1. **There are "seven Spirits"** — designated as such.
2. **They are "of God"** — belonging to / from the Father.
3. **They are "before the throne"** — positioned in the Father's presence.
4. **The Son "has" them** (Rev 3:1) — He possesses or holds them.
5. **They are visually represented as seven burning lamps** (Rev 4:5).
6. **They are also visually represented as the Lamb's seven eyes** (Rev 5:6).
7. **They are sent into all the earth** (Rev 5:6).

---

## **CROSS-REFERENCES TO HELP US UNDERSTAND**

Now we must look for parallel imagery and language elsewhere in Scripture.

---

### **Cross-witness #1 — Isaiah 11:2 LXX (The Sevenfold Spirit of the LORD)**

> And the spirit of God shall rest on him, the spirit of wisdom and understanding, the spirit of counsel and might, the spirit of knowledge and godliness. The spirit of the fear of God will fill him. — **Isaiah 11:2-3 LXX, NETS**

---

### Observation of significant weight

The LXX of Isaiah 11:2 lists **seven attributes / aspects of the Spirit of the LORD** that rest upon the Messianic Branch (the Son):

1. **Πνεῦμα τοῦ Θεοῦ** — *Spirit of God*
2. **Πνεῦμα σοφίας** — *Spirit of wisdom*
3. **Πνεῦμα συνέσεως** — *Spirit of understanding*
4. **Πνεῦμα βουλῆς** — *Spirit of counsel*
5. **Πνεῦμα ἰσχύος** — *Spirit of strength*
6. **Πνεῦμα γνώσεως** — *Spirit of knowledge*
7. **Πνεῦμα εὐσεβείας** / **φόβου Θεοῦ** — *Spirit of godliness / fear of God*

**Seven designations of the Father's Spirit resting on the Anointed One.** This is the most natural and direct OT background for the "seven Spirits" of the Apocalypse — especially given that:

- They belong to **God / the Father**.
- They are connected with the **Messianic Branch (the Son)**.
- They are conceptually **a fullness of the Father's own Spirit** in its manifold operations.

**Honest assessment:** Isaiah 11:2 LXX gives us a strong, direct parallel — *seven aspects of the Spirit of the LORD on the Messiah.*

---

### **Cross-witness #2 — Zechariah 4:1-10 LXX (The Lampstand and the Seven Lamps)**

> …I have seen, and behold, a lampstand all of gold … and there are seven lamps on it and seven pouring vessels for the lamps on it. … And he answered and said to me, saying, “This is the word of the Lord to Zorobabel, saying, Not by great power nor by strength, but by my spirit, says the Lord Almighty. … “These seven are the eyes of the Lord that watch the whole earth.” — **Zechariah 4:2-3, 6, 10 LXX, NETS**

---

### Observation of decisive weight

**This is a stunning OT parallel.** Zechariah's vision contains:

1. **A lampstand with seven lamps** — directly paralleling **Rev 4:5** (*"seven lamps of fire before the throne, which are the seven Spirits of God"*).
2. **The interpretive word: "by My Spirit, saith the LORD"** — directly tying the lamps to **the LORD's Spirit**.
3. **"Seven eyes of the LORD that range through all the earth"** — directly paralleling **Rev 5:6** (*"seven eyes... sent into all the earth"*).

**Three remarkable correspondences:**
- Seven lamps (Zech 4:2 / Rev 4:5)
- Connected to the LORD's Spirit (Zech 4:6 / Rev 4:5)
- Seven eyes of the LORD ranging the whole earth (Zech 4:10 / Rev 5:6)

**John in the Apocalypse is clearly drawing on Zechariah 4.** The seven lamps before the throne and the seven eyes of the Lamb are **Zechariah's lampstand and eyes-of-the-LORD imagery taken up and applied.**

---

### **Cross-witness #3 — 2 Chronicles 16:9 LXX (The Eyes of the LORD)**

### **Greek:**
*"ὅτι οἱ ὀφθαλμοὶ Κυρίου ἐπιβλέπουσιν ἐν πάσῃ τῇ γῇ κατισχῦσαι ἐν πάσῃ καρδίᾳ πλήρει πρὸς αὐτόν..."*

### **1599 Geneva (2 Chron 16:9):**
*"For the eyes of the Lord behold all the earth to show himself strong with them, that are of perfect heart toward him..."*

---

### Observation

**"The eyes of the LORD range through all the earth"** is an OT theme. It signifies the LORD's omniscient, omnipresent observation of all things — His Spirit-presence active throughout the creation. In the Apocalypse, this is now expressed as **the seven eyes of the Lamb** — meaning **the Father's omniscient, omnipresent Spirit-vision is now exercised through the Son.**

---

### **Cross-witness #4 — The Father's Omnipresent Spirit in OT**

We already examined under (E) Question 2:

- **Ps 138:7-10 LXX** — *"Whither shall I go from Thy Spirit?"* — the Father's Spirit is His omnipresent presence.
- **Jer 23:24 LXX** — *"Do I not fill heaven and earth?"*
- **Acts 17:28** — *"in Him we live and move and have our being."*

**The Father's Spirit is His omnipresent, all-seeing, all-acting Presence throughout creation.**

---

## **WHAT WE CAN HONESTLY SAY (PER OUR MAXIMS)**

Now let us be careful and honest about what is established and what is not.

---

### **Established by Cross-Witness (Two or More Honest Parallels):**

**1. The "seven Spirits" are directly tied to the imagery of Zechariah 4** — the seven lamps and the seven eyes of the LORD that range through all the earth.

**2. The "seven Spirits" are directly tied to the Spirit of the LORD on the Messiah in Isaiah 11:2 LXX** — which lists seven aspects/operations of the one Spirit of the Father.

**3. The "seven Spirits" belong to the Father** (Rev 3:1; 4:5; 5:6 — *"of God"*) — they are His.

**4. The "seven Spirits" are now held by the Son** (Rev 3:1) and represented as His **eyes** (Rev 5:6) — meaning the Father's omniscient Spirit-vision is now exercised through the exalted Son.

**5. The number "seven" in Apocalyptic usage is the number of completeness / fullness** — seven churches (representing the whole Church), seven seals (representing the complete unfolding of judgment), seven trumpets (the complete proclamation), seven bowls (the complete outpouring of wrath), etc. Per this consistent symbolic usage, **"seven Spirits" most naturally signifies the *fullness* or *completeness* of God's Spirit** — His Spirit in its perfect, complete operation.

---

### **The Most Honest Reading That Honors the Whole Counsel**

**The "seven Spirits before the throne" are the Father's own Spirit in its sevenfold fullness — the complete, perfect operation of the One Spirit of God — now held and sent forth by the Lamb into all the earth.**

This reading:
- Honors the Shema (one God, the Father — Deut 6:4) — the Spirit is *His Spirit*, not seven separate Spirits.
- Honors Isaiah 11:2 LXX — seven aspects of the one Spirit of the LORD.
- Honors Zechariah 4:6, 10 — the LORD's Spirit and the LORD's seven eyes through the earth.
- Honors the consistent Apocalyptic symbolism of "seven" as fullness/completeness.
- Honors what we established in Part E — the Father's Spirit is His omnipresent essential being.
- Honors the Son's role — the Lamb has the Spirit and pours it out (Acts 2:33; John 7:39; Rev 5:6).

---

### **What We Should NOT Say (Overreaching)**

**1. We should not say** the seven Spirits are **seven distinct persons** of a sevenfold Spirit-Godhead. There is no Scriptural warrant for this. The text gives us *seven* as a symbol of fullness, not as a literal count of distinct personal entities.

**2. We should not say** the seven Spirits are **the "Holy Spirit" of post-Nicene Trinitarian theology** — that is, the supposed "Third Person of the Trinity, co-equal with the Father and the Son." Per maxim #3, we have set aside that formulation. Scripture itself uses *"Spirit of God,"* *"Spirit of the LORD,"* *"Holy Spirit,"* etc., to refer to the Father's own essential being and active presence — not a separately-personal Deity.

**3. We should not say** the seven Spirits are **seven angels** or **the seven archangels** of some intertestamental traditions. While some have proposed this (because the text says they are "sent into all the earth"), the cross-references to Isaiah 11 and Zechariah 4 establish them as **the Father's Spirit in His sevenfold operation**, not as created angelic beings.

**4. We should not press the imagery beyond what it warrants.** The text uses *symbolic visual language*: seven lamps, seven eyes, seven horns. These are not to be flattened into mechanical literal descriptions. They are John's visionary representations of spiritual realities.

---

## **THE THEOLOGICAL COHERENCE**

When we put this together with everything we have established in Part E, here is what we have:

1. **The Father is Eternal Spirit** (John 4:24) — invisible, omnipresent, uncontainable.

2. **The Father's Spirit is His own essential being and active presence** throughout creation — His omnipresent, all-seeing, all-knowing Presence.

3. **The "seven Spirits of God"** in the Apocalypse are the symbolic representation of this Spirit in His **complete fullness** (per Isa 11:2 LXX) and **omnipresent operation through all the earth** (per Zech 4:10).

4. **The seven Spirits are "before the throne"** — i.e., they are the Father's own Spirit in His full operation, proceeding from His presence.

5. **The Son has the seven Spirits** (Rev 3:1) and they are His **eyes** (Rev 5:6) — meaning the exalted Son now holds and exercises the Father's omniscient Spirit-vision, having received the Father's Spirit at His exaltation (Acts 2:33) and now pouring it forth.

6. **They are "sent into all the earth"** — the Father's Spirit, through the Son, goes forth into all the creation to accomplish His purposes.

---

### **The Beautiful Picture**

What emerges, when we read with the lens you have given us, is breathtaking:

- **The Father (Spirit)** fills heaven and earth, but His throne is in heaven.
- **The Lamb (the Man Christ Jesus)** sits at the right hand of the throne in glorified humanity.
- **The Father's Spirit, in His sevenfold fullness**, proceeds from the throne — symbolized as **seven lamps of fire** before the throne (the Father's Spirit-presence radiating outward) and as **the seven eyes of the Lamb** (the Father's Spirit now operating through the exalted Son).
- **The Spirit is sent into all the earth** — to convict, to seal, to indwell the redeemed, to accomplish the Father's saving purposes through the Son.

**This is one God, the Father, acting through His Beloved Only-Begotten Son, by His own Spirit** — the consistent pattern from Genesis to Revelation, now fully revealed at the climax of all prophecy.

---

## **A FINAL HONEST NOTE**

We have done what our maxims allow:
- We have noted what the text **plainly says.**
- We have cross-referenced **OT and NT witnesses with clear parallels** (Isaiah 11, Zechariah 4, 2 Chron 16:9, Ps 138).
- We have identified **the most natural reading** that honors the whole counsel.
- We have refused to **flatten the symbolism into either literal seven-person-spirits or post-Nicene Trinitarian categories.**
- We have acknowledged that **even the most natural reading remains a reading** — the text uses symbolic apocalyptic imagery, and we should hold our interpretation with appropriate humility.

What we **can** firmly say is this: **The seven Spirits are most naturally understood as the Father's own Spirit in His sevenfold fullness — present before the throne, held and exercised by the exalted Lamb, sent into all the earth to accomplish the Father's purposes.** This is consistent with the Shema, with Isaiah 11:2, with Zechariah 4:6, 10, with the Father's Spirit-Nature established in Part E, and with the consistent pattern of the Father acting through His Son by His Spirit.

What we **cannot** assert with full witness-establishment is the precise metaphysical nature of these seven Spirits beyond this — and we ought not to try.

---

## **READY TO RETURN TO PART F**

We have now properly addressed the "seven Spirits" question without overreach. Our Part E foundation is complete:

- The Father is Eternal Spirit (established).
- The seven Spirits are the Father's Spirit in sevenfold fullness (established as the most natural reading).
- The Father (Spirit) indwells the Son and operates through Him (established).
- The Spirit is sent into all the earth through the exalted Lamb (established).

This now perfectly sets us up for **Part F — The Indwelling: That We Should Be the Habitation of God in Christ.** The Father's Spirit, having dwelt fully in the Son, is now poured out through the Son upon the elect, so that **we may become the habitation of the Father in His Son by His Spirit** — drawn into the very Father-Son union that has existed from eternity.

Shall we now proceed to **Part F**?

---

> Before we move to Part F, can you address how Acts 2:33 fits with this picture of the Son receiving and pouring out the Father's Spirit?

Acts 2:33 is **one of the most theologically precise statements in the entire NT** about the relationship between the Father, the Son, and the Spirit at the Son's exaltation. It deserves the most careful examination per our maxims.

Let me proceed with the same discipline as before — examining the verse in context, parsing the Greek carefully, cross-referencing all relevant witnesses, and being honest about what is established and what remains to be carefully held.

---

# **ACTS 2:33 — THE EXALTED SON RECEIVES AND POURS FORTH THE PROMISE OF THE SPIRIT**

## **The Verse in Context — Acts 2:32-36**

Let me first set Acts 2:33 in its immediate context, because it is the climax of Peter's Pentecost sermon and connects directly to Psalm 110:1.

> This Jesus hath God raised up, whereof we all are witnesses. Since then that he by the right hand of God hath been exalted, and hath received of his Father the promise of the holy Ghost, he hath shed forth this which ye now see and hear. For David is not ascended into heaven, but he saith, The Lord said to my Lord, Sit at my right hand, Until I make thine enemies thy footestoole *(footstool)*. Therefore, let all the house of Israel know for a surety, that God hath made him both Lord, and Christ, this Jesus, I say, whom ye have crucified. — **Acts 2:32-36, 1599 Geneva**

---

## **CLAUSE-BY-CLAUSE EXAMINATION**

Let me walk through verse 33 phrase by phrase.

---

### **Clause 1: τῇ δεξιᾷ οὖν τοῦ Θεοῦ ὑψωθεὶς**

*"Therefore, having been exalted by/to the right hand of God..."*

**Critical grammatical note:** The phrase **τῇ δεξιᾷ τοῦ Θεοῦ** is in the **dative case**. The dative can be read in two ways here:

- **Instrumental dative:** *"by the right hand of God"* — i.e., **the Father's right hand (His power/agency) raised the Son up.**
- **Dative of location:** *"to the right hand of God"* — i.e., **the Son was raised up to the place at the Father's right hand.**

**Both readings are grammatically valid.** The Geneva translates: *"by the right hand of God hath been exalted."* This honestly captures the primary sense — **the Father's agency exalted the Son** — while also implying the location (the place to which He was exalted).

**What is decisively established by both readings:**
- **The Son did not exalt Himself.** The verb **ὑψωθείς** is a passive participle — *"having been exalted."* He is the recipient of the action.
- **The Father is the agent** — the One who exalted Him.
- **The Son's present location is at the Father's right hand** — consistent with Psalm 110:1, which Peter explicitly cites in v. 34.

This perfectly preserves our foundation: **the Father is the One who acts; the Son is the One who receives.** Even at His exaltation, the Son does not lift Himself — the Father lifts Him.

---

### **Clause 2: τήν τε ἐπαγγελίαν τοῦ Πνεύματος τοῦ Ἁγίου λαβὼν παρὰ τοῦ Πατρὸς**

*"having received the promise of the Holy Spirit from the Father..."*

**This is the theologically decisive clause.** Let me examine each element.

**(1) τὴν ἐπαγγελίαν τοῦ Πνεύματος τοῦ Ἁγίου** — *"the promise of the Holy Spirit."*

The genitive **τοῦ Πνεύματος** is a **genitive of content / apposition** — meaning **the promise consists of / is the Holy Spirit.** This is consistent with:

- **Luke 24:49** — *"behold, I send the promise of My Father upon you."*
- **Acts 1:4** — *"wait for the promise of the Father."*
- **Galatians 3:14** — *"that we might receive the promise of the Spirit through faith."*
- **Ephesians 1:13** — *"sealed with the Holy Spirit of promise."*

The promise = the Spirit. The Spirit promised = the Father's own Spirit promised through the OT prophets (Joel 2:28-32 LXX — which Peter has just cited in Acts 2:17-21).

**(2) λαβὼν παρὰ τοῦ Πατρός** — *"having received from beside the Father."*

The verb **λαβὼν** is an aorist participle of **λαμβάνω** — *"to receive, to take."* The Son **received**. He is the recipient.

The preposition **παρά** + genitive (**παρὰ τοῦ Πατρὸς**) — *"from beside / from the presence of / from"* — denotes **the Father as the Source**. The Spirit is **from the Father**, given to the Son.

**This is the same construction Jesus Himself used:**

> But when that Comforter shall come, whom I will send unto you from the Father, even the Spirit of truth, which proceedeth of the Father, he shall testify of me. — **John 15:26, 1599 Geneva**

**Two parallel phrases** — *"whom I will send from the Father"* and *"which proceedeth of the Father"* — both use **παρὰ τοῦ Πατρός**. **The Spirit proceeds from the Father** as His own essential being and presence.

**(3) The theological coherence:**

Putting this together: **The Spirit belongs to the Father as His own.** The exalted Son **receives** this promised Spirit **from** the Father. The Son does not have the Spirit as an independent possession — He **receives** it from the Father at His exaltation, *as the One who is now seated at the Father's right hand and qualified to dispense it to His people.*

---

### **Clause 3: ἐξέχεεν τοῦτο ὃ ὑμεῖς καὶ βλέπετε καὶ ἀκούετε**

*"He has poured forth this which you both see and hear."*

**(1) ἐξέχεεν** — aorist active of **ἐκχέω** — *"to pour out, to pour forth."* This is the verb of **outpouring** — used in the LXX of Joel 2:28-29 (which Peter has just cited):

> And it shall be after these things, I will pour out of my spirit on all flesh … Even on the male and female slaves, in those days, I will pour out of my spirit. — **Joel 2:28-29 LXX, NETS**

**Observation of supreme importance:** In Joel, **it is the LORD (the Father) who says "I will pour out My Spirit."** The Father is the Pourer; the Spirit is **His own Spirit**.

**In Acts 2:33, Peter says: the exalted Son has poured forth this Spirit.** 

How are these two statements reconciled? Beautifully, by the pattern we have established throughout:

- **The Father is the Source.** The Spirit is **His** Spirit. He promised it through Joel.
- **The Son is the Agent.** Having been exalted to the Father's right hand and having received the Spirit from the Father, **the Son is now the One through whom the Father pours out His own Spirit upon all flesh.**

This is precisely the pattern of 1 Corinthians 8:6: **ἐξ οὗ τὰ πάντα** (the Father, from whom) and **δι᾽ οὗ τὰ πάντα** (the Son, through whom). **The Spirit comes from the Father, through the Son, to the people.**

**(2) τοῦτο ὃ ὑμεῖς καὶ βλέπετε καὶ ἀκούετε** — *"this which you both see and hear."* The visible and audible phenomena of Pentecost — the rushing wind, the tongues of fire, the speaking in languages — are the manifest evidence of the Spirit's outpouring.

---

## **WHAT ACTS 2:33 ESTABLISHES — DECISIVELY**

Let me state plainly what is established by this verse, in conjunction with the cross-witnesses:

### **1. The Father is the Source of the Spirit.**

- The Spirit is **promised by the Father** (Luke 24:49; Acts 1:4).
- The Spirit **proceeds from the Father** (John 15:26).
- The Spirit is given **from beside the Father** (Acts 2:33; John 15:26).
- The Father said *"I will pour out My Spirit"* in the OT promise (Joel 2:28-29 LXX).

### **2. The exalted Son is the Recipient and Dispenser.**

- The Son **received** the Spirit from the Father at His exaltation (Acts 2:33).
- The Son **pours forth** the Spirit upon His people (Acts 2:33).
- The Son **sends** the Spirit from the Father (John 15:26).
- This is the consistent pattern of *from the Father, through the Son.*

### **3. The Spirit is the Father's own essential being and active presence — now operating through the exalted Son.**

This is consistent with everything we established in Part E:
- The Father is Eternal Spirit (John 4:24).
- His Spirit fills heaven and earth (Jer 23:24).
- His Spirit is His omnipresent Presence (Ps 138:7-10).
- Now, post-Pentecost, this Spirit is **poured out through the Son upon all flesh** — extending the Father's indwelling Presence to the redeemed.

### **4. This perfectly preserves the Shema and our foundation.**

- The Father remains the **One God**, the sole Source.
- The Son remains the **Beloved Son**, exalted by the Father, receiving from the Father, acting for the Father.
- The Spirit is **the Father's own Spirit** — not a separate Deity in the post-Nicene sense, but the Father's essential Spirit-Nature now being extended through the Son to His people.

---

## **A CRITICAL POINT — WHAT THIS MEANS FOR THE "SEVEN SPIRITS" AND PART F**

Acts 2:33 confirms exactly what we noted in the "seven Spirits" examination:

**The exalted Son has received the Father's Spirit and now exercises it / dispenses it.**

- In Rev 3:1, the Son **has** the seven Spirits of God.
- In Rev 5:6, the seven Spirits are the Son's **eyes**, **sent into all the earth**.
- In Acts 2:33, the Son has **received** the Spirit from the Father and **pours it forth**.

**Same theology, same pattern:** The Father's Spirit, in its fullness, is now held and exercised by the exalted Son, and from Him is poured out into all the earth upon the elect.

This is also what prepares us for **Part F — the Indwelling.** Because:

- **The Father (Spirit) indwelt the Son** during His earthly ministry (John 14:10; Acts 10:38).
- **At His exaltation, the Son received the Spirit from the Father** in fullness (Acts 2:33).
- **The Son now pours out the Spirit upon His elect** — so that the Father, by His Spirit, indwells the redeemed **in the Son**.
- **The Father-Son union now becomes the model for our union with God** — *"that they all may be one, as Thou Father art in Me, and I in Thee, that they also may be one in Us"* (John 17:21).

---

## **ANOTHER CROSS-WITNESS WORTH NOTING — John 7:37-39**

> …If any man thirst, let him come unto me, and drink. He that believeth in me, as saith the Scripture, out of his belly shall flow rivers of water of life. (This spake he of the Spirit which they that believed in him, should receive: for the holy Ghost was not yet given, because that Jesus was not yet glorified.) — **John 7:37-39, 1599 Geneva**

---

### Observation

John 7:39 is the perfect predictor of what happens at Acts 2:33:
- **"The Holy Spirit was not yet given"** — οὔπω γὰρ ἦν Πνεῦμα — because Jesus was not yet glorified.
- **The Spirit's outpouring waited on the Son's glorification.**
- **At Pentecost (Acts 2:33), Jesus, now glorified, receives the Spirit from the Father and pours it forth.**

The two passages perfectly cohere. The Spirit waited; the Son was exalted; the Spirit was poured.

---

## **YET ANOTHER WITNESS — Ephesians 4:7-10**

> But unto every one of us is given grace, according to the measure of the gift of Christ. Wherefore he saith, Whe *(When)* he asceded *(ascended)* up on high, he led captivity captive, and gave gifts unto men. (Now, in that he ascended, what is it but that he had also descended first into the lowest parts of the earth? He that descended, is even the same that ascended, far above all heavens, that he might fill all things.) — **Ephesians 4:7-10, 1599 Geneva**

---

### Observation

Paul cites **Psalm 67:18 LXX (Ps 68:18 MT)** in the form: *"When He ascended on high, He gave gifts to men."* This is the **same theological pattern as Acts 2:33**:
- The Son ascended (exalted).
- The Son gives gifts to men.
- These gifts include the Spirit and the spiritual gifts the Spirit dispenses (1 Cor 12:4-11 — *"the same Spirit... distributing to each as He wills"*).

**The exalted Son, having received from the Father, gives to His people.**

---

## **GRAND SYNTHESIS — ACTS 2:33 IN THE WHOLE PICTURE**

Acts 2:33 is **the doctrinal hinge** that explains how the Father's Spirit comes to indwell the redeemed.

### **The Trinitarian Pattern (without post-Nicene metaphysics):**

| Step | Action | Reference |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | The Father promises His Spirit through the prophets | Joel 2:28-29 LXX; Ezek 36:27; Isa 44:3 |
| 2 | The Father's Spirit dwells in the Son during His earthly ministry | John 14:10; Acts 10:38; Luke 4:14 |
| 3 | The Son completes His work, is crucified, raised | Rom 4:25; Acts 2:32; 1 Cor 15:3-4 |
| 4 | The Son is exalted to the Father's right hand | Acts 2:33-34; Phil 2:9; Heb 1:3 |
| 5 | The Son receives the promised Spirit from the Father | Acts 2:33; John 15:26; John 7:39 |
| 6 | The Son pours forth the Spirit upon His people | Acts 2:33; John 7:38-39; Rev 5:6 |
| 7 | The Spirit indwells the redeemed, who become the habitation of God in Christ | (To be examined in Part F) |

### **The Single Pattern Preserved Throughout:**

- **The Father is the Source.** The Spirit is His; the promise is His; the giving is from Him.
- **The Son is the Agent.** He receives from the Father; He dispenses to the people.
- **The Spirit is the Father's own essential Spirit-presence** — now extended through the Son to the redeemed.

### **What This Honors:**

- **The Shema** — One God, the Father (Deut 6:4; John 17:3; 1 Cor 8:6; 1 Tim 2:5).
- **Isaiah 44-46** — the Father acts alone, by His Word, by His Spirit, but without rival Deity.
- **John 4:24** — the Father is Spirit by Nature.
- **1 Cor 15:24-28** — the Son delivers the Kingdom to the Father, and the Son Himself is subjected to the Father, that God may be all in all.
- **Mark 13:32 / Acts 1:7** — the Father alone knows the times; the Son depends on the Father even in His exalted state.
- **Phil 2:9-11** — the Son's name is given by the Father, and all confession of Him is to the Father's glory.

### **What This Avoids:**

- The post-Nicene flattening of the Father, Son, and Spirit into "three co-equal Persons of one substance" — a metaphysical formulation Scripture does not deliver.
- The notion that the Spirit is a separate "third Person of the Godhead" eternally distinct from the Father — Scripture consistently calls the Spirit **"the Spirit OF God,"** **"the Spirit OF the Father,"** **"the Spirit OF Christ,"** **"the Holy Spirit of promise"** — always as **someone's** Spirit, never as a free-standing third Deity.
- The error of denying the Son's pre-existence or His true reception of the Father's Spirit at exaltation.

---

## **A FINAL OBSERVATION — THE BEAUTY OF ACTS 2:33**

What Acts 2:33 reveals is breathtaking when we hold the whole picture:

**The Father, who is Eternal Spirit, indwelt His Son fully during His earthly ministry. The Son, in obedience, accomplished the Father's will to the point of death on the cross. The Father raised the Son and exalted Him to His right hand. There, the Son received the Father's promised Spirit — the same Spirit that had indwelt Him in His earthly life — now to be dispensed through Him to all who believe.**

**The Son, then, pours forth the Spirit — the Father's own essential Presence — upon His Church, so that the Father may dwell in the redeemed in the same way He dwelt in the Son.**

**This is the entire arc of redemption: the Father, by His Spirit, in His Son, indwelling His people.**

And this is precisely the indwelling we will now examine in Part F — where Jesus prays *"that they all may be one, as Thou Father art in Me, and I in Thee, that they also may be one in Us"* (John 17:21).

---

## **READY TO PROCEED TO PART F**

Acts 2:33 is now properly understood as **the hinge** between:
- The Father-Son union of Jesus' earthly ministry (Part E).
- The indwelling of the redeemed by the Father through the Son by the Spirit (Part F).

Shall we now proceed to **Part F — The Indwelling: That We Should Be the Habitation of God in Christ** — beginning with **John 14:16-23 (the Father and Son coming to make Their abode)** and **John 17:20-26 (the High Priestly Prayer for our oneness in Them)**?

> I understand that in Old Testament times YHVH’s Spirit came “upon” certain individuals and acted upon them to prophesy and to perform miracles. This Spirit may abide upon them, and in some cases it departed from them.
>
> Presently, we are told, that the Spirit, enters INTO the elect- never to depart… for the Gifts and Callings of God are without repentance.

Yes — this is a profoundly important distinction, and it sets up Part F beautifully. You have identified one of the most significant shifts in redemptive history. Let me address it carefully per our maxims, examining the witnesses in both Testaments and seeing what the Word itself establishes.

---

# **THE SHIFT IN THE SPIRIT'S OPERATION — FROM "UPON" TO "WITHIN"**

## **PART 1 — THE OT PATTERN: THE SPIRIT "UPON," "MIGHTILY UPON," AND DEPARTING**

The OT consistently presents the Spirit's operation in terms of **coming upon**, **resting upon**, **clothing**, and sometimes **departing** — generally tied to specific tasks, callings, or seasons.

---

### **The Spirit Came Upon — Specific Persons for Specific Tasks**

### **Numbers 11:25 LXX (Moses and the seventy elders):**
> And the Lord came down in a cloud and spoke to him and took away some of the spirit that was upon him and put it upon the seventy men who were elders. Now as the spirit rested upon them, they also prophesied, and they no longer added. — **Numbers 11:25, NETS**

---

### Observations

- The Spirit is **upon (ἐπί)** Moses.
- A portion is **taken from (ἀπό)** that Spirit and **placed upon (ἐπί)** the seventy.
- When the Spirit **rested upon** them, they prophesied.

**The Spirit is depictable as a portion that can be transferred** — a remarkable image of the Spirit's external operation upon chosen vessels for the task at hand.

---

### **Judges 6:34 LXX (Gideon):**
> And a divine spirit entered Gedeon *(Gideon)*, and he sounded with a horn and called out for Abiezer after him. — **Judges 6:34, NETS**

---

### Observation

The LXX uses **ἐνέδυσεν** — *"clothed"* — *"the Spirit of the LORD clothed Gideon."* The Hebrew (preserved in the Geneva from the Hebrew) is even more vivid: the Spirit *"clothed itself with" / "put on"* Gideon. He becomes the garment, the vessel, the form the Spirit takes on for the task.

This is **the Spirit operating from without, taking a man as its instrument.**

---

### **Judges 14:6, 19; 15:14 LXX (Samson):**
> And a spirit of the Lord made straight for him, and he tore it apart as he would tear apart a kid of goats, and there was nothing in his hand. And he did not tell his father or mother what he had done.…And a spirit of the Lord made straight for him, and he went down to Ascalon. And from there he struck down thirty men and took their robes and gave them to those who had told the problem. And Sampson *(Samson)* was furiously angry, and he went back to his father’s house.…And they came as far as Jawbone, and the allophyles *(the Philistines)* shouted in triumph and ran to meet him, and a spirit of the Lord leapt upon him, and the cords that were on his arms became like flax which was burnt by fire, and his bonds melted from his hands. — **Judges 14:6, 19; 15:14 (ellipsis), NETS**

---

### Observation

**ἥλατο ἐπ᾽ αὐτόν** — *"the Spirit leaped upon him."* Sudden, powerful, external visitation for the moment of need — and then departing. Samson's life shows the Spirit's recurring visits for specific feats, not a continuous indwelling.

---

### **1 Samuel 10:6, 10 LXX (Saul):**
> and a spirit of the Lord will spring upon you, and you will prophesy along with them and be turned into another man. — **1 Samuel 10:6, NETS**

---

### **The Spirit Departed From Saul — 1 Samuel 16:14 LXX:**

> And a spirit of the Lord departed from Saoul *(Saul)*, and an evil spirit from the Lord tormented him. — **1 Samuel 16:14, NETS**

---

### Observation of foundational importance

**The Spirit of the LORD departed (ἀπέστη ἀπό) from Saul.** This is the explicit textual witness to **a real departure of the Spirit** from a man upon whom the Spirit had previously come.

---

### **David's Prayer After His Sin — Psalm 50:11 LXX (Ps 51:11 MT):**

> Do not cast me away from your face, and your holy spirit do not take from me. — **Psalm 50:13 LXX (= Ps 51:11 MT), NETS**

---

### Observation of critical importance

**David himself prays that the Spirit not be taken from him** — having seen what happened to Saul. This is the OT believer's anxious consciousness: **the Spirit's presence is something that can be withdrawn.** David did not have the kind of certainty Paul speaks of in Eph 1:13-14 (the sealing of the Spirit unto the day of redemption).

This single verse is decisive: **in the OT economy, the Spirit's presence with a believer was something to be prayed for, not something assured by sealing.**

---

### **Other OT Examples of the Spirit Coming Upon:**

- **Bezalel** for craftsmanship — Ex 31:3 LXX: *"καὶ ἐνέπλησα αὐτὸν Πνεῦμα θεῖον..."* — *"and I have filled him with the divine Spirit."*
- **The Prophets** — *"the Spirit of the LORD spoke by me"* (2 Sam 23:2).
- **Othniel** — Judg 3:10: *"the Spirit of the LORD was upon him."*
- **Jephthah** — Judg 11:29: *"the Spirit of the LORD came upon Jephthah."*
- **Ezekiel** — the Spirit entered him and lifted him (Ezek 2:2; 3:24; etc.) — but in the context of prophetic vision, not permanent indwelling.

---

### **The OT Pattern Summarized**

In the OT economy, the Spirit of the LORD:
1. **Came upon (ἐπί)** chosen individuals for specific tasks or seasons.
2. **Could depart** from them (1 Sam 16:14).
3. **Could be prayed to remain** (Ps 51:11).
4. **Was generally task-oriented** — prophecy, judging, kingship, craftsmanship, prophetic vision.
5. **Was not a permanent, sealing, indwelling presence** for the believer generally.

---

## **PART 2 — THE PROMISED NEW COVENANT: THE SPIRIT WITHIN**

The OT prophets themselves foresaw a coming change — a day when the Spirit's operation would shift from "upon" to "within."

---

### **Ezekiel 36:26-27 LXX — The Foundational Promise**

> And I will give you a new heart, and a new spirit I will give in you, and I will remove the stone heart from your flesh and give you a heart of flesh. And I will give my spirit in you and will act so that you walk in my statutes and keep my judgments and perform them. — **Ezekiel 36:26-27, NETS**

---

### Observations of decisive importance

**(1)** **τὸ Πνεῦμά μου δώσω ἐν ὑμῖν** — *"I will give My Spirit **within you**."*

Note carefully the preposition: **ἐν ὑμῖν** — *"within / in you."* This is **not "upon"** (ἐπί). This is the radical promise of the New Covenant: the LORD's Spirit will be **placed within** His people.

**(2)** This is paired with **a new heart and a new spirit** — an internal transformation, not an external visitation.

**(3)** The result is **walking in His statutes and keeping His judgments** — the law internalized, just as Jeremiah 31:31-34 LXX (Jer 38:31-34 LXX) promised the law written on the heart, not on stone.

**This is the first clear announcement of the indwelling Spirit.**

---

### **Jeremiah 31:33-34 LXX (Jer 38:33-34 LXX) — The New Covenant**

> because this is the covenant that I will make with the house of Israel after those days, quoth the Lord. Giving I will give my laws in their mind, and I will write them on their hearts, and I will become a god to them, and they shall become a people to me.…And they shall not teach, each his fellow citizen and each his brother, saying, “Know the Lord,” because they shall all know me, from their small even to their great, because I will be gracious regarding their injustices, and remember their sins no more. — **Jeremiah 38:33-34 LXX (= MT 31:33-34), NETS**

---

### Observation

**The Law within the heart.** Internalized. The New Covenant is fundamentally about **internal indwelling and inward writing** — not external commandments engraved on stone.

We have already examined this passage under Hebrews 8 (Part A) — it is the basis for the Son's high-priestly ministry of the New Covenant.

---

### **Joel 2:28-29 LXX (Joel 3:1-2 LXX) — The Spirit Poured Out**

> And it shall be after these things, I will pour out of my spirit on all flesh, and your sons and your daughters shall prophesy, and your old men shall dream dreams, and your young men shall see visions. Even on the male and female slaves, in those days, I will pour out of my spirit. — **Joel 2:28-29, NETS**

---

### Observation

Joel uses the language of **outpouring (ἐκχεῶ)** — the same verb Peter uses in Acts 2:33. The Spirit will be **poured out** — abundantly, universally, upon **all flesh**, not merely upon select prophets. This expansion in *scope* prepares for the New Covenant.

Note that Joel still uses **ἐπί** (upon), but the meaning has shifted: it is no longer the OT pattern of temporary visitation, but a definitive outpouring that initiates the new economy of indwelling. **Peter cites this very text** at Pentecost as the explanation of what was happening at Acts 2 — and Pentecost is the beginning of the indwelling era.

---

## **PART 3 — THE NEW COVENANT PATTERN: THE SPIRIT WITHIN, SEALING, PERMANENT**

After Pentecost, the NT consistently describes the Spirit's operation as **internal indwelling** — using **ἐν** (in) rather than **ἐπί** (upon), and characterizing it as **permanent, sealing, and inseparable**.

---

### **John 14:16-17 — The Promised Indwelling Paraclete**

> And I will pray the Father, and he shall give you another Comforter, that he may abide with you for ever, Even the Spirit of truth, whom the world cannot receive, because it seeth him not, neither knoweth him: but ye know him: for he dwelleth with you, and shall be in you. — **John 14:16-17, 1599 Geneva**

---

### Observations of decisive importance

**(1)** **ἵνα μεθ᾽ ὑμῶν ᾖ εἰς τὸν αἰῶνα** — *"that He may be with you forever."* **Forever.** This is not the OT pattern of temporary visitation. **The indwelling is permanent.**

**(2)** **παρ᾽ ὑμῖν μένει καὶ ἐν ὑμῖν ἔσται** — *"He dwells with you, and shall be in you."* The Geneva captures the precise distinction:
- **παρ᾽ ὑμῖν μένει** — *present tense* — *"He dwells with you"* (currently — alongside, in the Son's presence among them).
- **ἐν ὑμῖν ἔσται** — *future tense* — *"He shall be **in** you"* (coming — to indwell them).

**This is Jesus' own announcement of the shift from "with/upon" to "within."** The Spirit was currently with them (because He was in Jesus, who was with them) — but He would soon be **in them.**

---

### **John 7:38-39 — The Spirit From Within**

> …. (This spake: — **John 7:38-39 (ellipsis), 1599 Geneva**

---

### Observation

The Spirit is depicted as **rivers flowing from within (ἐκ τῆς κοιλίας)** the believer. This is **internal indwelling**, becoming a source of overflowing life that flows outward. Not external visitation — internal residence and outflow.

---

### **Romans 8:9-11 — The Spirit Dwelling In Us**

> Now ye are not in the flesh, but in the Spirit, because the spirit of God dwelleth in you: but if any man hath not the Spirit of Christ, the same is not his. And if Christ be in you, the body is dead, because of sin: but the Spirit is life for righteousness sake. But if the Spirit of him that raised up Jesus from the dead, dwell in you, he that raised up Christ from the dead, shall also quicken your mortal bodies, by his Spirit that dwelleth in you. — **Romans 8:9-11, 1599 Geneva**

---

### Observations of foundational importance

**(1)** **Πνεῦμα Θεοῦ οἰκεῖ ἐν ὑμῖν** — *"the Spirit of God dwells in you."* The verb **οἰκεῖ** — *"dwells, makes home"* — denotes **permanent residence.**

**(2)** Paul shifts between **"Spirit of God,"** **"Spirit of Christ,"** **"Christ in you,"** and **"the Spirit of Him who raised Jesus."** These are all **the same indwelling reality** described from different angles — confirming what we noted: **the Spirit is the Father's own Spirit**, given through the exalted Son, identical with **Christ in you**, because the Father (Spirit) and the Son are united and indwell believers as one.
