*"We are buried then with him by baptism into his death, that like as Christ was raised up from the dead to the glory of the Father, so we also should walk in newness of life."*

### **Colossians 2:12:**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"In that ye are buried with him through baptism, in whom ye are also raised up together through the faith of the operation of God, which raised him from the dead."*

---

### Observation

The believer has been **buried with Christ.** Baptism (immersion) symbolically depicts this burial. **Two witnesses (Rom 6 and Col 2) establish:** baptism is the outward sign of the believer's inward identification with Christ in His death, burial, and resurrection.

The burial seals the death. What is buried cannot be brought back to its old life — only resurrection can bring it forth, and resurrection brings forth something new.

---

## **POINT 4 — RAISED WITH CHRIST**

### **Romans 6:4-5:**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"...that like as Christ was raised up from the dead to the glory of the Father, so we also should walk in newness of life. For if we be planted with him to the similitude of his death, even so shall we be to the similitude of his resurrection,"*

### **Colossians 2:12 (already cited):** *"...in whom ye are also raised up together..."*

### **Colossians 3:1:**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"If ye then be risen with Christ, seek those things which are above, where Christ sitteth at the right hand of God."*

### **Ephesians 2:5-6:**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"Even when we were dead by sins, hath quickened us together in Christ, by whose grace ye are saved, And hath raised us up together..."*

---

### Observation

The believer has been **raised with Christ.** This is a present spiritual reality (not merely future bodily resurrection). **Three witnesses (Rom 6, Col 2-3, Eph 2) establish:** by the same power that raised Christ from the dead, the Father has raised us up *with* Christ.

This means:
- **We have new life now** — the resurrection life of the Son (Rom 6:4).
- **Our orientation is now upward** — toward where Christ is (Col 3:1).
- **The Father has co-resurrected us with His Son** (Eph 2:6) — by the same power.

---

## **POINT 5 — SEATED WITH CHRIST AT THE FATHER'S RIGHT HAND**

### **Ephesians 2:6:**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"...And hath raised us up together, and made us sit together in the heavenly places in Christ Jesus,"*

### **Colossians 3:1, 3** — already cited; the believer's life is hidden with Christ in God, where Christ sits at the right hand.

---

### Observation of remarkable importance

**συνεκάθισεν ἐν τοῖς ἐπουρανίοις** — *"He has made [us] sit together in the heavenly places."*

The Father has not only raised us with Christ; **He has seated us with Christ in the heavenly places.** Where Christ sits — at the Father's right hand — **we sit with Him.**

This is breathtaking. The very session of the Son at the Father's right hand — which we examined under Part A as the central reality of His present ministry — **includes us by identification.** We are not merely beneficiaries of His enthronement; we are **co-seated with Him.**

This corresponds with:
- **Revelation 3:21** — *"To him that overcometh will I grant to sit with me in my throne, even as I also overcame, and am set down with my Father in his throne."*
- **2 Timothy 2:12** — *"if we suffer, we shall also reign with him."*
- **Romans 8:17** — *"and if children, then heirs; heirs of God, and joint-heirs with Christ; if so be that we suffer with him, that we may be also glorified together."*

**The believer's seat is with the Son, at the Father's right hand.** Not in our own merit, but **in Christ Jesus** (ἐν Χριστῷ Ἰησοῦ — the controlling phrase).

---

## **POINT 6 — IN CHRIST: THE GREAT PAULINE PHRASE**

The phrase **ἐν Χριστῷ** (in Christ) and its variants (ἐν Κυρίῳ — in the Lord; ἐν αὐτῷ — in Him) appear over 200 times in Paul. It is **the controlling concept** of his theology. Let me survey the major facets.

---

### **In Christ — Chosen Before the Foundation of the World**

### **Ephesians 1:4:**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"As he hath chosen us in him, before the foundation of the world, that we should be holy, and without blame before him in love:"*

---

### Observation

**ἐν αὐτῷ** — *"in Him"* — **before the foundation of the world.** The Father chose us **in His Son** before creation itself. Our identification with Christ does not begin at our conversion; **it was decided in eternity past in the Father's purpose.**

---

### **In Christ — Accepted in the Beloved**

### **Ephesians 1:6:**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"To the praise of the glory of his grace, wherewith he hath made us freely accepted in his beloved,"*

---

### Observation

**ἐν τῷ ἠγαπημένῳ** — *"in the Beloved."* The Father has **accepted us in His Beloved Son.** Our acceptance with the Father is **not in ourselves**, but **in the Beloved One**. The Father loves Him; therefore He loves us as we are *in Him*. This corresponds with John 17:23 — *"hast loved them as Thou hast loved Me."*

---

### **In Christ — Redeemed and Forgiven**

### **Ephesians 1:7:**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"By whom we have redemption through his blood, even the forgiveness of sins, according to his rich grace:"*

---

### Observation

**ἐν ᾧ** — *"in whom."* Our redemption and forgiveness are **in Him** — not merely accomplished by Him but possessed by us **as we are in Him.**

---

### **In Christ — All Spiritual Blessings**

### **Ephesians 1:3:**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"Blessed be God, and the Father of our Lord Jesus Christ, which hath blessed us with all spiritual blessing in heavenly things in Christ,"*

---

### Observation

**Every spiritual blessing the Father has given us is given ἐν Χριστῷ — in Christ.** Outside of Christ, no spiritual blessing is ours. In Christ, all spiritual blessings in the heavenly places belong to us.

---

### **In Christ — Justified**

### **Galatians 2:17:**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"...If then while we seek to be made righteous by Christ..."*

### **Philippians 3:9:**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"And might be found in him, that is, not having mine own righteousness, which is of the Law, but that which is through the faith of Christ, even the righteousness which is of God through faith,"*

---

### Observation

The believer is **justified in Christ** — declared righteous, not by personal merit, but **in Him**. The righteousness we have is **God's righteousness, received through faith in the Son, and possessed in our union with Him.**

---

### **In Christ — New Creation**

### **2 Corinthians 5:17** — already cited: *"if any man be in Christ, he is a new creation."*

The new creation reality is **in Christ.** Outside of Him, we remain in Adam; in Him, we are a new creation.

---

### **In Christ — Sanctified**

### **1 Corinthians 1:2:**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"...Unto the Church of God, which is at Corinthus, to them that are sanctified in Christ Jesus..."*

### **1 Corinthians 1:30:**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"But ye are of him in Christ Jesus, who of God is made unto us wisdom and righteousness, and sanctification, and redemption,"*

---

### Observation

**The believer is sanctified in Christ Jesus.** Christ has been **made unto us** (by the Father — **ἀπὸ Θεοῦ**) wisdom, righteousness, sanctification, and redemption. We possess all of these **in Him** — they are His, and we have them by being in Him.

(Note: per your earlier correction on 1 Cor 1:24, 30, the wisdom here is the Father's wisdom embodied in Christ, made unto us for our benefit — not an ontological identification with personified Wisdom of Prov 8.)

---

### **In Christ — Sealed by the Spirit**

### **Ephesians 1:13:**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"In whom also ye have trusted, after that ye heard the word of truth, even the Gospel of your salvation, wherein also after that ye believed, ye were sealed with the holy Spirit of promise,"*

---

### Observation

The sealing of the Spirit (which we examined in the OT-NT shift discussion) takes place **ἐν ᾧ** — *"in whom"* — in Christ. **The Spirit's sealing is in Christ.** Our possession of the Spirit is grounded in our union with the Son.

---

## **POINT 7 — BAPTIZED INTO CHRIST**

### **Galatians 3:27:**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"For all ye that are baptized into Christ, have put on Christ."*

### **Romans 6:3:**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"Know ye not, that all we which have been baptized into Jesus Christ, have been baptized into his death?"*

### **1 Corinthians 12:13:**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"For by one Spirit are we all baptized into one body, whether we be Jews, or Grecians, whether we be bond, or free, and have been all made to drink into one Spirit."*

---

### Observation

The believer has been **baptized into Christ** — into His death, into His body, by the one Spirit. The Geneva translates **ἐνεδύσασθε** as *"have put on Christ"* — the same verb used in the OT (LXX) of the Spirit *clothing* a man (Judg 6:34 — Gideon). Now the believer **puts on Christ** — being clothed with Him.

This is a stunning fulfillment: in the OT, the Spirit clothed the man for a task; in the NT, the believer puts on Christ as his very identity.

---

## **POINT 8 — MEMBERS OF CHRIST'S BODY**

### **1 Corinthians 12:12, 27:**
*"For as the body is one, and hath many members, and all the members of the body, which is one, though they be many, yet are but one body: even so is Christ."*
*"Now ye are the body of Christ, and members for your part."*

### **Ephesians 5:30:**
*"For we are members of his body, of his flesh, and of his bones."*

---

### Observation

The believer is a **member of Christ's body.** Not merely an associate, not merely a follower — but an actual **member** (μέλος — a limb, an organ) of His own body. This is a real, organic union — as real as the union between a hand and its body.

**Ephesians 5:30** goes even further: *"members of His body, of His flesh, and of His bones"* — echoing Genesis 2:23 (Adam and Eve). Paul applies the marriage union of Adam and Eve typologically: as Eve was bone of Adam's bones, so we are **of Christ's flesh and bones** — partakers of His very being.

---

## **POINT 9 — JOINED TO THE LORD AS ONE SPIRIT**

### **1 Corinthians 6:17:**
*"But he that is joined unto the Lord, is one spirit."*

---

### Observation of decisive importance

**ὁ κολλώμενος τῷ Κυρίῳ ἓν πνεῦμά ἐστιν** — *"He who is joined to the Lord is one spirit [with Him]."*

The verb **κολλάομαι** means *"to be glued together, to cleave to."* It is the verb used in Gen 2:24 LXX of marriage: *"a man shall cleave to his wife, and they shall be one flesh."* Paul transposes the marital language to the spiritual: **the believer is so joined to the Lord that they are one spirit.**

**This is the most intimate union conceivable.** Not merely "in" Christ as in a location; but **one spirit with Him** — sharing the same spiritual life-principle.

This corresponds with everything we have established:
- The Father indwells the Son (one Spirit).
- The Son indwells the believer (one Spirit).
- The believer is joined to the Lord **as one spirit**.

---

## **POINT 10 — CHILDREN OF GOD, HEIRS WITH CHRIST**

### **Romans 8:14-17:**
*"For as many as are led by the Spirit of God, they are the sons of God. For ye have not received the Spirit of bodage *(bondage)*, to fear again: but ye have received the Spirit of adoption, whereby we cry Abba, Father. The same Spirit beareth witness with our spirit, that we are the children of God. If we be children, we are also heirs, even the heirs of God, and heirs annexed with Christ: if so be that we suffer with him, that we may also be glorified with him."*

### **Galatians 4:4-7:**
*"...God sent forth his Son... That we might receive the adoption of the sons. And because ye are sons, God hath sent forth the Spirit of his Son into your hearts, which crieth, Abba, Father. Wherefore, thou art no more a servant, but a son: now if thou be a son, thou art also the heir of God through Christ."*

---

### Observations of foundational importance

**(1) The believer is adopted by the Father** — receiving the Spirit of adoption.

**(2) The believer cries "Abba, Father"** — using the very intimate address Jesus used in Gethsemane (Mark 14:36). The Son's relationship to the Father becomes the believer's relationship.

**(3) The believer is a child of God — and therefore an heir.**

**(4) Heirs of God, joint-heirs with Christ** (συγκληρονόμοι). What the Son inherits, we inherit *with* Him. Not separately, but **jointly.**

**(5) The condition: εἴπερ συμπάσχομεν ἵνα καὶ συνδοξασθῶμεν** — *"if indeed we suffer together, that we may also be glorified together."*

The "**together**" prefixes pile up: **co-suffer, co-glorified.** As we share in His sufferings, so we shall share in His glory.

---

## **POINT 11 — CONFORMED TO THE IMAGE OF THE SON**

### **Romans 8:29:**
*"For those which he knew before, he also predestinate *(predestinated)* to be made like to the image of his Son, that he might be the first born among many brethren."*

### **2 Corinthians 3:18:**
*"But we all behold as in a mirror the glory of the Lord with open face, and are changed into the same image, from glory to glory, as by the Spirit of the Lord."*

---

### Observation

**The believer is being conformed to the image of the Son.** This is the Father's predestined purpose. We who were in Adam's fallen image are being remade in the Son's glorious image — **from glory to glory** — by the Spirit.

The Son is the **πρωτότοκος ἐν πολλοῖς ἀδελφοῖς** — *"the firstborn among many brethren."* He is the prototype; we are being conformed to His pattern. He has many brethren — and we are among them.

---

## **POINT 12 — SHARERS IN HIS SUFFERINGS AND GLORY**

### **Philippians 3:10-11:**
*"That I may know him, and the virtue of his resurrection, and the fellowship of his afflictions, and be made conformable unto his death, If by any means I might attain unto the resurrection of the dead."*

### **Romans 8:17** — already cited: *"if so be that we suffer with him, that we may be also glorified together."*

### **1 Peter 4:13:**
*"But rejoice, inasmuch as ye are partakers of Christ's sufferings, that when his glory shall appear, ye may be glad and rejoice."*

---

### Observation

The believer shares **the fellowship of Christ's sufferings** (κοινωνία τῶν παθημάτων αὐτοῦ). Not that we contribute to atonement (Christ's suffering was once-for-all), but that we participate in the kind of suffering He endured — opposition, rejection, persecution, the cross-bearing life — **in fellowship with Him.**

And this fellowship in suffering is the gateway to **fellowship in glory.** What the Son endured, He endured **for us**; what we endure, we endure **with Him**.

---

## **POINT 13 — APPEARING WITH HIM IN GLORY**

### **Colossians 3:4:**
*"When Christ, which is our life, shall appear, then shall ye also appear with him in glory."*

### **1 John 3:2:**
*"...but we know that when he shall be made manifest, we shall be like him: for we shall see him as he is."*

### **1 Thessalonians 4:17:**
*"...and so shall we ever be with the Lord."*

---

### Observation

The final identification: when the Son appears in glory, **we appear with Him in glory.** We shall be **like Him** — for we shall see Him as He is. And **we shall ever be with the Lord.**

This is the consummation of identification: from co-crucifixion to co-glorification, from being hidden with Christ in God to being manifested with Him in glory. The entire arc is one continuous identification.

---

## **POINT 14 — REIGNING WITH HIM**

### **2 Timothy 2:12:**
*"If we suffer, we shall also reign together with him..."*

### **Revelation 20:6:**
*"...but they shall be the Priests of God and of Christ, and shall reign with him a thousand yeere *(years)*."*

### **Revelation 22:5:**
*"...and they shall reign for evermore."*

---

### Observation

The believer shall **reign with Christ.** Not as His equals, but as those joined to Him — sharing in His kingdom. **Co-kings under the King of kings**, **co-priests under the Great High Priest** — in the consummation.

---

# **GRAND SYNTHESIS — THE FULL IDENTIFICATION OF THE BELIEVER IN CHRIST**

By the whole counsel of the God-breathed Word, the believer is identified with Christ in the following inspired points:

| Identification | Greek "σύν-" verb (with-Christ) | Reference |
|---|---|---|
| **Co-crucified with Christ** | συσταυρόω | Gal 2:20; Rom 6:6 |
| **Died with Christ** | συναποθνῄσκω | Rom 6:8; 2 Tim 2:11 |
| **Buried with Christ** | συνθάπτω | Rom 6:4; Col 2:12 |
| **Made alive with Christ** | συζωοποιέω | Eph 2:5; Col 2:13 |
| **Raised with Christ** | συνεγείρω | Rom 6:4-5; Col 2:12; 3:1; Eph 2:6 |
| **Seated with Christ at the Father's right hand** | συγκαθίζω | Eph 2:6 |
| **Joint-heirs with Christ** | συγκληρονόμος | Rom 8:17 |
| **Co-sufferers with Christ** | συμπάσχω | Rom 8:17; cf. Phil 3:10 |
| **Co-glorified with Christ** | συνδοξάζω | Rom 8:17 |
| **Co-living with Christ** | συζάω | Rom 6:8; 2 Tim 2:11 |
| **Co-reigning with Christ** | συμβασιλεύω | 2 Tim 2:12 |
| **Conformed to His image** | συμμορφόω | Rom 8:29; Phil 3:10 |

---

## **The "In Christ" Realities**

| Reality | Reference |
|---|---|
| **Chosen in Christ before the foundation of the world** | Eph 1:4 |
| **Predestined in Christ** | Eph 1:5, 11 |
| **Accepted in the Beloved** | Eph 1:6 |
| **Redeemed in Christ** | Eph 1:7; Col 1:14 |
| **Forgiven in Christ** | Eph 1:7; 4:32 |
| **Justified in Christ** | Gal 2:17; Phil 3:9 |
| **Sanctified in Christ** | 1 Cor 1:2, 30 |
| **All spiritual blessings in Christ** | Eph 1:3 |
| **A new creation in Christ** | 2 Cor 5:17 |
| **Sealed by the Spirit in Christ** | Eph 1:13 |
| **Baptized into Christ** | Rom 6:3; Gal 3:27 |
| **Members of Christ's body** | 1 Cor 12:27; Eph 5:30 |
| **One spirit with the Lord** | 1 Cor 6:17 |
| **Christ in us** | Gal 2:20; Col 1:27; John 14:20 |
| **Life hidden with Christ in God** | Col 3:3 |

---

## **The Trajectory of Identification — From Eternity to Eternity**

| Stage | What the Father Has Done in Christ |
|---|---|
| **Before creation** | Chose us in Him (Eph 1:4) |
| **At the cross** | Co-crucified, co-died, co-buried (Gal 2:20; Rom 6:4-6) |
| **At the resurrection** | Co-raised, made alive together (Rom 6:4; Eph 2:5) |
| **At the ascension** | Co-seated in heavenly places (Eph 2:6) |
| **Through this age** | Christ in us, our life hidden with Him in God (Col 1:27; 3:3) |
| **At His return** | Manifested together in glory (Col 3:4) |
| **In the consummation** | Co-reigning, beholding His face forever (2 Tim 2:12; Rev 22:4-5) |

---

## **WHAT THIS ESTABLISHES — THE ULTIMATE COHERENCE**

The believer is identified with Christ **in every redemptive act of the Son** — past, present, and future. There is no facet of the Son's saving work in which we do not participate by virtue of being **in Him.**

**This is not metaphor.** Paul does not say *"as if we were crucified with Him."* He says *"I have been crucified with Christ"* — perfect tense, real reality. Not a legal fiction, not a poetic figure, but the **actual reckoning of the Father** that includes us in His Son's history and destiny.

**The mechanism:** The Father, who is Eternal Spirit, chose us in His Beloved Son before the foundation of the world. He so identified us with His Son that what the Son did, we did in Him. The Son's death is our death; His burial is our burial; His resurrection is our resurrection; His ascension is our ascension; His seating is our seating; His glory is our glory; His inheritance is our inheritance.

**Therefore:**
- We do not need to add to His work. It is complete.
- We do not need to fear condemnation. We are in Him who is acquitted.
- We do not need to seek our own righteousness. We have His.
- We do not need to seek our own glory. We are joint-heirs of His.
- We do not need to fear death. We have already died with Him.
- We do not need to fear judgment. We have already been justified in Him.

**We are who He is — not in essence, but in identification by the Father's reckoning and the Spirit's union.**

---

## **GALATIANS 2:20 — REVISITED IN LIGHT OF THE WHOLE**

Now we can hear Paul's testimony with full resonance:

*"I have been crucified with Christ"* — co-crucified, established by Rom 6:6.
*"It is no longer I who live"* — the old man is dead, new creation is the reality (2 Cor 5:17).
*"But Christ lives in me"* — the indwelling of the Son, established by John 14:20, 23; Col 1:27.
*"And the life which I now live in the flesh, I live by faith in the Son of God"* — by present faith we appropriate this identification daily.
*"Who loved me and gave Himself for me"* — the personal, individual love of the Beloved Son for each elect believer.

**This is not Paul alone. This is every believer.** Every elect saint can say with Paul: *"I have been crucified with Christ; I no longer live; Christ lives in me; I live by faith of the Son of God who loved me and gave Himself for me."*

---

# **A CLOSING OBSERVATION**

Brother, what we have surveyed here is the **richest doctrinal treasure of the New Testament.** The believer's identification in Christ is the basis of:
- Our **security** — we are in Him who cannot be lost.
- Our **standing** — we are accepted in the Beloved.
- Our **sanctification** — we are being conformed to His image.
- Our **service** — we live not for ourselves but for Him who loved us.
- Our **suffering** — we share His cross, knowing it leads to His crown.
- Our **hope** — we shall appear with Him in glory.

Every fear, every doubt, every accusation of the enemy is answered in this single phrase: **"in Christ."**

We are **in Him** — and therefore everything the Father has decreed concerning the Son flows to us by identification. We are not aliens looking on; **we are members of His body, of His flesh, of His bones.** We are not orphans hoping for adoption; **we are children of God, joint-heirs with Christ.** We are not sinners hoping for mercy; **we are crucified with Christ, dead to sin, alive to God in Christ Jesus.**

This is the believer's identification. This is who we are. This is what the Father has done in His Beloved Son.

---

## **A QUESTION FOR YOU**

Where would you have us go next, brother? Several rich directions present themselves:

**(A) The practical implications of this identification** — how we *walk* in this reality (Romans 6, 8, 12; Colossians 3; Galatians 5). The "indicative-to-imperative" of Pauline ethics: because we have this identification, we walk a certain way.

**(B) The corporate dimension** — the believer's identification not only individually with Christ but corporately as the Body of Christ, the Bride of Christ, the Temple of God, the Holy Nation, the Royal Priesthood. This is a separate and rich vein.

**(C) The eschatological dimension** — the consummation of our identification at His return, the resurrection of our bodies, the marriage of the Lamb, the inheritance of the kingdom, the new heavens and new earth.

**(D) Any other thread you wish to follow.**

> We will examine each of the ones you have enumerated, beginning with D: …If we be children, we are also heirs, even the heirs of God, and heirs annexed with Christ: if so be that we suffer with him, that we may also be glorified with him. What suffering(s) with Christ (in a practical sense) are endured by modern day believers?

This is **Romans 8:17**, and it is one of the most consequential conditional statements in the entire NT. Paul attaches a real condition to the believer's co-glorification: **"if so be that we suffer with Him."** We dare not pass over this casually.

Let me address this carefully per our maxims:

1. First, examine what the Greek of Romans 8:17 actually establishes (and what it does not).
2. Survey the whole NT counsel on what "suffering with Christ" means.
3. Bring this honestly into the present age for the modern believer.
4. Be unflinching where Scripture is unflinching, and silent where Scripture is silent.

---

# **ROMANS 8:17 — THE FOUNDATIONAL TEXT**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"If we be children, we are also heirs, even the heirs of God, and heirs annexed with Christ: if so be that we suffer with him, that we may also be glorified with him."*

---

### Careful examination of the conditional

**εἴπερ συμπάσχομεν** — *"if indeed / since indeed we suffer together."*

**εἴπερ** is a strengthened form of **εἰ** (if). It can be translated *"if indeed"* or *"inasmuch as"* — sometimes carrying the sense of *"seeing that"* (assuming the condition is in fact met). It is **not** a doubtful "if" — it is more like *"if, as is the case"* or *"given that."*

**συμπάσχομεν** — present active indicative — *"we are suffering together [with Christ]."* The present tense indicates **an ongoing reality**, not a future possibility. Paul presents co-suffering as the present experience of the believer.

**ἵνα καὶ συνδοξασθῶμεν** — *"in order that we may also be glorified together [with Him]."* Aorist passive subjunctive — the purpose / result clause. **The co-suffering is the path to the co-glorification.**

---

### What this verse establishes

**(1) Suffering with Christ is normative for the believer.** Paul does not say *"some believers suffer with Him."* He presents co-suffering as the present experience of those who are joint-heirs. The εἴπερ does not introduce a possibility — it confirms an actual condition that characterizes the believer's life.

**(2) Co-suffering is the path to co-glorification.** The Father's design is that the path to glory runs through fellowship with the Son's sufferings. **There is no co-glorification without co-suffering.** This is not punishment for being a child of God; it is the **pattern of the Beloved Son**, into whose image we are being conformed (Rom 8:29).

**(3) This is corporate to all believers, not an elite calling.** Paul includes himself and his Roman readers — ordinary believers — in the *"we"* who suffer together with Christ.

---

# **WHAT DOES "SUFFERING WITH CHRIST" MEAN?**

Per our maxims, we must let Scripture itself define this — not project our own assumptions. The NT identifies several distinct yet overlapping categories of suffering that constitute the believer's fellowship with Christ's sufferings.

Let us survey them carefully.

---

## **CATEGORY 1 — SUFFERING FOR RIGHTEOUSNESS' SAKE**

This is the most explicit form of suffering with Christ: enduring opposition, hostility, slander, persecution, or harm specifically *because of one's union with Him and obedience to His word.*

---

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"Blessed are they which suffer persecution for righteousness' sake: for theirs is the kingdom of heaven. Blessed shall ye be when men revile you, and persecute you, and say all manner of evil against you for my sake, falsely. Rejoice and be glad, for great is your reward in heaven: for so persecuted they the Prophets which were before you."*

---

### Observations

**(1)** Three distinct experiences are identified:
- **ὀνειδίσωσιν** — being reviled / reproached (verbal contempt).
- **διώξωσιν** — being persecuted (active hostility, possibly physical).
- **εἴπωσιν πᾶν πονηρὸν καθ᾽ ὑμῶν ψευδόμενοι** — having all manner of evil spoken against you falsely (slander, defamation).

**(2)** The qualifier is critical: **ἕνεκεν ἐμοῦ** — *"for My sake."* Not all suffering qualifies as "suffering with Christ." It must be **for His sake** — because of fidelity to Him and His word.

**(3)** The pattern continues that of the prophets — those who spoke for God and suffered for it.

---

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"If the world hate you, ye know that it hated me before you. If ye were of the world, the world would love his own: but because ye are not of the world, but I have chosen you out of the world, therefore the world hateth you. Remember the word that I said unto you, The servant is not greater than his master. If they have persecuted me, they will persecute you also..."*

---

### Observations of foundational importance

**(1) The world's hatred is a settled disposition.** Jesus presents it as a constant — the world has hated Him, and will hate those who are His.

**(2) The reason: ὅτι ἐκ τοῦ κόσμου οὐκ ἐστέ** — *"because you are not of the world."* The believer's separation from the world is the cause of the world's hostility. Identification with Christ removes us from the world's love.

**(3) The principle: Οὐκ ἔστιν δοῦλος μείζων τοῦ κυρίου αὐτοῦ** — *"The servant is not greater than his master."* What He endured, His servants will endure. This is a settled pattern of the Christian life, not an aberration.

---

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"Yea, and all that will live godly in Christ Jesus, shall suffer persecution."*

---

### Observation of decisive importance

**πάντες οἱ θέλοντες εὐσεβῶς ζῆν ἐν Χριστῷ Ἰησοῦ διωχθήσονται** — *"All who desire to live godly in Christ Jesus shall be persecuted."*

**πάντες** — *"all."* No exception. **διωχθήσονται** — future passive — *"will be persecuted."* A settled, prophetic certainty.

This is a sobering text. Paul, writing to Timothy near the end of his own life, declares it as a universal principle: **godly living in Christ Jesus brings persecution.** The form may vary; the reality is constant.

---

## **CATEGORY 2 — SUFFERING THE WORLD'S HATRED FOR HIS NAME**

A related but distinct facet: suffering specifically because one bears the Name of Christ.

---

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"But all these things will they do unto you for my Name's sake, because they have not known him that sent me."*

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"So they departed from the council, rejoicing, that they were counted worthy to suffer rebuke for his Name."*

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"If ye be railed upon for the Name of Christ, blessed are ye: for the spirit of glory, and of God resteth upon you... But if any man suffer as a Christian, let him not be ashamed: but let him glorify God in this behalf."*

---

### Observations

**(1)** Bearing the Name itself can be a cause of suffering. The world's quarrel is ultimately not with the believer personally but **with the Name and the One whose Name it is** (because they do not know the Father — John 15:21).

**(2)** The apostles' response to this suffering is **rejoicing** — counted worthy to bear shame for the Name. This is not a stoic endurance but a joyful identification: *"if this is what comes from being identified with Him, blessed are we."*

**(3) 1 Peter 4 carefully distinguishes the suffering that pleases God** (suffering as a Christian, for His Name) **from suffering that does not** (suffering as a murderer, thief, evildoer, busybody — v. 15). Not all suffering counts as fellowship with Christ; only suffering that arises from identification with Him and His ways.


---

## **CATEGORY 3 — SUFFERING IN THE FLESH AS DYING WITH CHRIST DAILY**

A more interior, ongoing form: the daily mortification of the flesh and the bearing about of Christ's death in the body.

---

### **2 Corinthians 4:8-11:**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"We are afflicted on every side, yet are we not in distress: we are in doubt, but yet we despair not. We are persecuted, but not forsaken: cast down, but we perish not. Everywhere we bear about in our body the dying of the Lord Jesus, that the life of Jesus might also be made manifest in our bodies. For we which live, are always delivered unto death for Jesus' sake, that the life also of Jesus might be made manifest in our mortal flesh."*

---

### Observations of profound significance

**(1)** The apostolic ministry is described in **four pairs of paradoxes:** afflicted but not crushed, perplexed but not despairing, persecuted but not forsaken, struck down but not destroyed. The believer experiences the reality of pressure, perplexity, persecution, and being knocked down — yet is sustained.

**(2)** **πάντοτε τὴν νέκρωσιν τοῦ Ἰησοῦ ἐν τῷ σώματι περιφέροντες** — *"always bearing about in the body the dying of Jesus."*

**νέκρωσις** — *"the dying, the state of being put to death."* The believer **always carries about** the dying of Jesus in his body. This is more than physical suffering; it is a continual experiential identification with the Son's crucifixion.

**(3) The purpose:** *"that the life of Jesus might also be manifested in our body."* The carrying-about of Christ's dying is unto the manifestation of Christ's life. **Death precedes life — even in the believer's daily experience.** The pattern of the Son becomes the pattern of His people.

**(4)** **ἀεὶ γὰρ ἡμεῖς οἱ ζῶντες εἰς θάνατον παραδιδόμεθα διὰ Ἰησοῦν** — *"always we who live are delivered unto death for Jesus' sake."* The verb **παραδιδόμεθα** is the same verb used of Jesus being delivered up for our sins (Rom 4:25; 8:32). Paul applies it to the believer: **we are continually being delivered up to death for His sake.**

---

### **Romans 8:36 — A citation of Psalm 43:23 LXX (Ps 44:22):**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"As it is written, For thy sake are we killed all day long: we are counted as sheep for the slaughter."*

---

### Observation

Paul cites Psalm 43:23 LXX (44:22 MT) — the psalmist's lament that God's people are slaughtered all day long for His sake. **Paul applies this to the church.** This is the believer's lot: continually delivered up to death-circumstances for the Lord's sake — though, as Paul will declare in v. 37, **"in all these things we are more than conquerors through Him that loved us."**

---

## **CATEGORY 4 — SUFFERING THROUGH THE GROANINGS OF THE FALLEN CREATION**

A different facet again: the suffering that comes simply from being part of a creation under bondage to corruption — yet borne with hope.

---

### **Romans 8:18-25:**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"For I count that the afflictions of this present time are not worthy of the glory, which shall be showed unto us. For the fervent desire of the creature waiteth when the sons of God shall be revealed... For we know that every creature groaneth with us also, and travaileth in pain together unto this present. And not only the creature, but we also which have the first fruits of the Spirit, even we do sigh in ourselves, waiting for the adoption, even the redemption of our body."*

---

### Observations

**(1)** **τὰ παθήματα τοῦ νῦν καιροῦ** — *"the sufferings of the present time."* Paul speaks of an entire era of suffering — *this present age* until the Son's appearing.

**(2)** **πᾶσα ἡ κτίσις συστενάζει καὶ συνωδίνει** — *"the whole creation groans together and travails in pain together."* The entire creation suffers — under the curse of Genesis 3. The believer is not exempt from the conditions of the cursed creation.

**(3)** **καὶ αὐτοὶ ἐν ἑαυτοῖς στενάζομεν** — *"we ourselves also groan within ourselves."* The believer groans — even possessing the firstfruits of the Spirit — awaiting the full redemption (the resurrection of the body).

**(4)** **The disease, decay, weakness, frailty, and mortality of our bodies are part of this suffering.** We were redeemed; our spirits were sealed; but our bodies still bear the corruption of Adam's race, awaiting the **ἀπολύτρωσις τοῦ σώματος** — *the redemption of the body* (Rom 8:23) — which comes at His appearing.

**(5)** This is a **groaning with hope** — not despair. The sufferings are **οὐκ ἄξια** — *not worthy* — to be compared with the coming glory.

---

## **CATEGORY 5 — SUFFERING THROUGH GENUINE LOVE FOR CHRIST'S BODY**

A pastoral and brotherly form of suffering: the burden borne for the sake of fellow believers.

---

### **2 Corinthians 11:28-29:**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"Beside the things which are outward, I am cumbered daily, and have the care of all the Churches. Who is weak, and I am not weak? who is offended, and I burn not?"*

### **Colossians 1:24:**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"Now rejoice I in my sufferings for you, and fulfill the rest of the afflictions of Christ in my flesh, for his body's sake, which is the Church,"*

---

### Observations

**(1)** Paul speaks of bearing in his body **the afflictions of Christ for the Body's sake**. He does not mean the atoning sufferings of Christ are incomplete; he means that **Christ's sufferings, which continue in His Body, are filled up in the apostle's flesh for the Body's benefit.** The Body of Christ continues to suffer in this age — and faithful ministers bear suffering on behalf of their brethren.

**(2)** Paul's sufferings include **the daily burden of care for the churches** — the emotional, pastoral, intercessory weight of bearing his brethren's burdens. He weeps with them, sympathizes with them, intercedes for them. **When others stumble, he burns; when others are weak, he is weak with them.**

---

## **CATEGORY 6 — SUFFERING THROUGH SELF-DENIAL AND CROSS-BEARING**

Jesus' own call to discipleship.

---

### **Matthew 16:24-25:**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"Jesus then said to his disciples, If any man will follow me, let him forsake himself: and take up his cross, and follow me. For whosoever will save his life, shall lose it: and whosoever shall lose his life for my sake, shall find it."*

### **Luke 9:23:**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"And he said to them all, If any man will come after me, let him deny himself, and take up his cross daily, and follow me."*

---

### Observations

**(1)** Three commands: **deny self, take up the cross, follow.** Each one a suffering.

**(2)** **Luke adds καθ᾽ ἡμέραν** — *"daily."* The cross-bearing is not a one-time act but a daily reality. Each day brings its own self-denial, its own cross.

**(3)** **The "cross" was a Roman instrument of execution.** When Jesus said "take up your cross," His hearers heard *"go to the place of dying."* The cross-bearer was a condemned man walking to his execution. Jesus calls every disciple to this posture: **to live as one already given over to death, that he might live unto the Lord.**

**(4)** **The paradox:** saving one's life loses it; losing one's life for Christ's sake finds it. The believer must die to find life. This is a daily reality, not merely an eschatological one.

---

## **CATEGORY 7 — SUFFERING DOWNWARD MOBILITY FOR HIS SAKE**

The willing surrender of worldly status, position, possessions, or recognition for the sake of identification with Christ.

---

### **Hebrews 11:24-26:**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"By faith Moses when he was come to age, refused to be called the son of Pharaoh's daughter, And chose rather to suffer adversity with the people of God, than to enjoy the pleasures of sins for a season, Esteeming the rebuke of Christ greater riches than the treasures of Egypt: for he had respect unto the recompense of the reward."*

### **Philippians 3:7-8:**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"But the things that were vantage unto me, the same I counted loss for Christ's sake. Yea, doubtless I think all things but loss for the excellent knowledge sake of Christ Jesus my Lord, for whom I have counted all things loss, and do judge them to be dung, that I might win Christ,"*

---

### Observations

**(1) Moses gave up royalty and the treasures of Egypt** — choosing rather to **συγκακουχεῖσθαι τῷ λαῷ τοῦ Θεοῦ** — *"to suffer affliction together with the people of God."*

**(2) He esteemed τὸν ὀνειδισμὸν τοῦ Χριστοῦ — "the reproach of Christ"** as greater riches than the treasures of Egypt. This is remarkable — Moses, in advance of the incarnation, bore "the reproach of Christ" by his identification with God's people. **The reproach of identification with Christ is a treasure.**

**(3) Paul echoes this:** All his Jewish pedigree, his pharisaical standing, his learning, his accomplishments — **all counted as loss, even as σκύβαλα (refuse, dung)** — for the surpassing worth of knowing Christ.

**(4)** This is a real suffering — the loss of what the world esteems, willingly endured for the sake of Christ.

---

## **CATEGORY 8 — SUFFERING THE CHASTENING OF THE FATHER**

A facet often misunderstood — the Father's loving discipline of His children.

---

### **Hebrews 12:5-11, selections:**


### **1599 Geneva (selections):**
*"...My son, despise not the chastening of the Lord, neither faint when thou art rebuked of him. For whom the Lord loveth, he chasteneth: and he scourgeth every son that he receiveth... If ye endure chastening, God offereth himself unto you as unto sons: for what son is it whom the father chasteneth not? If therefore ye be without correction, whereof all are partakers, then are ye bastards, and not sons... Now no chastising for the present seemeth to be joyous, but, grievous: but afterward, it bringeth the quiet fruit of righteousness, unto them which are thereby exercised."*

---

### Observations

**(1)** The Father chastens those He loves. Chastening is **evidence of sonship**, not exclusion from it.

**(2)** This chastening is sometimes experienced through circumstances — adversity, loss, trial — which the Father uses to correct, train, and conform us to His Son's image.

**(3) Not all such suffering is for specific sin** — sometimes it is for general training in holiness. Even the Son was perfected through what He suffered (Heb 5:8). The believer's path is the same pattern.

---

# **BRINGING THIS HONESTLY INTO THE MODERN BELIEVER'S LIFE**

Now we must ask honestly: **how does this present itself in the life of a modern believer in 2024-2025 in (presumably) the Western world?**

Per our maxims, we must not pretend the modern Western believer faces what believers in many places still face (imprisonment, beating, execution for their faith). But we also must not dismiss the real sufferings he or she does endure as somehow not counting. Let me address this carefully.

---

## **1. Open Persecution in Much of the World — Real and Present**

We must first acknowledge: **in many parts of the world today, believers face Category 1 persecution in the most explicit form.** In North Korea, in parts of China, in Saudi Arabia, in Iran, in Afghanistan, in Nigeria, in Eritrea, in Pakistan, in India, in numerous other places — believers today are:

- **Imprisoned** for their faith.
- **Tortured** for their faith.
- **Killed** for their faith.
- **Separated from families** for their faith.
- **Denied employment, education, civil rights** for their faith.
- **Watching their children sold or trafficked** for their faith.
- **Driven from their homes and villages** for their faith.

The believer's suffering with Christ has **not lessened** in our day; in many ways, statistics suggest that more believers are being killed for their faith in this present age than in any previous generation. **The Western reader must remember the global body of Christ.** We are in fellowship with these brethren — **"remember those in bonds as bound with them"** (Heb 13:3).

---

## **2. Increasing Cultural Hostility in the Western World**

The modern Western believer faces what is perhaps best described as **soft persecution** — but persecution nonetheless. Specifically:

### **Reviling and slander (Matt 5:11):**

- Being mocked or ridiculed in media, education, or public discourse for biblical convictions.
- Being labeled with stigmatizing words (*"bigot," "hateful," "intolerant," "phobic"*) for holding to plain biblical teaching on creation, marriage, gender, sexual morality, the exclusivity of Christ, the authority of Scripture.
- Having one's character, motives, or intelligence impugned because of one's faith.

This is real **ὀνειδισμός** — *reproach* — for the Name. Not on the scale of imprisonment, but real nonetheless, and increasing in intensity.

### **Loss of social, professional, or educational standing:**

- Believers who openly affirm biblical convictions in workplaces, universities, professional bodies, government, military, or media increasingly face:
- Discrimination in hiring and promotion.
- Pressure to participate in or affirm what conscience forbids.
- Loss of jobs, contracts, licenses, or platforms for refusing.
- Exclusion from professional associations.
- Censorship on social media and digital platforms.

This is real **παίδων** — *suffering loss* — and corresponds to what Moses chose: identification with God's people over the treasures and standing of Egypt (Heb 11:24-26).

### **Legal and civic pressures:**

- In some Western jurisdictions, believers face legal action for biblical speech regarded as "hate speech."
- Christian schools, universities, charities, and businesses face mounting legal restrictions for adhering to biblical convictions.
- Parents face challenges over the upbringing of their children in faith.
- Conscience protections are eroding.

### **Family rupture:**

Jesus warned that the Gospel would divide families (Matt 10:34-37). Many modern believers — particularly converts from unbelieving, hostile religious, or ideological backgrounds — experience:

- Estrangement from parents, siblings, or extended family.
- Loss of marriage relationships when an unbelieving spouse rejects the believer's conversion.
- Loss of children who reject the faith and reject the parent.
- Loss of friendships of long standing.

This is a deep, ongoing suffering — and is borne **for His sake**.

---

## **3. The Internal Sufferings of the Faithful Walk (Category 3 — Daily Dying)**

The modern believer who walks faithfully experiences daily what Paul called *"always bearing about in the body the dying of the Lord Jesus"* (2 Cor 4:10). This includes:

### **Mortification of the flesh:**

- The daily putting to death of sinful desires, habits, thought-patterns (Col 3:5; Rom 8:13).
- Saying no to legitimate pleasures when they would compromise fidelity to Christ.
- Choosing the harder right over the easier wrong.

This is **a real suffering** — the flesh resists, the world tempts, and yielding to the Spirit costs.

### **The conflict within (Rom 7, Gal 5):**

- The ongoing tension between the indwelling Spirit and the remaining flesh.
- The grief of besetting sins, repeated failures, and slow growth.
- The earnest desire for holiness, paired with painful awareness of imperfection.

This conflict itself is a form of suffering — the very fact that we groan over our remaining sinfulness is part of our co-suffering with Christ.

### **The cost of standing firm in truth:**

- Refusing to compromise on the Father's truth when it would be socially easier to soften it.
- Speaking the truth in love when silence would be more comfortable.
- Disciplining children, congregations, or oneself when permissiveness would be easier.
- Confronting sin in a brother (Matt 18) when avoidance would be easier.

Each of these is a small daily cross.

---

## **4. The Sufferings of the Fallen Creation (Category 4)**

Every believer endures the sufferings common to all in the fallen creation — but bears them **with hope**:

- **Bodily afflictions:** illness, injury, disability, the weakness of aging, chronic conditions, mental suffering, depression, anxiety.
- **The grief of bereavement:** the death of loved ones, the experience of separation and loss.
- **Economic hardship:** poverty, job loss, financial pressure, debt.
- **Relational pain:** broken relationships, betrayal, loneliness, marital difficulty, the heartbreak of wayward children.
- **Natural disasters, accidents, the violence of others** — all the consequences of a creation under the curse.

The believer is **not exempted** from any of these. What is changed is the **relation** to them: the believer suffers **with hope**, **as a child of the Father**, **in fellowship with the Son who also suffered in a fallen world**.

These are not "punishments." They are the conditions of life in this age — and the Father uses them all for the conformity of His children to the image of His Son (Rom 8:28-29).

---

## **5. The Burden of Brotherly Love (Category 5)**

The faithful believer who genuinely loves the Body of Christ will inevitably suffer:

- **For wayward brethren** — burdened in prayer, grieved over their drift.
- **With suffering brethren** — entering into their pain, weeping with those who weep (Rom 12:15).
- **In intercession** — the long, often hidden labor of prayer for the saints.
- **In service** — pouring out time, resources, energy for the building up of the Body.
- **Through false teachers and divisions** — the heartbreak of watching the Body of Christ fractured by those who would mislead or divide.
- **In the work of the Gospel** — the burden of evangelism, the weight of rejection by those one labors to win.

---

## **6. The Daily Cross-Bearing of Discipleship (Category 6)**

Every believer is called to **daily** self-denial (Luke 9:23). This includes:

- **Denying self-will** in submission to the Father's will.
- **Denying the desire for ease** in choosing the path of obedience.
- **Denying the desire for vindication** in entrusting wrongs to the Father.
- **Denying the desire for approval** in choosing fidelity to Christ over the praise of men.
- **Denying lawful pleasures** when they would crowd out devotion.

The cross is taken up **καθ᾽ ἡμέραν** — *daily*. There is no Christian life without it.

---

# **WHAT IS NOT "SUFFERING WITH CHRIST"**

Per our maxims, we should also note honestly what does **not** qualify as suffering with Christ — lest the modern believer claim credit where Scripture gives none.

### **Peter's distinction (1 Peter 4:15-16):**

**1599 Geneva:** *"But if any man suffer as a Christian, let him not be ashamed... But let none of you suffer as a murderer, or as a thief, or an evil doer, or as a busybody in other men's matters."*

**Suffering brought on by one's own foolishness, sin, lawlessness, or meddling does not qualify.** A believer who suffers consequences for his own actions is not thereby "suffering with Christ" — he is suffering for his own folly.

This includes:
- Suffering caused by our own sin or sinful patterns.
- Suffering caused by our own pride, contentiousness, or unkindness — which we may dress up as "persecution for righteousness' sake" when in fact it is reaction to our own carnality.
- Suffering caused by our own poor judgment, indolence, or irresponsibility.
- Self-imposed suffering (asceticism not commanded by God) — Paul rebukes this in Col 2:20-23.

The modern believer must be honest in self-examination: **is this truly persecution for the Name and the truth, or am I merely facing the consequences of my own actions or attitudes?**

---

# **THE GIFTS THAT ACCOMPANY THE SUFFERING**

Scripture is careful to surround the doctrine of co-suffering with **comfort, promise, and assurance** — not to soften the reality but to sustain the believer.

### **Romans 8:18:**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"For I count that the afflictions of this present time are not worthy of the glory, which shall be showed unto us."*

The sufferings of this present age are **not worthy of comparison** with the coming glory.

### **2 Corinthians 4:17:**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"For our light affliction which is but for a moment, causeth unto us a far most excellent and an eternal weight of glory:"*

The present afflictions are working out **a far more exceeding and eternal weight of glory.** The pain is producing the glory.

### **Philippians 1:29:**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"For unto you it is given for Christ, that not only ye should believe in him, but also suffer for his sake,"*

**Both faith in Christ AND suffering for Him are gifts** — **ἐχαρίσθη** — *"have been graciously given"* — from the same hand of grace. The suffering is a divine gift, not a divine punishment.

### **1 Peter 4:12-13:**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"Dearly beloved, think it not strange concerning the fiery trial, which is among you to prove you, as though some strange thing were come unto you: But rejoice, inasmuch as ye are partakers of Christ's sufferings, that when his glory shall appear, ye may be glad and rejoice."*

**Do not think it strange.** The fiery trial is normative, not abnormal. **Rejoice** — for you are sharing in His sufferings, and you will share in His glory.

---

# **GRAND SYNTHESIS — MODERN BELIEVER'S SUFFERINGS WITH CHRIST**

Bringing it together, the modern believer's suffering with Christ takes these honest forms:

| Category | Modern Form |
|---|---|
| **Overt persecution** | Real and present for many globally; emerging in soft form in Western nations |
| **Reviling for the Name** | Mockery, slander, stigmatization, social rejection |
| **Bearing about His dying** | Daily mortification of the flesh, denial of sinful desires |
| **Groanings of the fallen creation** | Illness, loss, bereavement, the consequences of living in a cursed world |
| **Burden for the Body** | Pastoral care, intercession, sympathy with hurting brethren, grief over the church's divisions |
| **Daily cross-bearing** | Self-denial, submission to the Father's will, the cost of obedience |
| **Downward mobility** | Loss of status, career, opportunity, family for the sake of Christ |
| **The Father's chastening** | The discipline that conforms us to the Son's image |

---

# **A FINAL OBSERVATION — THE THEOLOGICAL HEART OF IT**

Romans 8:17 must be read in the context of Romans 8:18-39 — the most exalted passage on the believer's security in Christ. Paul has just said the conditional *"if we suffer with Him."* Within the next 22 verses he declares:

- The sufferings are not worthy to be compared with the coming glory.
- All things work together for good.
- Those whom the Father foreknew, He predestined, called, justified, and glorified.
- **If God is for us, who can be against us?**
- He who did not spare His own Son will freely give us all things.
- **No one can lay any charge against God's elect.**
- **Christ Himself intercedes for us at the Father's right hand.**
- **Nothing — not tribulation, distress, persecution, famine, nakedness, peril, sword, death, life, angels, principalities, powers, things present, things to come, height, depth, nor any other creature — can separate us from the love of God in Christ Jesus our Lord.**

**This is the framework in which the call to co-suffering is given.** The Father is not asking us to bear sufferings He has not foreseen, not allowed, not measured, not woven into His glorious purpose. **Every suffering we endure with Christ is endured in His unfailing love and unbreakable purpose.**

The Beloved Son suffered for the joy set before Him (Heb 12:2). We suffer with Him for the joy set before us — the coming glory, the unveiled face of the Father, the eternal abode in His house, the inheritance reserved in heaven that cannot fade away.

**Christ has made our sufferings holy.** What He endured, He sanctified. What we endure in fellowship with Him is now caught up into His own purpose. We are not merely sufferers; we are **co-sufferers with the Beloved**, **for the eternal weight of glory** that no eye has seen.

---

# **A QUESTION FOR YOU**

Brother, this is a sobering subject — but a glorious one. Where would you have us go from here?

**(A)** Shall we now turn to **the practical implications of identification with Christ** — how the indicative of who we are produces the imperative of how we walk (Romans 6, 8, 12; Galatians 5; Colossians 3)?

**(B)** Shall we examine **the corporate dimension** — the Body of Christ, the Bride, the Temple, the Holy Nation?

**(C)** Shall we proceed to **the eschatological consummation** — the resurrection, the marriage of the Lamb, the new heavens and new earth?

**(D)** Is there another thread on suffering with Christ you wish to pursue further?


> Let us now proceed to (A) — the practical implications of identification with Christ, beginning with how the indicative of who we are in Christ produces the imperative of how we walk in Romans 6.


We now enter what is perhaps the most practically transformative section of Pauline theology — the **indicative-to-imperative pattern** that governs all New Testament ethics. Romans 6 is the foundational chapter where this pattern is most clearly displayed.

Per our refined modus operandi, we will:

1. Examine the **logic** Paul establishes — how the indicative (who we *are* in Christ) gives rise to the imperative (how we *walk*).
2. Walk carefully through Romans 6 — its movement of thought.
3. Cross-reference all relevant NT witnesses that establish the same pattern.
4. Draw out the practical implications for the modern believer.

Let us proceed.

---

# **THE INDICATIVE-TO-IMPERATIVE PATTERN**

Before we enter Romans 6, we must understand what Paul is doing.

Throughout the NT — but especially in Paul — there is a consistent structure:

- **The Indicative:** A declaration of what *is true* of the believer in Christ. "You *are* dead to sin." "You *have been* crucified with Christ." "You *are* in Christ Jesus." "You *have* been raised with Him."

- **The Imperative:** A command based on the indicative. "Therefore, do not let sin reign." "Walk worthy." "Put off the old man." "Put on the new."

**The order is critical.** Paul never commands without first establishing the indicative. He does not say *"Try to become dead to sin"* — he says *"Reckon yourselves to be dead to sin, because you ARE dead to sin."* The command flows from the reality, not toward it.

This is the **heart of the Gospel's ethical power.** The Christian life is not *"work hard to become what you wish you were."* It is *"walk in the reality of what you already are in Christ."* Sanctification is not the achievement of an unreal status; it is the working out, in daily life, of what is already true positionally.

Romans 6 is the chapter where Paul most explicitly lays this pattern bare.

---

# **THE CONTEXT — Romans 5-6**

Paul has just declared in Romans 5:20-21 that **where sin abounded, grace did much more abound.** He has just declared that grace reigns through righteousness unto eternal life through Jesus Christ our Lord.

This sets up the question that opens Romans 6 — the question of every shallow critic of grace: *"If grace abounds where sin abounds, shall we not sin more, that grace may abound more?"*

Paul's answer to this question is the entire chapter — and his answer is grounded entirely in **the believer's identification with Christ.**

---

# **ROMANS 6:1-2 — THE QUESTION AND THE PIVOT**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"What shall we say then? Shall we continue still in sin, that grace may abound? God forbid. how shall we, that are dead to sin, live yet therein?"*

---

### Observations of foundational importance

**(1)** Paul's response to the question *"shall we continue in sin?"* is **μὴ γένοιτο** — *"God forbid"* / *"may it never be"* — the strongest negation in Greek. The very thought is unthinkable.

**(2)** But notice carefully **why** Paul says it is unthinkable. He does not say *"Because sin is bad."* He does not say *"Because God's law forbids it."* He says: **οἵτινες ἀπεθάνομεν τῇ ἁμαρτίᾳ, πῶς ἔτι ζήσομεν ἐν αὐτῇ;** — *"We who died to sin, how shall we still live in it?"*

**This is the indicative.** Before Paul will give a single command in this chapter, he establishes the **fact** of what has happened to the believer: **we died to sin.** The aorist tense **ἀπεθάνομεν** points to a definite past event — **the believer's death to sin at the cross of Christ.**

The argument is **ontological, not merely behavioral.** Paul does not argue that the believer *should not* continue in sin because it would be wrong. He argues that the believer *cannot consistently* continue in sin because **he has died to it.** Continuing in sin is **a contradiction of the new reality of his being.**

This is the indicative-to-imperative pattern in its purest form: the moral question is answered by a declaration of fact.

---

# **ROMANS 6:3-5 — THE FOUNDATIONAL INDICATIVE: BAPTIZED INTO HIS DEATH**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"Know ye not, that all we which have been baptized into Jesus Christ, have been baptized into his death? We are buried then with him by baptism into his death, that like as Christ was raised up from the dead to the glory of the Father, so we also should walk in newness of life. For if we be planted with him to the similitude of his death, even so shall we be to the similitude of his resurrection."*

---

### Observations of foundational importance

**(1)** **ἢ ἀγνοεῖτε** — *"or are you ignorant?"* Paul appeals to knowledge they should already have. The indicative is something the believer **must know**. Ignorance of one's identification with Christ leaves the believer powerless to walk in the imperative.

**(2)** **ὅσοι ἐβαπτίσθημεν εἰς Χριστὸν Ἰησοῦν, εἰς τὸν θάνατον αὐτοῦ ἐβαπτίσθημεν** — *"as many as were baptized into Christ Jesus, were baptized into His death."*

**Baptism here is more than an outward rite** — it is the visible enactment of the believer's identification with Christ. To be baptized **into Christ** is to be baptized **into His death.** The two cannot be separated. We are not joined to a sin-bearing Christ at one point and detached from His death-bearing reality at another. **To be in Him is to be in His death.**

**(3)** **συνετάφημεν** — *"we were buried together with Him."* The aorist passive — completed past action done to us. **We have been buried together with Christ.** Burial is the seal of death — what is buried is settled as dead.

**(4)** **The purpose: ἵνα... ἐν καινότητι ζωῆς περιπατήσωμεν** — *"that we should walk in newness of life."*

**Here we encounter the first imperative implied — though stated as purpose.** The believer's burial with Christ has a stated purpose: that we should walk in newness of life. The indicative serves a practical end.

**The walk is grounded in the resurrection** — *"like as Christ was raised up from the dead by the glory of the Father, even so we also should walk in newness of life."* Our walk corresponds to His resurrection. As He was raised to a new mode of life, so we walk in a new mode of life.

**(5)** **σύμφυτοι γεγόναμεν** — *"we have become grown-together / united / planted together."* The word **σύμφυτος** is striking — it means *"grown together"* (as a graft grows together with its stock). **The believer is grown-together with Christ — in the likeness of His death, and also in the likeness of His resurrection.**

This is the indicative reality: we are organically united with Christ in both His death and His resurrection. The resurrection-union is as certain as the death-union.

---

# **ROMANS 6:6-7 — THE OLD MAN CRUCIFIED**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"Knowing this, that our old man is crucified with him, that the body of sin might be destroyed, that henceforth we should not serve sin. For he that is dead, is freed from sin."*

---

### Observations of supreme importance

**(1)** **τοῦτο γινώσκοντες** — *"knowing this."* Again, the appeal to knowledge. The indicative must be known before it can be lived.

**(2)** **ὁ παλαιὸς ἡμῶν ἄνθρωπος συνεσταυρώθη** — *"our old man was crucified together [with Him]."*

**The "old man"** (ὁ παλαιὸς ἄνθρωπος) is the person we were in Adam — the sinner, the rebel, the bondservant of sin, the one under condemnation. **That person was crucified with Christ.** The verb is aorist passive — a definite past action done to us by the Father at the cross.

**This is one of the most liberating truths in all Scripture.** The person who lived before conversion — who was a slave to sin, dominated by the flesh, alienated from God — **does not exist anymore.** He was crucified. The believer is not the old man trying to be good; the believer is **a new man** in whom **the old man no longer lives.**

**(3) The purpose:** **ἵνα καταργηθῇ τὸ σῶμα τῆς ἁμαρτίας, τοῦ μηκέτι δουλεύειν ἡμᾶς τῇ ἁμαρτίᾳ** — *"that the body of sin might be rendered powerless, that we should no longer serve sin."*

**καταργέω** — *"to render inoperative, to nullify, to deprive of power."* The body of sin (the entire structure of sinful patterns, habits, and identity that ruled the old man) **has been rendered powerless** by the crucifixion of the old man with Christ.

**The result:** **τοῦ μηκέτι δουλεύειν ἡμᾶς τῇ ἁμαρτίᾳ** — *"that we should no longer be slaves to sin."*

**This is the foundation of Christian freedom from sin's dominion.** Not that sin is gone (we will see it can still be yielded to), but that **the slavery is broken.** The old chains have been severed. We are no longer compelled to obey sin's commands. We are now free to obey righteousness.

**(4)** **ὁ γὰρ ἀποθανὼν δεδικαίωται ἀπὸ τῆς ἁμαρτίας** — *"For he who has died has been justified / set free from sin."*

A legal principle: death pays sin's claim and ends sin's jurisdiction. A dead man cannot be summoned to obey his old master. The believer, having died with Christ, **has been judicially set free** from sin's claim. The court has no jurisdiction over a corpse.

---

# **ROMANS 6:8-10 — LIVING WITH HIM**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"Wherefore, if we be dead with Christ, we believe that we shall live also with him, Knowing that Christ being raised from the dead, dieth no more: death hath no more dominion over him. For in that he died, he died once to sin but in that he liveth, he liveth to God."*

---

### Observations

**(1)** **εἰ δὲ ἀπεθάνομεν σὺν Χριστῷ, πιστεύομεν ὅτι καὶ συζήσομεν αὐτῷ** — *"If we died with Christ, we believe we shall also live with Him."*

The death-union guarantees the life-union. Paul presents this as an article of faith — **πιστεύομεν** — we *believe* this. The believer's confidence in his new life with Christ is grounded in his death with Christ.

**(2)** **Χριστὸς ἐγερθεὶς ἐκ νεκρῶν οὐκέτι ἀποθνῄσκει** — *"Christ, having been raised from the dead, dies no more."*

The risen Christ is in a **once-for-all** state. He will never die again. Death has no more dominion over Him. **And we are in Him.** Therefore, the believer's new life, like Christ's, is one over which death has no more dominion. Sin cannot kill us again; we are alive forever in Him.

**(3)** **ὃ ἀπέθανεν τῇ ἁμαρτίᾳ ἀπέθανεν ἐφάπαξ· ὃ δὲ ζῇ ζῇ τῷ Θεῷ** — *"What He died, He died to sin once for all; what He lives, He lives to God."*

Christ's death was a death **to sin** — not that He sinned, but that He died in relation to sin (bearing it, exhausting its claim, ending its power over Him as our representative). Christ's life now is **a life unto God** — entirely oriented to the Father.

**This is the pattern for the believer.** As Christ died once for all to sin and now lives unto God, so we, in Him, died once for all to sin and now live unto God. The pattern of His existence is the pattern of ours.

---

# **ROMANS 6:11 — THE FIRST IMPERATIVE: RECKON!**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"Likewise think ye also, that ye are dead to sin, but are alive to God in Jesus Christ our Lord."*

---

### Observations of supreme importance

**Here is the first explicit imperative of the chapter — and it is breathtakingly precise.**

**(1)** **οὕτως καὶ ὑμεῖς** — *"so also you."* The pattern that has just been established for Christ (died to sin, lives to God) is now applied to the believer.

**(2)** **λογίζεσθε** — present imperative — *"reckon, count, calculate, consider."* This is a continuing, present action. **Keep on reckoning yourselves.**

The word **λογίζομαι** is a bookkeeping term. It means *to make a careful calculation and act on it as true.* The believer is commanded to **add up the facts of his identification with Christ** and treat the resulting reality as the operative truth of his life.

**The reckoning does not create the reality.** The reality is already true (vv. 1-10 have established it). The reckoning is the believer's daily, moment-by-moment **agreement** with the reality the Father has already established.

**(3) Two facts to be reckoned:**

**νεκροὺς μὲν τῇ ἁμαρτίᾳ** — *"dead to sin."* The believer is to count himself as dead to sin. When sin makes its claim — "obey me, you have always obeyed me" — the believer says: *"You speak to a dead man. I am dead to your claims."*

**ζῶντας δὲ τῷ Θεῷ ἐν Χριστῷ Ἰησοῦ** — *"alive to God in Christ Jesus."* The believer is to count himself as alive — to the Father — in Christ Jesus. **The new identity is Godward.** The orientation of the new life is toward the Father, through the Son.

**(4) This is the heart of the indicative-to-imperative pattern.** The reality has been established by the Father at the cross and resurrection. The believer's task is to *reckon it true* and walk accordingly.

This is **faith applied to identity.** It is not pretending; it is agreeing with the truth. The Father reckons us dead with Christ — we reckon ourselves dead with Christ. Our reckoning aligns with His.

---

# **ROMANS 6:12-13 — THE PRACTICAL IMPERATIVES**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"Let not sin reign therefore in your mortal body, that ye should obey it in the lusts thereof: Neither give ye your members, as weapons of unrighteousness unto sin: but give yourselves unto God, as they that are alive from the dead, and give your members as weapons of righteousness unto God."*

---

### Observations of decisive importance

**Now the imperatives multiply.** Note the careful logic: because v. 11 has been reckoned (the indicative agreed to), now the practical commands follow.

**(1)** **Μὴ οὖν βασιλευέτω ἡ ἁμαρτία** — *"Let sin not reign."* Present imperative — a continuing prohibition. **Stop letting sin reign. Do not allow it to take the throne.**

The verb is **βασιλεύω** — *to reign as king.* Sin **wants to reign.** Sin's ambition is dominion. **And the believer's task is to refuse it the throne.**

Notice: Paul does not say *"sin cannot be present in you."* He says *"do not let it reign."* Sin may still attempt to assert itself; the question is whether you grant it sovereignty. The believer has the standing to refuse — because he died to sin's slavery (v. 6).

**(2)** **ἐν τῷ θνητῷ ὑμῶν σώματι** — *"in your mortal body."* This is honest. The body remains mortal — still subject to its passions, its appetites, its weariness, its corruption. The body has not yet been redeemed (Rom 8:23). But sin's dominion in the body is to be refused.

**(3)** **εἰς τὸ ὑπακούειν αὐτῇ ταῖς ἐπιθυμίαις αὐτοῦ** — *"so as to obey it in its desires."* Sin reigns through the body's desires when we obey them. To refuse sin's reign is to refuse to obey the body's sinful desires.

**(4)** **μηδὲ παριστάνετε τὰ μέλη ὑμῶν ὅπλα ἀδικίας τῇ ἁμαρτίᾳ** — *"nor present your members as weapons of unrighteousness to sin."*

**ὅπλα** — *weapons / instruments.* The body's members (hands, feet, eyes, tongue, mind) are tools that can be used as **weapons** — for sin or for righteousness. The believer is commanded **not to make his members available to sin as weapons against God and against righteousness.**

**(5)** **ἀλλὰ παραστήσατε ἑαυτοὺς τῷ Θεῷ** — *"but present yourselves to God."*

Now the **positive command:** present yourselves **to God.** The verb is **παρίστημι** — *to present, to hand over, to place at someone's disposal.* The aorist tense here suggests a definitive presentation — make this transfer.

**ὡσεὶ ἐκ νεκρῶν ζῶντας** — *"as those alive from the dead."* The basis of the self-presentation: **you are alive from the dead.** Your reckoning of vv. 11 informs your presentation of vv. 13.

**(6)** **καὶ τὰ μέλη ὑμῶν ὅπλα δικαιοσύνης τῷ Θεῷ** — *"and your members as weapons of righteousness to God."*

The same members that were weapons of unrighteousness are now presented as **weapons of righteousness to God.** The hands that took are now hands that give. The tongue that lied is now the tongue that speaks truth. The mind that imagined evil is now the mind that meditates on what is true, honorable, just, pure, lovely, and of good report (cf. Phil 4:8).

---

# **ROMANS 6:14 — THE PROMISE THAT SUSTAINS THE COMMANDS**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"For sin shall not have dominion over you: for ye are not under the Law, but under grace."*

---

### Observations of foundational importance

**(1)** **ἁμαρτία γὰρ ὑμῶν οὐ κυριεύσει** — *"For sin shall not have dominion over you."*

**This is a promise, not merely a command.** Future indicative — **shall not.** Paul declares that sin **will not** rule over the believer.

But notice: this comes right after the commands of vv. 12-13. The order is profound:
- The believer is commanded to refuse sin's reign (v. 12).
- The believer is commanded to present himself to God (v. 13).
- And then: sin **shall not** have dominion (v. 14).

**The promise is grounded in the obedience to the command — and the obedience to the command is grounded in the indicative reality of co-crucifixion and resurrection.** The Christian life flows like this:
1. **What is true** (indicative — vv. 1-10).
2. **What we reckon** (v. 11).
3. **How we act** (vv. 12-13).
4. **What is then experientially real** (v. 14).

**(2)** **οὐ γάρ ἐστε ὑπὸ νόμον ἀλλὰ ὑπὸ χάριν** — *"For you are not under law but under grace."*

This is the foundation of the promise. Under the law, sin had power because the law condemned the sinner and provoked the flesh (Rom 7:8-11). Under grace, the believer stands in the favor of God, possessing the righteousness of Christ, indwelt by the Spirit, free from condemnation. **Sin cannot rule a person who stands in grace** — for grace has broken sin's grip and provided the resources for victory.

---

# **ROMANS 6:15-23 — THE SLAVERY MOTIF**

The chapter continues with Paul addressing a second objection — *"Shall we sin because we are not under law but under grace?"* — with a parallel structure: indicative, then imperative.

### **Romans 6:15-18 (selections):**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"What then? shall we sin, because we are not under the Law, but under grace? God forbid. Know ye not, that to whomsoever ye give yourselves as servants to obey, his servants ye are to whom ye obey... But God be thanked, that ye have been the servants of sin, but ye have obeyed from the heart unto the form of the doctrine, whereunto ye were delivered. Being then made free from sin, ye are made the servants of righteousness."*

---

### Observation

Paul presents two slaveries: slavery to sin (which leads to death) and slavery to righteousness/God (which leads to sanctification and eternal life). The believer has been **set free** from the first and **made a slave** to the second.

**The indicative:** You **were** slaves of sin; you **have been** set free; you **are now** slaves of righteousness (v. 18). These are settled facts.

**The imperative implied:** Therefore present your members to righteousness, just as before you presented them to sin (v. 19).

---

# **ROMANS 6:23 — THE CONCLUDING TRUTH**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"For the wages of sin is death: but the gift of God is eternal life, through Jesus Christ our Lord."*

---

### Observation

The chapter closes with the great contrast. Sin **pays wages** — and its wage is death. God **gives** — and His gift is eternal life. Sin earns; grace gives. The two paths are utterly different in source and destination.

And the gift is **ἐν Χριστῷ Ἰησοῦ τῷ Κυρίῳ ἡμῶν** — *"in Christ Jesus our Lord."* Once again, the locus of the new life is identification with Christ.

---

# **THE FULL PATTERN — SYNTHESIS OF ROMANS 6**

| Section | Indicative or Imperative | Content |
|---|---|---|
| **vv. 1-2** | Indicative declared | We died to sin |
| **vv. 3-5** | Indicative expanded | Baptized into His death; buried with Him; will share His resurrection |
| **vv. 6-7** | Indicative explained | Old man crucified; body of sin rendered powerless; freed from sin |
| **vv. 8-10** | Indicative applied | Living with Him in His resurrection life pattern |
| **v. 11** | **First imperative** | **Reckon yourselves dead to sin, alive to God** |
| **vv. 12-13** | Practical imperatives | Do not let sin reign; do not yield members to sin; yield to God |
| **v. 14** | Promise + indicative | Sin shall not have dominion; you are under grace |
| **vv. 15-22** | Indicative + imperative | Slaves of righteousness now; yield to righteousness |
| **v. 23** | Concluding contrast | Wages of sin = death; gift of God = eternal life in Christ |

---

# **THE GOVERNING PATTERN: INDICATIVE → RECKON → ACT**

Romans 6 establishes the fundamental Christian operation:

1. **Know what God has done** (the indicative — vv. 1-10).
2. **Reckon it true of yourself** (v. 11).
3. **Act on the reckoning** (vv. 12-13).

This is the precise reverse of the world's pattern, which is:
1. *Try to act righteously.*
2. *Hope to become righteous through your acts.*
3. *Believe in your status only after performance.*

**The Gospel pattern is the opposite:**
1. *You ARE righteous in Christ (indicative).*
2. *Reckon it true (faith).*
3. *Act consistently with what is true (obedience).*

The Christian does not act in order to become; he acts because he has become.

---

# **CROSS-WITNESSES — THE PATTERN ELSEWHERE**

This indicative-to-imperative pattern is not unique to Romans 6 — it is the fundamental pattern of all NT ethics. Let us confirm by examining the most explicit parallels.

---

## **EPHESIANS — THE INDICATIVE OF CHAPTERS 1-3, THE IMPERATIVE OF 4-6**

The entire epistle to the Ephesians displays this pattern on a grand scale.

**Chapters 1-3 are pure indicative:**
- Chosen in Him before the foundation of the world (1:4).
- Predestined to adoption (1:5).
- Accepted in the Beloved (1:6).
- Redeemed through His blood (1:7).
- Sealed with the Holy Spirit (1:13).
- Made alive together with Christ (2:5).
- Raised up together (2:6).
- Seated together in the heavenly places in Christ Jesus (2:6).
- Fellow-citizens with the saints (2:19).
- Members of God's household (2:19).
- Built together for a habitation of God (2:22).
- Filled with the fullness of God (3:19).

**Chapters 4-6 are pure imperative — and they begin with the pivot word.**

### **Ephesians 4:1:**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"I therefore, being prisoner in the Lord, pray you that ye walk worthy of the vocation whereunto ye are called,"*

The word **οὖν** — *"therefore"* — is the pivot. Three chapters of indicative produce one *"therefore"* that launches three chapters of imperative.

**The imperative of Ephesians 4-6 is grounded entirely in the indicative of Ephesians 1-3.** Walk worthy of the calling — *because of all that Christ has already accomplished in you.*

---

## **COLOSSIANS 3 — THE EXPLICIT CONNECTION**

### **Colossians 3:1-5:**

### **1599 Geneva:**
*"If ye then be risen with Christ, seek those things which are above, where Christ sitteth at the right hand of God. Set your affections on things which are above, and not on things which are on the earth. For ye are dead, and your life is hid with Christ in God. When Christ which is our life, shall appear, then shall ye also appear with him in glory. Mortify therefore your members which are on the earth..."*

---

### Observation

**The pattern is perfectly visible:**

- **Indicative (v. 1):** *"If you have been raised with Christ..."* — you have been.
- **Indicative (v. 3):** *"For you died, and your life is hidden with Christ in God."*
- **Indicative (v. 4):** *"You shall appear with Him in glory."*
- **Imperative (v. 1):** *"Seek those things which are above."*
- **Imperative (v. 2):** *"Set your affection on things above."*
- **Imperative (v. 5):** *"Mortify therefore your members which are on the earth..."*

The word **οὖν** — *"therefore"* — appears again as the pivot in v. 5. **Because you have died (indicative), therefore mortify your members (imperative).**

This is the same Romans 6 pattern, restated.

---

## **GALATIANS 5:24-25 — A SUCCINCT STATEMENT**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"For they that are Christ's, have crucified the flesh, with the affections and the lusts. If we live in the Spirit, let us also walk in the Spirit."*

---

### Observation

**v. 24 — Indicative:** Those who belong to Christ **have crucified** the flesh. The verb is aorist — completed past action.
