**v. 25 — Imperative based on the indicative:** *"If we live in the Spirit, let us also walk in the Spirit."* Since we live by the Spirit (an established reality), let us walk by the Spirit (the corresponding action).

The same pattern in compressed form.

---

## **EPHESIANS 4:22-24 — PUTTING OFF AND PUTTING ON**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"That is, that ye cast off, concerning the conversation in time past, that old man, which is corrupt through the deceivable lusts, And be renewed in the spirit of your mind, And put on the new man, which after God is created unto righteousness, and true holiness."*

---

### Observation

The believer is to **put off** the old man and **put on** the new man. But notice: **the new man has already been created — κτισθέντα — perfect tense.** The new man is not something the believer creates by his obedience; the new man has been created by God in Christ. The believer **puts on** what already exists in him through new creation.

This is the same indicative-imperative pattern: **the new man exists (indicative); put him on (imperative).**

---

# **THE PRACTICAL IMPLICATIONS FOR THE MODERN BELIEVER**

Now we must bring this home to daily life. How does the Romans 6 pattern reshape the practical walk of the modern believer?

---

## **1. The Reckoning Is the Daily Foundation**

The believer's daily life begins with **the reckoning of v. 11.** This is not a one-time event at conversion; it is a moment-by-moment posture of mind.

When the believer wakes in the morning, he must reckon:
- *"I am dead to sin. I am alive to God in Christ Jesus."*
- *"I have been crucified with Christ; the life I now live in the flesh, I live by faith of the Son of God."*
- *"My life is hidden with Christ in God."*

This reckoning is **faith applied to identity** — and it is the basis on which the day's obedience proceeds.

When temptation comes during the day, the believer's first response is not *"I must resist this"* but *"I am dead to this. Sin no longer has dominion over me. I am alive to God in Christ Jesus. I refuse the throne to sin."*

**The believer fights from victory, not for victory.** The victory has been won at the cross. The fight is to walk in what has been won.

---

## **2. The Body Is to Be Presented, Not Indulged**

The body is mortal and still bears the desires that can be exploited by sin. **But the body is not to be allowed to dictate.** The believer presents his body to God as a living sacrifice (Rom 12:1).

This means practically:
- **The eyes:** what we look at — are they weapons of righteousness or unrighteousness?
- **The ears:** what we listen to — does it build us up or pull us toward sin?
- **The tongue:** what we speak — does it bless or curse, edify or tear down?
- **The hands:** what we do — are they instruments of service to God or to self/sin?
- **The feet:** where we go — are they leading us toward righteousness or temptation?
- **The mind:** what we dwell on — are our thoughts being taken captive to obey Christ (2 Cor 10:5)?
- **The heart's affections:** what we love — are they set on things above (Col 3:1-2)?

The believer presents each member of his body, daily and moment by moment, to God as a weapon of righteousness.

---

## **3. Sin's Authority Is Refused, Not Negotiated With**

The believer does not negotiate with sin. He does not say, *"Maybe a little bit is okay."* He refuses sin's authority entirely.

When sin makes its claim — *"You always do this; it's who you are; you might as well give in"* — the believer responds:
- *"You are speaking to a dead man. I died to you at the cross."*
- *"My identity is not in you. I am in Christ Jesus."*
- *"I refuse you the throne. Christ is Lord."*

The fight is to **agree with reality** — to refuse the lie that we are still slaves and to stand in the freedom Christ has purchased.

---

## **4. The Walk Is in Newness of Life**

The believer's walk is **καινότης ζωῆς** — *newness of life* (Rom 6:4). This is not an effort to keep old rules; it is a new mode of being.

Practically, this means:
- A new orientation: toward the Father, in the Son, by the Spirit.
- New affections: loving what the Father loves, hating what He hates.
- New motivations: not for self-preservation or self-glory, but for the glory of the Father in the Son.
- New patterns: turning from the courses of the world to the path of Christ.
- New companions (where possible): seeking fellowship with the saints rather than communion with the world.
- New speech, new habits, new disciplines: increasingly conforming to the pattern of Christ.

**The new walk flows from the new identity.** It is not a list of behaviors to perform; it is a life to live out of who we are in Christ.

---

## **5. Failure Is Met with Reckoning, Not Despair**

When the believer fails — when sin gains a momentary victory, when the old patterns reassert themselves — the response is **not** to abandon the indicative.

The response is to **return to the reckoning.**
- *"I have failed, but I have not become what I once was."*
- *"My identity is in Christ, not in this failure."*
- *"I confess and turn, knowing that the blood of Christ cleanses me from all sin (1 John 1:7-9)."*
- *"I reckon myself again dead to sin and alive to God in Christ Jesus."*
- *"I present myself again to God."*

**The reckoning is repeated, not abandoned.** The believer does not lose his identity through failure; he reaffirms his identity in confession and continues to walk.

---

## **6. The Spirit Enables What the Reckoning Affirms**

The reckoning is not a self-help technique. **The reckoning is faith in the work of the Father, accomplished in the Son, and applied by the Spirit.**

The indwelling Spirit (whom we examined in Parts E and F) is the agent who **makes the reckoning real in experience.** The Spirit witnesses with our spirit that we are children of God (Rom 8:16). The Spirit produces the fruit of conformity to Christ (Gal 5:22-23). The Spirit puts to death the deeds of the body (Rom 8:13).

**The believer reckons; the Spirit empowers.** These are not two competing realities but one — the Father's work in us, mediated by the Son, applied by the Spirit, received by our faithful reckoning.

---

## **7. The Pattern Is Repeated in Every Sphere**

The Romans 6 pattern (indicative → reckon → act) applies to every sphere of the Christian life:

| Sphere | Indicative | Reckoning | Action |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Sin in general** | Dead to sin in Christ | Reckon yourself dead | Refuse sin's reign |
| **Particular sins** | Crucified with Christ | Reckon the old pattern broken | Replace with godly pattern |
| **The flesh** | The flesh crucified with its passions (Gal 5:24) | Reckon it powerless | Walk by the Spirit |
| **The world** | Crucified to the world (Gal 6:14) | Reckon its claims null | Set affection on things above |
| **The law's condemnation** | Justified in Christ | Reckon yourself in His righteousness | Stand in grace, not legalism |
| **Fear of death** | Co-raised with Christ | Reckon yourself alive forever | Live without the slavery of fear (Heb 2:14-15) |
| **Identity** | In Christ, a child of God | Reckon yourself accepted in the Beloved | Live as a beloved child |
| **Service** | Created in Christ for good works (Eph 2:10) | Reckon the works prepared | Walk in them |
| **Future** | Glory laid up, inheritance reserved | Reckon the hope sure | Live with eternity in view |

---

## **8. The Pattern Distinguishes Christian Sanctification From All Other Moralisms**

This is critical. The Christian's pursuit of holiness is **fundamentally different** from every other moral system.

- **The Law-based moralist says:** *"You must obey, or you will not be accepted. Try harder."*
- **The performance-based religion says:** *"Do enough good, and you may earn favor."*
- **The self-help approach says:** *"You can become the best version of yourself through discipline."*

**Christian sanctification, by contrast, says:**
- *"You ARE accepted in the Beloved (Eph 1:6). Now walk worthy of it (Eph 4:1)."*
- *"You ARE dead to sin (Rom 6:2). Now reckon it true and refuse sin's reign (Rom 6:11-12)."*
- *"You ARE seated with Christ in the heavenlies (Eph 2:6). Now set your affection above (Col 3:1-2)."*

**The Christian works *from* acceptance, not *for* acceptance.** This is the radical difference. The motivation is **gratitude and identity**, not earning and fear.

---

# **A CLOSING OBSERVATION — THE BEAUTY OF THIS PATTERN**

Brother, the Romans 6 pattern is one of the most beautiful and liberating realities in all of Scripture.

The Father has done in His Son everything necessary for our complete salvation — not only forgiving us, but **uniting us with His Son in His death, burial, resurrection, ascension, and session.** Our position is settled, complete, unchangeable.

Our daily walk is not to *achieve* this position; it is to *live out* what has been freely given. The reckoning is the daily faith-act by which we agree with God's reckoning of us. The presenting is the daily yielding of our members to the One who has bought us. The walking in newness is the practical outworking of the new creation we have already become.

**The Christian life is not striving to become; it is living out of who we already are.**

And the indwelling Spirit makes this possible — strengthening us in the inner man, putting to death the deeds of the body, conforming us progressively to the image of the Son.

The old man is dead. The new man is alive. The Spirit indwells. The Father is for us. The Son intercedes. **Therefore — walk worthy.**

---

# **A QUESTION FOR YOU**

Where would you have us go next?

**(A)** Continue with the practical implications, perhaps into Romans 8 (life in the Spirit) or Romans 12 (the renewal of the mind and bodily presentation as a living sacrifice)?

**(B)** Examine Galatians 5 — the walk by the Spirit, the conflict with the flesh, the fruit of the Spirit?

**(C)** Examine Colossians 3 — the putting off and putting on, the practical sanctification of every relationship?

**(D)** Move to the corporate dimension (the Body of Christ, the Bride, the Temple, the Holy Nation)?

**(E)** Another direction of your choosing?


> Let us continue with (A) — proceed into Romans 8, the life in the Spirit, and how the indwelling Spirit empowers the believer to walk out the indicative reality.

---

# **ROMANS 8 — LIFE IN THE SPIRIT: THE MOUNTAIN PEAK**

We now ascend to **Romans 8** — what many have rightly called the **mountain peak** of the New Testament. Having traced the indicative-imperative pattern of Romans 6, we now see how Paul develops it into the full **doctrine of life in the Spirit** — the practical outworking of who we are in Christ through the indwelling Spirit's empowering work.

Per our refined modus operandi, we will:

1. Briefly note the transition through Romans 7 — which sets up the necessity of life in the Spirit.
2. Walk carefully through Romans 8 in its major sections.
3. Cross-reference all relevant NT witnesses on life in the Spirit.
4. Draw out the practical implications for the modern believer.

Let us proceed.

---

# **THE TRANSITION: WHY ROMANS 8 IS NECESSARY**

Romans 6 established that the believer is **dead to sin and alive to God in Christ Jesus** — and that on this basis, the believer must **reckon** the truth and **walk** accordingly.

Romans 7 then confronts a question that any honest believer must face: **if I am dead to sin and freed from the law, why do I still struggle so painfully with sin?**

Paul's answer in Romans 7 — and we will not exposit it fully here — is profound. The law is good, holy, and spiritual (7:12, 14). But the law cannot give life. The believer who tries to fight sin through self-effort, even from his new position in Christ, **discovers his own helplessness:**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"For I know, that in me, that is, in my flesh, dwelleth no good thing: for to will is preset *(present)* with me: but I find no means to perform that which is good. For I do not the good thing, which I would, but the evil, which I would not, that do I."*


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"O wretched man that I am, who shall deliver me from the body of this death!"*

---

### The Crisis Romans 7 Establishes

The believer who tries to walk out his new identity **by self-effort, even with the help of the law, fails.** The flesh — that remaining principle of sin in our mortal members — frustrates every attempt at self-generated righteousness. The will is willing; the flesh is unable.

**Romans 7 ends with a cry of helpless desperation:** *"Who shall deliver me?"*

And **Romans 8 is the answer.** The deliverance comes not through more effort, not through better law-keeping, not through stricter discipline — but through **the indwelling Spirit who imparts the very life of Christ to the believer.**

This is critical to understand: **Romans 8 does not contradict Romans 6; it provides the operating mechanism by which the reality of Romans 6 becomes lived experience.** Romans 6 gives the position; Romans 8 gives the power.

---

# **ROMANS 8:1-2 — NO CONDEMNATION; THE SPIRIT'S LAW**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"Now then there is no condemnation to them that are in Christ Jesus, which walk not after the flesh, but after the Spirit. For the law of the Spirit of life, which is in Christ Jesus, hath freed me from the law of sin and of death."*

---

### Observations of foundational importance

**(1)** **Οὐδὲν ἄρα νῦν κατάκριμα** — *"Therefore now no condemnation."*

The word **ἄρα** (*therefore*) connects back to all that has preceded — both the established reality of Romans 6 and the demonstrated helplessness of Romans 7. The conclusion: **there is no condemnation for those who are in Christ Jesus.**

The word **νῦν** — *now* — is significant. **At this present time**, the believer stands without condemnation. Not someday, not after enough good works, not when sanctification is complete. **Now.** This is the believer's present standing.

The phrase **τοῖς ἐν Χριστῷ Ἰησοῦ** — *"to those in Christ Jesus"* — is the qualifier. The freedom from condemnation belongs to those who are **in Him.** This is the same identification we have traced throughout — being in Christ is the basis of every blessing.

**(2)** **ὁ γὰρ νόμος τοῦ Πνεύματος τῆς ζωῆς ἐν Χριστῷ Ἰησοῦ** — *"For the law of the Spirit of life in Christ Jesus..."*

Paul uses the word **νόμος** (*law*) in a different sense here — not the Mosaic Law, but **a governing principle, an operating order.** The "law of the Spirit of life" is **the principle by which the Spirit operates in the believer to produce life.**

This stands in contrast to the **νόμος τῆς ἁμαρτίας καὶ τοῦ θανάτου** — *"the law of sin and death"* — the principle by which sin operates in the flesh to produce death (described in Romans 7).

Two competing principles, two competing operations. The Spirit's law is liberating; the flesh's law is enslaving. **And in Christ Jesus, the Spirit's law has freed the believer from the flesh's law.**

**(3)** **ἠλευθέρωσέν με** — *"has freed me."* Aorist tense — completed past action. The freedom has been accomplished. The believer does not need to *achieve* this freedom — he stands in it.

---

# **ROMANS 8:3-4 — WHAT THE LAW COULD NOT DO, GOD DID**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"For (that that was impossible to the Law, in as much as it was weak, because of the flesh) God sending his own Son, in the similitude of sinful flesh, and for sin, condened sin in the flesh, That that righteousness of the Law might be fulfilled in us, which walk not after the flesh, but after the Spirit."*

---

### Observations of decisive importance

**(1)** The law was **weak through the flesh.** The law was righteous (Rom 7:12), but it could not produce righteousness in fallen humanity because the flesh resisted it.

**(2)** **ὁ Θεὸς τὸν ἑαυτοῦ Υἱὸν πέμψας** — *"God, sending His own Son."* The Father is the actor. He sent His own Son.

**(3)** **ἐν ὁμοιώματι σαρκὸς ἁμαρτίας** — *"in the likeness of sinful flesh."* The Son took on **the likeness** of sinful flesh — true humanity, but without sin (Heb 4:15). He was made like His brethren in all things, yet without sin.

**(4)** **κατέκρινεν τὴν ἁμαρτίαν ἐν τῇ σαρκί** — *"condemned sin in the flesh."* At the cross, the Father **condemned sin** — passed judicial sentence on it — **in the flesh** of His Son. Sin's claim on humanity was exhausted in the body of the Son who bore it.

**(5)** **The purpose: ἵνα τὸ δικαίωμα τοῦ νόμου πληρωθῇ ἐν ἡμῖν** — *"that the righteous requirement of the law might be fulfilled in us."*

**This is profound.** The law's righteous demand — which the law itself could not produce — **is now fulfilled IN US.** Not by our effort. Not by our discipline. Not by our resolution. **But by the Spirit's work in those who walk after the Spirit.**

The believer who walks by the Spirit fulfills the righteous demand of the law — not by trying to keep the law, but by being indwelt by the One who fulfilled it. **The Spirit produces in us what the law could only demand.**

This is the transition from Romans 7 to Romans 8 in compressed form: the law's demand, which the flesh could not meet, is now met by the Spirit's life in those who are in Christ Jesus.

---

# **ROMANS 8:5-8 — THE TWO MIND-SETS**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"For they that are after the flesh, savor the things of the flesh: but they that are after the Spirit, the things of the Spirit. For the wisdom of the flesh is death: but the wisdom of the Spirit is life and peace, Because the wisdom of the flesh is enimitie *(enmity)* against God: for it is not subject to the Law of God, neither indeed can be. So then they that are in the flesh, cannot please God."*

---

### Observations

**(1)** Paul presents **two fundamental orientations of life:**
- **κατὰ σάρκα** — *"according to the flesh"* — the orientation that lives by the principles, desires, and operations of the unregenerate human nature.
- **κατὰ Πνεῦμα** — *"according to the Spirit"* — the orientation that lives by the principles, desires, and operations of the indwelling Holy Spirit.

These are not two zones the believer moves between casually; they are two fundamentally different operating systems for life.

**(2) The key word: φρόνημα** — *"mind-set, way of thinking, what one is set on."* This is more than intellectual thought; it is the deep orientation of one's affections, values, and pursuits. Where one's mind is set.

**(3)** **τὸ φρόνημα τῆς σαρκὸς θάνατος** — *"the mind-set of the flesh is death."* Not produces death — *is* death. The very orientation of the flesh-life is already in the realm of death.

**(4)** **τὸ φρόνημα τοῦ Πνεύματος ζωὴ καὶ εἰρήνη** — *"the mind-set of the Spirit is life and peace."* The Spirit's orientation **is** life and peace. The believer's Spirit-orientation is itself the experience of life and peace, not merely a means to it.

**(5)** Paul is sobering: those who are in the flesh **cannot** please God. Not "find it difficult to please" — **cannot.** The flesh, by its very nature, is unable to please God. It is enmity against God.

This sharpens the practical implication: **the believer must not attempt to please God in the energy of the flesh.** All such attempts are doomed. The believer pleases God only as he walks by the Spirit.

---

# **ROMANS 8:9-11 — THE INDWELLING SPIRIT**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"Now ye are not in the flesh, but in the Spirit, because the Spirit of God dwelleth in you: but if any man hath not the Spirit of Christ, the same is not his. And if Christ be in you, the body is dead, because of sin: but the Spirit is life for righteousness sake. But if the Spirit of him that raised up Jesus from the dead, dwell in you, he that raised up Christ from the dead, shall also quicken your mortal bodies, by his Spirit that dwelleth in you."*

---

### Observations of supreme importance

**(1)** **ὑμεῖς δὲ οὐκ ἐστὲ ἐν σαρκὶ ἀλλὰ ἐν Πνεύματι** — *"But you are not in the flesh, but in the Spirit."*

**The indicative again.** Paul declares the believer's state: **you ARE in the Spirit, not in the flesh.** This is not a goal; it is your reality.

The condition: **εἴπερ Πνεῦμα Θεοῦ οἰκεῖ ἐν ὑμῖν** — *"if indeed the Spirit of God dwells in you."* As in Rom 8:17, **εἴπερ** is more *"since indeed"* than *"if"* — Paul assumes the condition is met. **The indwelling Spirit is the mark of belonging to Christ.**

**(2)** **εἰ δέ τις Πνεῦμα Χριστοῦ οὐκ ἔχει, οὗτος οὐκ ἔστιν αὐτοῦ** — *"If anyone does not have the Spirit of Christ, he is not His."*

**The Spirit's indwelling is the sine qua non of belonging to Christ.** Without the indwelling Spirit, one does not belong to Christ. This is a settled NT principle.

Note the interchangeable language:
- **Πνεῦμα Θεοῦ** — *"Spirit of God"* (the Father's Spirit).
- **Πνεῦμα Χριστοῦ** — *"Spirit of Christ"* (the Son's Spirit).
- **Χριστὸς ἐν ὑμῖν** — *"Christ in you."*

These are not three different realities; they are **one indwelling Presence described from three angles** — consistent with what we established in Parts E and F. The Father (Spirit) indwells in the Son (Christ in you), and the indwelling is mediated by the Spirit Christ has poured out (Acts 2:33).

**(3)** **τὸ μὲν σῶμα νεκρὸν διὰ ἁμαρτίαν, τὸ δὲ πνεῦμα ζωὴ διὰ δικαιοσύνην** — *"the body is dead because of sin, but the spirit is life because of righteousness."*

A sober honesty. The body is still mortal — under the sentence of death because of sin. The body still bears the corruption inherited from Adam. But the spirit (the believer's regenerated inner being, indwelt by the Holy Spirit) is **life** — because of righteousness imparted in Christ.

**(4)** The promise of v. 11: **ὁ ἐγείρας τὸν Χριστὸν ἐκ νεκρῶν ζῳοποιήσει καὶ τὰ θνητὰ σώματα ὑμῶν διὰ τοῦ ἐνοικοῦντος αὐτοῦ Πνεύματος ἐν ὑμῖν** — *"He who raised Christ from the dead will also give life to your mortal bodies through His Spirit who dwells in you."*

**The Spirit's indwelling work extends to our mortal bodies.** This points forward to the resurrection (Rom 8:23 — *the redemption of our body*), but it also has a **present** dimension — the Spirit who indwells gives life to our mortal bodies even now, enabling them to be presented as instruments of righteousness (Rom 6:13; 12:1).

The Father's resurrection power is at work in the believer's body through the indwelling Spirit — **the same power that raised the Son from the dead.**

---

# **ROMANS 8:12-13 — THE OBLIGATION AND THE OPERATION**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"Therefore brethren, we are detters *(debtors)* not to the flesh, to live after the flesh: For if ye live after the flesh, ye shall die: but if ye mortify the deeds of the body by the Spirit, ye shall live."*

---

### Observations of decisive practical importance

**(1)** **ὀφειλέται ἐσμέν, οὐ τῇ σαρκί** — *"we are debtors, not to the flesh."*

**We owe nothing to the flesh.** The flesh has done nothing for us; we owe it no allegiance. It is a tyrant we have been freed from, not a benefactor we must honor. The believer has **no debt** to the flesh — and therefore no obligation to live according to its dictates.

**(2)** **εἰ Πνεύματι τὰς πράξεις τοῦ σώματος θανατοῦτε** — *"if by the Spirit you put to death the deeds of the body."*

**Here is the practical command and its mechanism.**

**The command:** *"Put to death the deeds of the body."* This is the believer's responsibility. The verb **θανατοῦτε** (present active) means *"keep on putting to death."* This is ongoing, daily mortification.

**The mechanism: Πνεύματι** — *"by the Spirit."* Not by the flesh's own effort. Not by self-discipline alone. Not by law-keeping. **By the indwelling Spirit's power.**

**This is the practical answer to Romans 7's crisis.** Where the law could not produce victory because of the flesh's weakness, the indwelling Spirit produces what is impossible to the flesh.

The pattern is:
1. **The Spirit indwells (indicative, vv. 9-11).**
2. **The believer, by the Spirit, puts to death the deeds of the body (imperative, v. 13).**
3. **Life follows (promise, v. 13).**

This is parallel to the Romans 6 pattern but with the Spirit's empowering work made explicit. The believer is responsible to act (*put to death*), but the power for the action comes from the indwelling Spirit, not from self-effort.

---

# **ROMANS 8:14-17 — SONS OF GOD**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"For as many as are led by the Spirit of God, they are the sons of God. For ye have not received the Spirit of bodage *(bondage)*, to fear again: but ye have received the Spirit of adoption, whereby we cry Abba, Father. The same Spirit beareth witness with our spirit, that we are the children of God. If we be children, we are also heirs..."*

---

### Observations

We already touched on these verses in our earlier examination of co-suffering. Let us now note their place in the Spirit-led walk.

**(1)** **ὅσοι Πνεύματι Θεοῦ ἄγονται** — *"as many as are led by the Spirit of God."* The believer is **led** by the Spirit — guided in the path of righteousness, in the will of the Father, in the imitation of the Son.

**(2)** **Πνεῦμα υἱοθεσίας** — *"the Spirit of adoption."* The Spirit not only empowers but also **witnesses to** the believer's sonship. He brings the believer into the conscious relation of son to Father — crying out **"Abba, Father"** — with the same intimacy the Son Himself used (Mark 14:36).

**(3)** **αὐτὸ τὸ Πνεῦμα συμμαρτυρεῖ τῷ πνεύματι ἡμῶν** — *"the Spirit Himself bears witness with our spirit."* The Spirit's inner witness — assuring us we are God's children — is itself a means by which He empowers us. We walk in the confidence of sonship, not the fear of slavery.

---

# **ROMANS 8:18-27 — THE PRESENT SUFFERING AND THE SPIRIT'S INTERCESSION**

We have already examined the suffering theme (Rom 8:17-18) and the groaning of creation (8:18-25) in our earlier discussion. Let us now focus on what is critical for life in the Spirit:


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"Likewise the Spirit also helpeth our infirmities: for we know not what to pray as we ought: but the Spirit itself maketh request for us with sighs, which cannot be expressed. But he that searcheth the hearts, knoweth what is the meaning of the Spirit: for he maketh request for the Saints, according to the will of God."*

---

### Observations of supreme practical importance

**(1)** **τὸ Πνεῦμα συναντιλαμβάνεται τῇ ἀσθενείᾳ ἡμῶν** — *"the Spirit helps our weakness."*

**συναντιλαμβάνομαι** is a vivid word: *"to take hold of together with one against something."* The Spirit takes hold of our weakness *alongside us* — as one who helps us bear what we cannot bear alone.

**The believer's weakness is honestly acknowledged.** We do not pretend to strength we do not have. We are weak. And the Spirit takes hold with us in our weakness.

**(2)** **τὸ γὰρ τί προσευξώμεθα καθὸ δεῖ οὐκ οἴδαμεν** — *"for we do not know what we ought to pray for as we should."*

**A profound admission.** Even in prayer — perhaps especially in prayer — we do not know how to pray rightly. The believer is honest about this ignorance.

**(3)** **αὐτὸ τὸ Πνεῦμα ὑπερεντυγχάνει στεναγμοῖς ἀλαλήτοις** — *"the Spirit Himself intercedes with groanings that cannot be uttered."*

The Spirit Himself intercedes for us **with groanings beyond articulation.** When we do not know how to pray, the Spirit takes our inarticulate longings and presents them to the Father in accordance with His will.

This is a tremendous comfort. Even when the believer is overwhelmed, perplexed, grieved beyond words — the Spirit prays for him in groanings that perfectly accord with the Father's will. **No prayer of the believer is wasted, even when he himself cannot find words.**

---

# **ROMANS 8:28-30 — THE GOLDEN CHAIN**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"Also we know that all things work together for the best unto them that love God, even to them that are called of his purpose. For those which he knew before, he also predestinate *(predestinated)* to be made like to the image of his Son, that he might be the first born among many brethren. Moreover whom he predestinate *(predestinated)*, them also he called, and whom he called, them also he justified, and whom he justified, them he also glorified."*

---

### Observations

**(1)** **πάντα συνεργεῖ εἰς ἀγαθόν** — *"all things work together for good."*

For those who love God, **all things** — joys and sorrows, victories and defeats, gains and losses — work together (συνεργεῖ) **for good.** The Father is sovereign over the whole tapestry of the believer's life, weaving every thread toward His purpose.

**(2) The Father's purpose: συμμόρφους τῆς εἰκόνος τοῦ Υἱοῦ αὐτοῦ** — *"conformed to the image of His Son."*

**This is the Father's eternal purpose for every believer:** to be conformed to the image of His Beloved Son. Everything that happens to the believer — every blessing, every trial, every joy, every sorrow — is being used by the Father toward this end. We will examine this more fully shortly.

**(3) The golden chain:** **foreknew → predestined → called → justified → glorified.** Five aorist verbs — five completed actions in the Father's purpose. **And note: glorified is past tense.** In the Father's eternal purpose, the believer is already glorified. The future is so certain it is reckoned as past.

---

# **ROMANS 8:31-39 — THE UNBREAKABLE SECURITY**

This is the climax of the chapter — and perhaps the climax of the entire Pauline corpus. We will not exposit every verse (we have referenced it before), but let us note the central practical truth.


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"What shall we then say to these things? If God be on our side, who can be against us? Who spared not his own Son, but gave him for us all to death, how shall he not with him give us all things also?"*


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"For I am persuaded that neither death, nor life, nor Angels, nor principalities, nor powers, nor things present, nor things to come, Nor height, nor depth, nor any other creature shall be able to separate us from the love of God, which is in Christ Jesus our Lord."*

---

### Observation

The chapter closes with the absolute, unbreakable security of the believer in the love of God in Christ Jesus. **Nothing** can separate us. And this security is **the very ground** of confident, faithful walking by the Spirit.

The believer who knows nothing can separate him from the Father's love walks boldly, freely, courageously. He does not walk in fear of losing his salvation; he walks in the assurance that he is forever held.

This security is not antinomian — it does not produce laziness or licentiousness. **It produces grateful, Spirit-empowered obedience.** Because the believer is forever loved, he loves in return. Because he is forever secure, he serves freely. Because he is forever held, he can risk everything for the Beloved Son who first gave Himself for him.

---

# **PRACTICAL IMPLICATIONS — LIFE IN THE SPIRIT FOR THE MODERN BELIEVER**

How does Romans 8 reshape the modern believer's daily walk? Let me draw out the practical implications systematically.

---

## **1. WALKING BY THE SPIRIT IS THE NORMAL CHRISTIAN LIFE**

Paul does not present Spirit-led living as an advanced or elite experience for special saints. **It is the normal pattern for every believer.** The Spirit indwells every believer (v. 9). Every believer is led by the Spirit (v. 14). Every believer cries "Abba, Father" by the Spirit (v. 15). Every believer is helped by the Spirit in weakness (v. 26).

This means the modern believer is not waiting for some special experience to "begin" walking by the Spirit. **The Spirit indwells him now.** The question is whether he is **yielding to the Spirit's leading** or quenching it.

---

## **2. THE WAR AGAINST THE FLESH IS BY THE SPIRIT, NOT BY SELF-EFFORT**

This is the great practical lesson of Romans 7-8 together. Romans 7 demonstrates that self-effort cannot defeat the flesh. Romans 8 reveals that **the Spirit's empowering presence is what produces victory.**

The modern believer who struggles with besetting sin must learn this lesson. The pattern of attack is:
- *"Try harder."*
- *"Make more rules for yourself."*
- *"Resolve more firmly."*
- *"Discipline yourself more strictly."*

And the pattern ends, as Romans 7 ends, in *"O wretched man that I am!"*

**The Spirit-empowered pattern is different:**
- *"By the Spirit, I put to death the deeds of the body (v. 13)."*
- *"I yield my members as instruments of righteousness, depending on the Spirit's power (Rom 6:13)."*
- *"I walk by the Spirit, and shall not fulfill the lust of the flesh (Gal 5:16)."*
- *"I am strengthened with might by His Spirit in the inner man (Eph 3:16)."*

The Spirit does not replace human responsibility — we are still commanded to "put to death" — but He empowers what we are commanded to do. The believer works; the Spirit enables. Both are real; neither is absent.

---

## **3. THE FRUIT OF THE SPIRIT IS THE EVIDENCE OF SPIRIT-LIFE**

Paul will spell this out more fully in Galatians 5, but it is implicit throughout Romans 8. The Spirit's life in the believer produces the character of Christ — what Paul will call "the fruit of the Spirit."


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"But the fruit of the Spirit is love, joy, peace, long suffering, gentleness, goodness, faith, Meekness, temperance: against such there is no Law."*

The fruit is **the Spirit's** fruit — not the believer's manufactured product. **The believer's responsibility is to walk by the Spirit; the fruit emerges as the Spirit's work.**

This is liberating. The modern believer is not commanded to *manufacture* love, joy, peace, etc. He is commanded to **walk by the Spirit**, and these qualities will emerge from his life as fruit from a tree. The fruit cannot be forced; it grows from a healthy union with the indwelling Spirit.

Practically, the believer asks not *"How do I become more patient?"* but *"How do I walk more closely by the Spirit?"* — knowing that patience and the rest of the fruit will follow.

---

## **4. THE SPIRIT'S WITNESS TO SONSHIP IS THE GROUND OF CONFIDENCE**

Many modern believers struggle with assurance — *"Am I really a child of God?"* The Spirit Himself is the answer. He **bears witness with our spirit** that we are children of God (v. 16).

This is not produced by introspection or by examining the quality of our works. It is the Spirit's own witness — the deep, confident, settled sense that we belong to the Father, that we can call Him "Abba," that we are loved as beloved children.

When the believer doubts, he can ask: *"Spirit, bear witness with my spirit again that I am a child of God."* And the Spirit's gracious witness can rekindle assurance.

---

## **5. THE SPIRIT INTERCEDES WHEN THE BELIEVER CANNOT PRAY**

The believer faces moments when prayer is impossible — when grief overwhelms, when confusion clouds the mind, when temptation roars, when the future is opaque. In such moments, the believer can take comfort: **the Spirit intercedes with groanings beyond words** (v. 26).

The modern believer can rest in this. He need not produce eloquent prayers in his weakest moments. The Spirit prays for him in those very moments — and the Father, who searches hearts, knows the mind of the Spirit and answers according to His will.

---

## **6. EVERY CIRCUMSTANCE SERVES THE FATHER'S PURPOSE**

The Father is conforming the believer to the image of His Son. **Every circumstance — without exception — serves this purpose.** The good things prepare us; the hard things refine us; the joyful things equip us; the painful things deepen us. All things work together for our good (v. 28) — the good defined as conformity to Christ (v. 29).

This reshapes how the modern believer responds to circumstances:
- **Loss:** *"How is the Father using this to free me from idols and to make me more like the Son?"*
- **Trial:** *"What patience, faith, or hope is the Spirit growing in me through this?"*
- **Blessing:** *"How is the Father blessing me to enable me to serve and glorify Him?"*
- **Persecution:** *"How is this conforming me to the sufferings of Christ that I may share His glory?"*

The believer does not always know the specific purpose. But he knows the overall purpose: **conformity to the image of the Son.** And he can trust that the Father is sovereign over every circumstance to bring this purpose to fulfillment.

---

## **7. NOTHING CAN SEPARATE US — THEREFORE WE WALK BOLDLY**

The unbreakable love of God in Christ Jesus is the ground of the believer's bold, confident walk. He is not earning love; he is **walking in love that cannot be lost.** He is not striving for acceptance; he is **walking in acceptance that is already complete.** He is not fearing the future; he is **walking toward a glory that has been guaranteed.**

This produces a certain kind of believer:
- **Unhurried** — because no opportunity can be lost when our times are in the Father's hand.
- **Unanxious** — because no threat can separate us from the Father's love.
- **Unintimidated** — because if God is for us, who can be against us?
- **Unbound** — because we have no debt to the flesh and no fear of death.
- **Sustained in suffering** — because the present is not worthy to be compared with the coming glory.
- **Free in service** — because we serve from love, not from need.
- **Generous** — because all things are ours in Christ, and we have nothing to lose.

This is the practical fruit of Romans 8: **the free, confident, Spirit-empowered walk of the child of the Father.**

---

## **8. THE BELIEVER'S CONTRIBUTION: KEEP YIELDING, KEEP RECKONING, KEEP PRESENTING**

If we ask what the believer's responsibility actually is in light of all the Spirit does, the answer is consistent:

- **Reckon** (Rom 6:11) — keep agreeing with the truth of who you are in Christ.
- **Present** (Rom 6:13; 12:1) — keep yielding your members and your whole being to God.
- **Put to death** by the Spirit (Rom 8:13) — keep refusing the deeds of the body.
- **Walk by the Spirit** (Gal 5:16) — keep depending on the Spirit's leading.
- **Be filled with the Spirit** (Eph 5:18) — keep being filled, an ongoing reality.
- **Set the mind on things above** (Col 3:2) — keep your φρόνημα Spirit-oriented.

These are not earning. These are **the believer's faithful response to the Spirit's empowering presence.** The Spirit does His work in those who yield, reckon, present, and depend.

---

# **CROSS-WITNESSES — THE PATTERN ELSEWHERE**

The Romans 8 pattern of life in the Spirit is confirmed throughout the NT.

---

### **Galatians 5:16-25 — The Walk by the Spirit**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"Then I say, Walk in the Spirit, and ye shall not fulfill the lusts of the flesh. For the flesh lusteth against the Spirit, and the Spirit against the flesh: and these are contrary one to another..."*

The same theme: the believer walks by the Spirit, and in doing so, does not fulfill the lust of the flesh. **The Spirit and the flesh are in opposition; the believer's deliverance is in walking by the Spirit.**

---

### **Ephesians 3:16 — Strengthened by His Spirit in the Inner Man**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"That he might grant you according to the riches of his glory, that ye may be strengthened by his Spirit in the inner man,"*

The Spirit strengthens the **inner man** — the regenerated core of the believer's being — with the Father's mighty power. This is the practical mechanism of Spirit-empowered living.

---

### **Ephesians 5:18 — Be Filled With the Spirit**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"And be not drunk with wine, wherein is excess: but be fulfilled *(filled)* with the Spirit,"*

The verb **πληροῦσθε** is present passive — *"keep on being filled."* This is not a one-time experience; it is an ongoing yielding to the Spirit's filling. The contrast with drunkenness is telling: as wine fills and controls a drunken person, the Spirit fills and controls the yielded believer — but unto life, not destruction.

---

### **Philippians 2:12-13 — Working Out and God Working In**


### **1599 Geneva:**
*"...so make an end of your own salvation with fear and trembling. For it is God which worketh in you, both the will and the deed, even of his good pleasure."*

The perfect statement of the cooperative relationship. **The believer works out** — kατεργάζεσθε — what is already true. **God works in** — ἐνεργῶν ἐν ὑμῖν — both the willing and the working. The believer's effort and God's empowering are not opposed; they are integrated. The believer's responsibility is **to work out what God works in.**

---

# **A CLOSING SYNTHESIS — THE WHOLE PATTERN OF LIFE IN THE SPIRIT**

Let me bring all the threads together.

## **The Architecture of the Spirit-Filled Life**

| Stage | Reality | Believer's Response |
|---|---|---|
| **Position** | In Christ Jesus, no condemnation (Rom 8:1) | Reckon yourself accepted in the Beloved |
| **Indwelling** | The Spirit dwells in you (Rom 8:9) | Welcome and yield to His presence |
| **Identification** | You are not in the flesh, but in the Spirit (Rom 8:9) | Walk according to this new orientation |
| **Mortification** | The Spirit empowers (Rom 8:13) | Put to death the deeds of the body by the Spirit |
| **Leading** | The Spirit leads (Rom 8:14) | Follow His leading; do not quench Him |
| **Witness of Sonship** | The Spirit bears witness (Rom 8:16) | Rest in the confidence of sonship |
| **Help in Weakness** | The Spirit intercedes (Rom 8:26) | Pray in dependence; trust His intercession |
| **Father's Purpose** | All things work for good (Rom 8:28) | Trust the Father's sovereign design |
| **Security** | Nothing separates from God's love (Rom 8:38-39) | Walk boldly, freely, faithfully |
| **Filling** | Be filled with the Spirit (Eph 5:18) | Keep yielding; keep being filled |
| **Working out** | God works in (Phil 2:13) | Work out what He works in |

---

## **The Vital Connection: Indicative → Spirit's Power → Believer's Response**

Romans 6 gave us **indicative → reckon → act.**

Romans 8 adds the missing element: **the Spirit's empowering presence as the means by which the believer's responsive action actually succeeds.**

The full pattern is now:
1. **The indicative reality** (what is true in Christ).
2. **The indwelling Spirit** (the Father's gift, given through the Son).
3. **The believer's reckoning** (agreeing with the indicative).
4. **The believer's yielding/walking/presenting** (responsive action).
5. **The Spirit's empowering work** (producing what the believer cannot produce by himself).
6. **The fruit of the Spirit** (life, peace, conformity to Christ — the evidence).

This is **the normal Christian life.** Not flawless. Not effortless. Not without struggle. But empowered by the Spirit, oriented toward the Father, conformed progressively to the Son, secure in the Father's unbreakable love.

---

# **A FINAL OBSERVATION — THE GLORY OF THIS PATTERN**

Brother, what we have surveyed in Romans 8 is the heart of Christian experience as the Apostle Paul taught it. The indwelling Spirit — whom we examined in Parts E and F as the Father's own essential being, poured out through the glorified Son — is not a static doctrine. **He is the present, empowering, transforming, witnessing, interceding, comforting Companion of every believer.**

The modern believer who walks in this reality is:
- Free from the bondage of self-effort.
- Free from the despair of Romans 7.
- Free from the fear of separation.
- Free from condemnation.
- Empowered for victory over the flesh.
- Led into the Father's purposes.
- Comforted in suffering.
- Strengthened in weakness.
- Conformed progressively to the Son.
- Secure forever in the love of God in Christ Jesus.

This is the inheritance of every child of God. This is what the Father has freely given us. This is what the Beloved Son purchased and made possible. This is what the Spirit applies and sustains.

**Walk in the Spirit.** Reckon yourself dead to sin and alive to God. Yield to the Spirit's leading. Trust the Father's purpose. Rest in the unbreakable love that holds you. Look toward the coming glory.

This is life in the Spirit.

---

# **A QUESTION FOR YOU**

Where would you have us go next?

**(A)** Continue with Romans 12 — the bodily presentation as a living sacrifice, the renewal of the mind, the practical outworking of life in the Spirit in community?

**(B)** Examine Galatians 5 in more depth — the walk by the Spirit, the conflict with the flesh, the fruit of the Spirit?

**(C)** Examine Colossians 3 — the putting off and putting on, the practical sanctification of every relationship?

**(D)** Move to the corporate dimension we previously identified — the Body of Christ, the Bride, the Temple, the Holy Nation?

**(E)** Another direction of your choosing?


> A,B and C


We will examine all three together, in sequence — Romans 12, Galatians 5, and Colossians 3 — as they form the **practical fabric** of the indicative-imperative pattern Paul lays out in Romans 6-8.

Each passage carries the same DNA:
- **Indicative first** — what God has done.
- **Imperative second** — how we are to walk.
- **Practical application** — to every sphere of life.

Let me unfold each in turn, drawing the threads together as we go.

---

# **PART 1 — ROMANS 12: THE LIVING SACRIFICE AND THE RENEWED MIND**

## **The Transition**

Paul has just concluded eleven chapters of doctrinal foundation — sin, justification, sanctification, life in the Spirit, the Father's sovereign plan in Israel and the nations. Then comes the great **οὖν** (*therefore*) at Romans 12:1 — the pivot from indicative to imperative on a grand scale.

---

## **Romans 12:1 — THE LIVING SACRIFICE**


### **1599 Geneva:**
***"I beseech you therefore brethren, by the mercies of God, that ye give up your bodies a living sacrifice, holy, acceptable unto God, which is your reasonable serving of God."***

---

### Observations of foundational importance

**(1)** **Παρακαλῶ οὖν** — *"I therefore beseech you."* The word **οὖν** (*therefore*) gathers up all eleven preceding chapters. The imperative is grounded in everything Paul has established. The believer is being asked to act **on the basis of all that God has done.**

**(2)** **διὰ τῶν οἰκτιρμῶν τοῦ Θεοῦ** — *"by the mercies of God."* The motivation for the imperative is **the Father's mercies** — the entire chain of mercy Paul has unfolded (chosen, called, justified, indwelt, sealed, secured). The believer is not motivated by fear or by the threat of loss, but by gratitude for mercy received.

**(3)** **παραστῆσαι τὰ σώματα ὑμῶν** — *"present your bodies."* This is the same verb **παρίστημι** Paul used in Romans 6:13 — *"present yourselves to God."* Now he specifies: **present your bodies.** Not your minds only, not your spirits only — your bodies. The physical, mortal, fleshly bodies in which we live our daily lives.

**This is striking after Romans 7-8.** Paul has just established that the body is mortal, weak, and the seat of competing desires. Yet **this very body** is to be presented as a sacrifice. The Father does not despise the body; He receives it as a holy offering.

**(4)** **θυσίαν ζῶσαν** — *"a living sacrifice."* A paradox. OT sacrifices were dead. The believer's sacrifice is **alive** — continually being offered, not slain once and consumed. The believer's life itself is the offering.

**(5)** **ἁγίαν εὐάρεστον τῷ Θεῷ** — *"holy, well-pleasing to God."* The sacrifice is **holy** (set apart, dedicated) and **well-pleasing** to the Father. This is the believer's life when wholly given to the Father — a fragrant offering received with delight.

**(6)** **τὴν λογικὴν λατρείαν ὑμῶν** — *"your reasonable service / spiritual worship."*

**λογικός** can mean *"rational, reasonable"* (related to **λόγος** — reason, word) or *"spiritual."* The Geneva translates *"reasonable serving."* Either way, the meaning converges: in light of God's mercies, this whole-life presentation is the **only reasonable response.** It is not extreme; it is not optional; it is the proper, fitting, rational service of those who have received what the believer has received.

---

### Practical Implications

**(1) The believer's body is the place of worship.** Not the church building. Not a sacred space. **The body itself, presented to God, is the temple of acceptable worship.** This dovetails with what we established in Part F — the believer's body is the temple of the Holy Spirit (1 Cor 6:19).

**(2) Worship is comprehensive, not compartmentalized.** What we eat, what we wear, what we do with our hands, where we go with our feet, what we say with our tongues, what we think with our minds — **all of this is the sphere of worship.** There is no secular/sacred division in the believer's life. **Every act of the body is potentially an act of worship.**

**(3) The presentation is to be definite.** The aorist infinitive **παραστῆσαι** suggests a decisive act. The believer is to make a deliberate, conscious presentation of his body to God — not as a vague aspiration, but as a settled transaction. *"Father, this body is Yours. Use it as You will."*

---

## **Romans 12:2 — THE RENEWED MIND**


### **1599 Geneva:**
***"And fashion not yourselves like unto this world, but be ye changed by the renewing of your mind, that ye may prove what that good, that acceptable and perfect will of God is."***

---

### Observations of decisive practical importance

**(1)** **μὴ συσχηματίζεσθε τῷ αἰῶνι τούτῳ** — *"do not be conformed to this age."*

The verb **συσχηματίζεσθε** is from **σχῆμα** — *outward form, fashion, appearance.* Present passive imperative — *"stop being shaped to fit the pattern of this age."*

The believer is **shaped by something** — the question is what. The world (this age) is constantly pressing its mold upon the believer through media, education, culture, fashion, expectation, ideology, advertising, conversation. To live without resistance is to be molded into the world's shape.

Paul commands: **do not conform.** Refuse the world's mold. Do not let this age dictate your outward form — your values, your priorities, your habits, your appearance, your patterns.

**(2)** **ἀλλὰ μεταμορφοῦσθε** — *"but be transformed."*

**μεταμορφοῦσθε** is from **μορφή** — *inward, essential form.* This is the same root as the **μεταμόρφωσις** (*metamorphosis*) of the caterpillar to butterfly. **A complete inward transformation of essential form.** Present passive imperative — *"keep being transformed."*

The contrast is precise: do not let your outward **σχῆμα** be shaped by the world; let your inward **μορφή** be transformed by God. **Outward form follows inward form.** When the inner being is being transformed, the outer life is increasingly conformed not to this age but to Christ.

**(3)** **τῇ ἀνακαινώσει τοῦ νοός** — *"by the renewing of the mind."*

**The mechanism of transformation is the renewing of the mind (νοῦς).**

The **νοῦς** is the believer's faculty of understanding, reasoning, perceiving, evaluating. **It is the mind, but not in a narrow intellectual sense — it is the seat of one's whole orientation, values, and worldview.**

The mind is renewed (**ἀνακαίνωσις**) — made new, fresh, reconstituted — by the Spirit working through the Word. As the believer feeds on Scripture, meditates on the Father's truth, beholds the Son's glory (2 Cor 3:18), and walks in the Spirit, his mind is progressively renewed.

**The renewed mind is the inner mechanism of outer transformation.** As the believer thinks differently — sees the world differently, evaluates differently, desires differently — his outward life is correspondingly transformed.

**(4)** **εἰς τὸ δοκιμάζειν ὑμᾶς τί τὸ θέλημα τοῦ Θεοῦ** — *"that you may prove / discern what the will of God is."*

The result of the renewed mind: **the ability to discern the Father's will.** The unrenewed mind cannot discern God's will — it is set on the things of the flesh (Rom 8:5-7). The renewed mind, oriented by the Spirit, can recognize, evaluate, and confirm what is the Father's good, well-pleasing, and perfect will.

**This is critically important for practical decision-making.** The believer does not need a special revelation for every decision — though he must be open to such. **The renewed mind, saturated with Scripture and yielded to the Spirit, can discern the Father's will in the ordinary course of life.**

---

### Practical Implications

**(1) The believer must be actively engaged in mind-renewal.** This requires:

- **Saturation in Scripture** — reading, meditating, memorizing, studying the Word so that the renewed mind has the material from which to think God's thoughts after Him.
- **Resistance to worldly inputs** — being discerning about what media, entertainment, conversation, and influences shape the mind.
- **Cultivation of holy thought patterns** — *"Whatsoever things are true, whatsoever things are honest, whatsoever things are just, whatsoever things are pure, whatsoever things are lovely, whatsoever things are of good report... think on these things"* (Phil 4:8 Geneva).
- **Walking in the Spirit** — for it is the Spirit who applies the Word to renew the mind.
- **Fellowship with the saints** — being shaped by godly community rather than by worldly community.

**(2) The renewing of the mind is an ongoing process.** The present tense **μεταμορφοῦσθε** suggests continuous action. The believer never reaches a point of completion in this life. He is always being transformed, always being renewed.

**(3) Discernment grows.** As the mind is renewed, the believer's ability to recognize the Father's will sharpens. What was once unclear becomes clearer. What was once a difficult judgment becomes an instinctive perception. The mature believer increasingly walks in the Father's will not by anguished deliberation but by Spirit-formed instinct.

---

## **Romans 12:3-21 — THE RENEWED MIND IN ACTION**

The remainder of Romans 12 spells out what the renewed mind looks like in practice. We will not exposit every verse, but let us note the major themes.

### **Romans 12:3-8 — Sober Self-Assessment and Gifts**

The renewed mind:
- **Does not think more highly of itself than it ought** (v. 3) — humility before God and others.
- **Recognizes itself as a member of one body** with diverse gifts (vv. 4-5).
- **Uses its gifts faithfully** — prophecy, ministry, teaching, exhortation, giving, ruling, mercy (vv. 6-8).

This anticipates the corporate dimension we will examine later. But notice: **the renewed mind has a sober estimation of self** and **a clear sense of place within the Body.**

---

### **Romans 12:9-13 — Love in Practice**


### **1599 Geneva:**
***"Let love be without dissimulation. Abhor that which is evil, and cleave unto that which is good. Be affectioned to love one another with brotherly love: in giving honor, go one before another. ..."***

The believer's love is to be **unhypocritical** — genuine, without pretense. The renewed mind hates evil and clings to good. It honors others. It is fervent in spirit, serving the Lord. It rejoices in hope, is patient in tribulation, continues steadfastly in prayer.

These are not isolated virtues; they are **the natural fruit of a renewed mind** living in the Spirit.

---

### **Romans 12:14-21 — Response to Enemies**


### **1599 Geneva:**
***"Bless them which persecute you: bless, I say, and curse not. ... Recompense to no man evil for evil: procure things honest in the sight of all men. ... Dearly beloved, avenge not yourselves, but rather give place unto wrath: for it is written, Vengeance is mine, I will repay, saith the Lord. ... Be not overcome of evil, but overcome evil with goodness."***

The most counter-cultural section of the chapter. The renewed mind:
- **Blesses persecutors** rather than cursing them.
- **Does not repay evil for evil.**
- **Does not avenge itself** but leaves vengeance to the Father.
- **Overcomes evil with good.**

This is impossible to the flesh. **Only the renewed mind, empowered by the Spirit, can love enemies in this way.** It is the very pattern of Christ on the cross.

---

# **PART 2 — GALATIANS 5: THE WALK BY THE SPIRIT AND THE WAR WITH THE FLESH**

## **The Context**

Paul has spent the first four chapters of Galatians defending the Gospel of grace against the Judaizers who insisted that Gentile believers must keep the Mosaic Law to be saved. Now, in chapters 5-6, he turns to the practical implications: **how is the believer to live who is free from the law?**

The answer is not antinomianism (lawlessness) but **walking by the Spirit.** The believer is free from the law as a system of justification, but he is by no means free to indulge the flesh. He is freed *from* law and sin *into* the Spirit's lordship.

---

## **Galatians 5:1 — STAND FAST IN FREEDOM**


### **1599 Geneva:**
***"Stand fast therefore in the liberty wherewith Christ hath made us free, and be not entangled again with the yoke of bondage."***

---

### Observation

**Χριστὸς ἠλευθέρωσεν** — *"Christ has freed us."* Aorist tense — the freedom is accomplished. The believer **stands** in this freedom. He does not negotiate it, does not earn it, does not re-create it. He stands in what Christ has won.

**στήκετε οὖν** — *"stand therefore."* The command is to stand firm. Not to revert to the yoke of bondage (the law as a system of justification or the flesh as a master).

---

## **Galatians 5:13-15 — FREEDOM, NOT LICENSE**


### **1599 Geneva:**
***"For brethren, ye have been called unto liberty: only use not your liberty as an occasion unto the flesh: but by love serve one another. For all the Law is fulfilled in one word, which is this, Thou shalt love thy neighbor as thyself. But if ye bite and devour one another, take heed least *(lest)* ye be consumed one of another."***

---

### Observation

The believer is called to freedom — but freedom is not license. The believer is freed **from** the flesh's tyranny, not freed **for** the flesh's gratification. The believer's freedom is fulfilled in **loving service** to one another.

**The whole law is fulfilled in love** (citing Lev 19:18) — not because the believer is under the law for justification, but because the believer, walking by the Spirit, produces what the law required.

---

## **Galatians 5:16-17 — THE FOUNDATIONAL COMMAND**


### **1599 Geneva:**
***"Then I say, Walk in the Spirit, and ye shall not fulfill the lusts of the flesh. For the flesh lusteth against the Spirit, and the Spirit against the flesh: and these are contrary one to another, so that ye cannot do the same things that ye would."***

---

### Observations of decisive importance

**(1)** **Πνεύματι περιπατεῖτε** — *"walk by the Spirit."* Present imperative — continuous walking. The Christian life is not a one-time decision; it is a continuous walk.

**The dative Πνεύματι is instrumental** — *"by means of the Spirit."* The Spirit is both the **path** and the **power** of the walk. The believer walks **by** the Spirit's leading and **in** the Spirit's empowering.

**(2) The promise:** **ἐπιθυμίαν σαρκὸς οὐ μὴ τελέσητε** — *"you shall by no means fulfill the lust of the flesh."*

**οὐ μή** with the aorist subjunctive is the strongest possible negation in Greek — *"absolutely not, by no means, in no way."* The promise is absolute: **the believer who walks by the Spirit will not fulfill the flesh's lust.**

This is the practical answer to every besetting sin. The believer does not defeat the flesh by attacking it directly; he defeats it by **walking in the Spirit** — at which point the flesh's lust is bypassed.

**(3)** **ἡ γὰρ σὰρξ ἐπιθυμεῖ κατὰ τοῦ Πνεύματος, τὸ δὲ Πνεῦμα κατὰ τῆς σαρκός** — *"the flesh lusts against the Spirit, and the Spirit against the flesh."*

**Two opposing principles within the believer.** This is honest. The flesh (the remaining principle of sin in our mortal members) does not disappear at conversion. It lusts (ἐπιθυμεῖ — *desires, craves*) against the Spirit. And the Spirit lusts against the flesh — opposes it, contradicts it, fights it.

**These are contrary** (ἀλλήλοις ἀντίκειται). The believer cannot serve both. The believer's life is a constant choice — who will I walk with today, this hour, this moment? The Spirit or the flesh?

---

## **Galatians 5:19-21 — THE WORKS OF THE FLESH**


### **1599 Geneva:**
***"Moreover the works of the flesh are manifest, which are adultery, fornication, uncleanness, wantonness, Idolatry, witchcraft, hatred, debate, emulations, wrath, contentions, seditions, heresies, Envy, murthers *(murders)*, drunkenness, gluttony, and such like, whereof I tell you before, as I also have told you before, that they which do such things, shall not inherit the kingdom of God."***

---

### Observations

**(1)** **φανερά** — *"manifest, plain to see."* The works of the flesh are obvious. They are not hidden mysteries; they are visible in the lives of those who walk by the flesh.

**(2) The list is comprehensive** — covering:
- **Sexual immorality** (πορνεία, ἀκαθαρσία, ἀσέλγεια).
- **False religion** (εἰδωλολατρία, φαρμακεία — idolatry, sorcery).
- **Relational disorder** (ἔχθραι, ἔρις, ζῆλος, θυμοί, ἐριθεῖαι, διχοστασίαι, αἱρέσεις, φθόνοι — hatred, strife, jealousy, outbursts of wrath, selfish ambition, dissensions, factions, envy).
- **Lifestyle excess** (μέθαι, κῶμοι — drunkenness, revelries).

**The list is not exhaustive** — *"and such like"* (καὶ τὰ ὅμοια τούτοις) — but illustrative. The flesh produces these kinds of things wherever it reigns.

**(3) The warning:** *"They which do such things shall not inherit the kingdom of God."*

This is sobering. **A pattern of life characterized by the works of the flesh** is incompatible with citizenship in the kingdom. Paul is not saying a believer who falls into one of these sins is lost; he is saying that **those whose lives are characterized by these patterns** demonstrate they are not in the Spirit's possession.

The mark of a true believer is not the absence of all sin (we still battle the flesh), but the **direction** of life — being increasingly turned away from these patterns by the Spirit's work.

---

## **Galatians 5:22-23 — THE FRUIT OF THE SPIRIT**


### **1599 Geneva:**
***"But the fruit of the Spirit is love, joy, peace, long suffering, gentleness, goodness, faith, Meekness, temperance: against such there is no Law."***

---

### Observations of supreme importance

**(1) Note: ὁ καρπός — singular, not "the fruits."** It is **one fruit** with nine facets. The Spirit's fruit is **a single integrated character** — not a buffet from which we can select preferences.

**(2)** Compare with the works of the flesh. The flesh produces "works" (ἔργα — plural, fragmented, disordered acts). The Spirit produces "fruit" (καρπός — singular, organic, integrated growth).

**This contrast is profound:** the flesh manufactures (works); the Spirit grows (fruit). The flesh produces by effort; the Spirit produces by organic life within. **The fruit cannot be forced; it grows naturally from a healthy union with the Spirit.**

**(3) The nine facets:**

- **ἀγάπη** — love (sacrificial, willed love)
- **χαρά** — joy (the deep joy of the Lord)
- **εἰρήνη** — peace (the peace of God that surpasses understanding)
- **μακροθυμία** — long-suffering / patience with people
- **χρηστότης** — kindness, useful service
- **ἀγαθωσύνη** — goodness, moral excellence
- **πίστις** — faith / faithfulness
- **πραΰτης** — meekness, gentle strength
- **ἐγκράτεια** — self-control

**These are the character of Christ.** Each one was perfectly displayed in Him. As the Spirit conforms the believer to the Son's image (Rom 8:29), these qualities increasingly appear in the believer's life — not by his manufacturing them, but by the Spirit's growing them.

**(4)** **κατὰ τῶν τοιούτων οὐκ ἔστιν νόμος** — *"against such things there is no law."*

A wonderful note. No law condemns these qualities. The believer who walks in the Spirit and produces this fruit is automatically in fulfillment of every law's righteous demand — without being under the law.

---

## **Galatians 5:24-25 — THE CRUCIFICATION OF THE FLESH AND THE WALK BY THE SPIRIT**


### **1599 Geneva:**
***"For they that are Christ's, have crucified the flesh, with the affections and the lusts. If we live in the Spirit, let us also walk in the Spirit."***

---

### Observations

**(1)** **οἱ τοῦ Χριστοῦ Ἰησοῦ τὴν σάρκα ἐσταύρωσαν** — *"those who belong to Christ Jesus have crucified the flesh."* Aorist tense — a completed past act.

This recapitulates Romans 6 in compressed form. **The flesh has been crucified** with its passions and lusts. The believer's identification with Christ's cross includes the crucifixion of the flesh.

**(2)** **εἰ ζῶμεν Πνεύματι, Πνεύματι καὶ στοιχῶμεν** — *"if we live by the Spirit, let us also walk by the Spirit."*

**The indicative-imperative pattern again, in compressed form:**
- **Indicative:** *"We live by the Spirit"* (an established reality).
- **Imperative:** *"Let us walk by the Spirit"* (the corresponding action).

The verb **στοιχῶμεν** is military — *"march in step, walk in line, keep in formation."* The believer who lives by the Spirit is to **march in step with the Spirit** — keeping in line with His leading, His pace, His direction.

---

# **PART 3 — COLOSSIANS 3: THE PUTTING OFF AND PUTTING ON**

## **The Context**

Paul has just spent Colossians 1-2 establishing the supremacy and fullness of Christ — Creator, Sustainer, Head of the Body, the Image of the invisible God, in whom dwells all the fullness of the Godhead bodily. He has warned against false philosophies that would diminish Christ. Now in chapter 3, he turns to the practical implications: **since you are united with this Christ, here is how you walk.**

---

## **Colossians 3:1-4 — THE INDICATIVE FOUNDATION**


### **1599 Geneva:**
***"If ye then be risen with Christ, seek those things which are above, where Christ sitteth at the right hand of God. Set your affections on things which are above, and not on things which are on the earth. For ye are dead, and your life is hid with Christ in God. When Christ which is our life shall appear, then shall ye also appear with him in glory."***

---

### Observations

We touched on these verses earlier in our examination of identification. Now we see them in their practical-imperative context.

**(1) The indicative reality (vv. 1, 3-4):**
- **You have been raised with Christ.**
- **You died.**
- **Your life is hidden with Christ in God.**
- **You will appear with Him in glory.**

These are the established facts of identification.

**(2) The imperatives flow from the indicative:**

- **τὰ ἄνω ζητεῖτε** — *"seek the things above."* Present imperative — keep seeking.
- **τὰ ἄνω φρονεῖτε** — *"set your mind on the things above."* Present imperative — keep your mind oriented upward.

The believer's orientation is to be **upward** — toward where Christ is. This is not other-worldly escapism; it is **eternal perspective** in the midst of earthly life.

**(3) The Connecting Word:** Notice the **οὖν** (*therefore*) in v. 1. **Because you have been raised** (indicative), **therefore seek the things above** (imperative). The pattern is exactly the same as Romans 6, 8, 12.

---

## **Colossians 3:5-9 — PUTTING OFF**


### **1599 Geneva:**
***"Mortify therefore your members which are on the earth, fornication, uncleanness, the inordinate affection, evil concupiscence, and covetousness which is idolatry. For the which things' sake the wrath of God cometh on the children of disobedience. Wherein ye also walked once, when ye lived in them. But now put ye away even all these things, wrath, anger, maliciousness, cursed speaking, filthy speaking, out of your mouth. Lie not one to another, seeing that ye have put off the old man with his works,"***

---

### Observations

**(1)** **Νεκρώσατε** — *"put to death."* Aorist imperative — a definitive action. The believer is to **put to death** the earthly members.

This is parallel to Romans 8:13 — *"if by the Spirit you put to death the deeds of the body."* The believer's responsibility is active — to put to death — but the empowering is the Spirit's.

**(2) First list — sins of corrupt desire:**
- **πορνεία** — sexual immorality
- **ἀκαθαρσία** — uncleanness
- **πάθος** — passion (especially shameful)
- **ἐπιθυμίαν κακήν** — evil desire
- **πλεονεξίαν** — covetousness, **which is idolatry**

These are the sins of disordered desire — wanting what is not ours to have.

**(3) Second list — sins of corrupt relationship:**
- **ὀργήν** — wrath
- **θυμόν** — anger (sudden outbursts)
- **κακίαν** — malice
- **βλασφημίαν** — slander
- **αἰσχρολογίαν** — filthy/abusive speech

These are the sins that destroy relationships within the Body.

**(4)** **μὴ ψεύδεσθε εἰς ἀλλήλους** — *"do not lie to one another."* A specific application of the relational sins. Lying is incompatible with the new identity in Christ.

**(5)** **ἀπεκδυσάμενοι τὸν παλαιὸν ἄνθρωπον** — *"having put off the old man."*

**The verb ἀπεκδυσάμενοι is aorist middle — a completed action.** The old man has been put off. The believer is not striving to put off what is still part of his identity; he is **walking in the reality** that the old man has already been put off in Christ.

This is the indicative undergirding the imperative. **Because the old man has been put off** (positionally), **therefore put off these practices that belonged to him** (practically).

---

## **Colossians 3:10-11 — PUTTING ON THE NEW MAN**


### **1599 Geneva:**
***"And have put on the new, which is renewed in knowledge after the image of him that created him. Where is neither Grecian nor Jew, circumcision nor uncircumcision, Barbarian, Scythian, bond, free: But Christ is all, and in all things."***

---

### Observations

**(1)** **ἐνδυσάμενοι τὸν νέον** — *"having put on the new [man]."* Again aorist middle — a completed action. The new man has been put on.

**(2)** **τὸν ἀνακαινούμενον εἰς ἐπίγνωσιν κατ᾽ εἰκόνα τοῦ κτίσαντος αὐτόν** — *"being renewed unto knowledge according to the image of Him who created him."*

The new man is being **continually renewed** (present participle) **unto full knowledge** of the One in whose **image** he was created.

The image is Christ Himself — the perfect Image of the invisible God (Col 1:15). The new man is being progressively renewed to fully bear that image.

**(3)** **ὅπου οὐκ ἔνι...** — *"where there is not..."* — **in the new man, the old divisions are abolished:**
- Greek and Jew
- Circumcised and uncircumcised
- Barbarian, Scythian
- Slave, free

**ἀλλὰ τὰ πάντα καὶ ἐν πᾶσιν Χριστός** — *"but Christ is all and in all."*

In the new humanity that is the Body of Christ, **Christ is all** — the unifying reality that transcends every former division. This anticipates the corporate dimension we will examine later.

---

## **Colossians 3:12-17 — THE NEW MAN IN PRACTICE**


### **1599 Geneva:**
***"Now therefore as the elect of God holy and beloved, put on the bowels of mercies, kindness, humbleness of mind, meekness, long suffering, Forbearing one another, and forgiving one another, if any man have a quarrel to another: even as Christ forgave, even so do ye. And above all these things put on love, which is the bond of perfectness. And let the peace of God rule in your hearts, to the which ye are called in one body, and be ye thankful."***

---

### Observations

**(1)** **Ἐνδύσασθε οὖν** — *"therefore put on."* The imperative flows from the identity: **as elect, holy, and beloved of God**, you are to put on these characteristics.

**(2) The garments of the new man:**
- **σπλάγχνα οἰκτιρμοῦ** — *"bowels of mercies"* (deep, visceral compassion)
- **χρηστότητα** — kindness
- **ταπεινοφροσύνην** — humility
- **πραΰτητα** — meekness
- **μακροθυμίαν** — long-suffering
- **ἀνεχόμενοι ἀλλήλων** — bearing with one another
- **χαριζόμενοι ἑαυτοῖς** — forgiving one another (literally, *"granting grace"*)

**(3) The pattern:** **καθὼς καὶ ὁ Κύριος ἐχαρίσατο ὑμῖν οὕτως καὶ ὑμεῖς** — *"as the Lord forgave you, so also you."*

**The believer forgives because and as he has been forgiven.** The Father's gracious treatment in Christ becomes the pattern of our treatment of others.

**(4)** **ἐπὶ πᾶσιν δὲ τούτοις τὴν ἀγάπην, ὅ ἐστιν σύνδεσμος τῆς τελειότητος** — *"and above all these things, love, which is the bond of perfectness."*

**Love is the binding garment that holds all the others together.** Without love, kindness becomes flattery, humility becomes self-loathing, patience becomes mere endurance. **Love is the unifying principle.**

**(5)** **ἡ εἰρήνη τοῦ Χριστοῦ βραβευέτω ἐν ταῖς καρδίαις ὑμῶν** — *"let the peace of Christ rule in your hearts."*

The verb **βραβευέτω** is from **βραβεύς** — *an umpire, an arbiter.* Christ's peace is to be the **umpire** in the believer's heart — settling disputes, making decisions, ruling over conflicts. When peace departs from the heart, it is often the signal that something is wrong in our direction.

---

## **Colossians 3:18-4:1 — PRACTICAL SANCTIFICATION IN RELATIONSHIPS**


### **1599 Geneva (summary):**
***"Wives, submit yourselves unto your husbands, as it is comely in the Lord. ... Husbands, love your wives, and be not bitter unto them. ... Children, obey your parents in all thing *(things)* for that is well pleasing unto the Lord. ... Fathers, provoke not your children to anger, least *(lest)* they be discouraged. ... Servants, be obedient unto them that are your masters according to the flesh, in all things, not with eye service as men pleasers, but in singleness of heart, fearing God. ... Ye masters, do unto your servants, that which is just, and equal, knowing that ye also have a master in heaven."***

---

### Observation

The practical sanctification reaches into every sphere of relationship:
- **Marriage:** wives submit, husbands love.
- **Family:** children obey, fathers do not provoke.
- **Work/economic relationships:** servants serve as unto the Lord, masters treat servants justly.

**The renewed mind transforms every relationship.** The Father's purposes for family, work, and social relations are honored not because of law but because **the new man, indwelt by the Spirit, walks in love.**

---

## **Colossians 3:17 — THE GOVERNING PRINCIPLE**


### **1599 Geneva:**
***"And whatsoever ye shall do, in word or deed, do all in the Name of the Lord Jesus, giving thanks to God even the Father by him."***

---

### Observation

**The single, governing principle of all practical Christian living:**

- **Whatever you do — in word or deed — do all in the Name of the Lord Jesus.**
- **Give thanks to God the Father through Him.**

Every action of the believer's life is to be done **in the Name of the Lord Jesus** — that is, as one belonging to Him, representing Him, acting in His authority, for His glory. And every action is to be accompanied by **thanksgiving to the Father through Him.**

**This single verse captures the comprehensive nature of Christian practice.** No part of life is excluded. No moment is secular. Every word and deed is to be in His Name and to the Father's glory.

---

# **GRAND SYNTHESIS — THE THREE PASSAGES TOGETHER**

Romans 12, Galatians 5, and Colossians 3 are not three different teachings — they are **three angles on the same reality:** how the indicative of who we are in Christ produces the imperative of how we walk.

## **The Common Pattern**

| Element | Romans 12 | Galatians 5 | Colossians 3 |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Indicative foundation** | God's mercies (11 chapters of doctrine) | Christ has freed us (1:1-4:31) | Raised with Christ; died; life hidden with Him |
| **Pivot word** | οὖν (12:1) | οὖν (5:1, implied throughout) | οὖν (3:1, 5, 12) |
| **Body presentation** | Present your bodies (12:1) | Walk by the Spirit (5:16) | Mortify your members (3:5) |
| **Mind renewal** | Renewing of the mind (12:2) | Mind set on Spirit (implied) | Set affection on things above (3:1-2) |
| **Discernment of will** | Prove what is good will of God (12:2) | Led by the Spirit (5:18) | Walk in wisdom (3:16) |
| **Putting off** | Cast off works of darkness | Crucified the flesh (5:24) | Put off the old man (3:9) |
| **Putting on** | Christ's character | Fruit of the Spirit (5:22-23) | Put on the new man (3:10-14) |
| **Love as governing** | Love without dissimulation (12:9) | Through love serve one another (5:13) | Love is the bond of perfectness (3:14) |
| **Comprehensive scope** | All relationships, including enemies | Every part of life | Every sphere — marriage, family, work |

---

## **The Single Practical Theology**

What emerges from all three passages is **one coherent doctrine of practical sanctification:**

**The believer who has died with Christ, been raised with Him, is seated with Him at the Father's right hand, and is indwelt by the Spirit — this believer is to walk in newness of life by:**

1. **Presenting his body as a living sacrifice** (Rom 12:1) / **mortifying his earthly members** (Col 3:5) — the comprehensive yielding of the physical body to God.

2. **Being renewed in the mind** (Rom 12:2; Col 3:10) — the ongoing transformation of the inner orientation by the Word and the Spirit.

3. **Walking by the Spirit** (Gal 5:16) — depending moment by moment on the Spirit's leading and empowering.

4. **Putting off the works of the old man** (Col 3:5-9; Gal 5:19-21) — actively refusing the patterns of the flesh.

5. **Putting on the new man** (Col 3:10-14) — actively cultivating the patterns of Christ's character.

6. **Walking in love** (Rom 12:9-21; Gal 5:13-14; Col 3:14) — the unifying principle of all Christian practice.

7. **Doing all in the Name of the Lord Jesus, with thanksgiving to the Father** (Col 3:17) — the comprehensive governing principle.

---

## **The Practical Modern Application**

For the modern believer, this means:

**In the morning:** Reckon yourself dead to sin and alive to God. Present your body to the Father as a living sacrifice. Set your mind on things above. Open the Word and feed the renewing process.

**Throughout the day:** Walk by the Spirit. Yield to His leading. Put off the works of the flesh as they arise. Put on the characteristics of Christ. Do every word and deed in the Name of the Lord Jesus, with thanksgiving.

**In relationships:** Love without dissimulation. Bless those who persecute. Forgive as Christ has forgiven. Honor your spouse, parents, children, employer, employees, brethren. Be at peace with all men so far as it depends on you. Overcome evil with good.

**In the mind:** Resist the world's mold. Embrace the Spirit's transformation. Think on what is true, honorable, just, pure, lovely, of good report. Take every thought captive to obey Christ. Renew the mind through Scripture, prayer, and fellowship.

**In moments of failure:** Return immediately to the reckoning. Confess. Trust the cleansing blood. Receive forgiveness. Get up and walk again. Do not despair; do not abandon the walk; do not let one failure define the day.

**In suffering:** Recognize the Father's purpose — conformity to the Son. Trust that all things work together for good. Hold fast the unshakable hope. Look beyond present sufferings to the coming glory.

**In all things:** Live as one who belongs to the Father, walks in the Son, is indwelt by the Spirit, and is destined for glory. **You are not who you were. You are in Christ. Walk worthy.**

---

# **A CLOSING OBSERVATION**

Brother, what we have surveyed in these three passages is **the genius of the New Testament's ethic.** It is not a system of rules to be followed; it is a life to be lived out of who we already are. It is not the achievement of an unreal status; it is the working out of what is positionally true.

The modern believer who walks in this reality is:
- **Free** — from law as a means of justification, from the flesh as a master, from the world as a mold.
